# Developer notes

Monster Girl Quest! Online keeps its first name inside: the `Multiplayer` install folder, `Multiplayer.dll`, the `MGQParadox.Multiplayer` projects, `MGQ_Multiplayer`, the `MGQ_Mp` modules and the DLL's `mp_` functions. The `mgqmp` strings in join and world codes, key derivation labels and player ids must never change, since every world and player id on the relay derives from them.

## Layout

```
MGQ-Online.slnx                       Visual Studio solution
Directory.Build.targets               puts vswhere.exe on the PATH, which the NativeAOT link needs
GameScript/Multiplayer.rb             Ruby, what every way of playing together shares; loads the others
GameScript/Multiplayer/Scripts/       Ruby, the other scripts, see the table below
GameScript/Tests/                     Ruby, tests of the scripts outside the game, run.rb runs them all
MGQParadox.Multiplayer/               C# NativeAOT project -> Multiplayer.dll, and the package
MGQParadox.Multiplayer.Tests/         xUnit tests of the DLL, 32-bit like it
Relay/                                the relay server, see Relay/README.md
package/Multiplayer/                  static files shipped as-is
Shipping/                             publish output, git-ignored
docs/DEVELOPER.md                     this file
docs/GUIDE.md                         the player guide, which README.md links to
docs/images/                          the guide's and README's screenshots
.github/workflows/release.yml         tests, builds and attaches the zip on release
.github/workflows/scripts.yml         tests the scripts on every push
.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml the form players report bugs with
```

**Scripts.** `Multiplayer.rb` is a Patch folder mod: the mod loader loads `Patch/**/*.rb` sorted, capitals first, so it comes after `Discord_RPC.rb` of the Discord mod. At its end it loads the other scripts from `Patch/Multiplayer/Scripts/` itself (`load_scripts`), in the order of its `SCRIPTS`, which the table below follows. They are `.rbx` files because the loader recurses into folders: as `.rb` files it would load them a second time, in alphabetical order. A script may only use what loaded before it at load time; later ones are reached at run time (`defined?`). The order also decides how the battle scripts' hooks wrap each other, since they install them as the game starts running, the last loaded first. A script that is missing or fails to load is logged to the in-game log, and the others load all the same.

**Hooks.** A game method that more than one script follows, such as `Scene_Map#update_scene`, `Spriteset_Map#update` and `#dispose`, `Game_Map#update`, `Graphics.update`, `SceneManager.run`, `Scene_Title#start` and `#update` or `Game_Interpreter#execute_command`, is wrapped once, by `core_hooks.rbx`; each script registers a block with `MGQ_MpHooks.before` or `.after` under its own name instead of wrapping the method itself. The blocks run on the object the method runs on, with its arguments, in the order nested wraps would: before blocks from the last script loaded to the first, after blocks from the first to the last. So `battles_pvp.rbx` still puts the shared data back before `world.rbx` closes the world as the title screen starts. A failing block is logged once and the others run on. A hook that changes what a method does or returns is a wrap of that script's own, made with `MGQ_MpHooks.around` from a block that gets the object, the arguments and the original, registered under the script's name as well, so a script loaded again replaces its wrap instead of wrapping the method once more. Only what the registry cannot express stays a plain alias guarded by a `hookable?`: `world.rbx`'s redirect of `File` and `Dir` into a world's save folder and its `Bitmap#initialize`. Holding the player is wrapped once too: a script registers with `MGQ_MpHooks.hold_player`, and while any block says so the player stands still (`Game_Player#movable?`) and the menu stays shut (`Scene_Map#update_call_menu`), as during a held encounter or the leader's story scene.

| File | Module | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `Multiplayer.rb` | `MGQ_Multiplayer` | The foundation every mode shares: `Log` (`Logs/Multiplayer InGame <session start>.log`, one per game session, without a line or size limit), `load_scripts` (the other scripts), `Link` (the DLL's `mp_*` functions, each named once with its signature in `Link::EXPORTS`; `Link.read` takes a state the DLL writes into a buffer, and when the text grew between the size probe and the copy it asks again with a larger buffer, up to `READ_TRIES` (4) times, before it returns ""), `Background` (keeps the game running while another window is in front, and has `Input` report no buttons meanwhile), `Key` (F-keys the game's `Input` does not know), `Capture` (takes the buttons away from the game while any screen of the mod reads them, each holding them until it gives them back), `Discord` (the bridge client, see below). |
| `core_log.rbx` | `MGQ_MpLog` | The scripts' lines in the in-game log. A script's module extends it and sets `LOG_TAG`, which starts its lines: `log` writes one, `log_once` only the first time for its key, for what would otherwise fail every frame. |
| `core_hooks.rbx` | `MGQ_MpHooks` | The game methods several scripts follow, each wrapped once, with the blocks the scripts register (see Hooks above). `Multiplayer.rb` registers its `Graphics.update` and `SceneManager.run` blocks once the scripts loaded. |
| `core_game_access.rbx` | `MGQ_MpGame` | What the mod reads and writes of the game that the game keeps private: `get` and `set` take a field by its key of `FIELDS`, `call` a private method of `METHODS`. A field the game no longer has is logged once by its name, so a game update that renames one is followed in this one script. The game's classes get no accessors of the mod's own, which another mod's could clash with. The scans over every field of an object (a battle's windows, an action's result) stay where they are. |
| `core_hotkeys.rbx` | `MGQ_MpHotkeys` | The hotkeys the player binds: the action wheel (B), the emote wheel (E), the chat (T), the World overview, which outside a world opens the PvP battle screen (F11), accepting and declining the notification box's first invite (Y, N), the party box's size (Tab, which in a raid boss battle also crops the boss's HP bar to its gauges, see [HP bars](#hp-bars-ui_raid_barrbx)), and joining a battle nearby in a Raid World (J). `code` reads the key from `Patch/Multiplayer/Player.ini` (`key_wheel`, `key_emotes`, `key_chat`, `key_overview`, `key_accept`, `key_decline`, `key_party_box`, `key_join_battle`, Windows key codes), else the default; `pressed?` reads it with `MGQ_Multiplayer::Key`, and `label` names it for the texts. A press while a confirm box of the mod is open (`confirm_open?`, `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm.open?` at run time) is read and dropped by `pressed?`, logged, so nothing opens under the box and the box's Y never answers an invite. With Mod Config Remake installed, `register` adds a key binding per hotkey (`:keybind`, with `:value` and `:on_change` going to Player.ini, since `$game_system.conf` lives in each save). |
| `ui.rbx` | `MGQ_MpUi`, `MGQ_MpUi::Style`, `MGQ_MpUi::Motion`, `MGQ_MpUi::PlayerColor`, `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm`, `Sprite_MpConfirmBox`, `Window_MpInfo`, `Sprite_MpListBox`, `Sprite_MpMessageBox` | What the screens and boxes share (see [Shared UI](#shared-ui-uirbx)): `Style` (the flat boxes' colors, font sizes, padding, timings and outlined and plated texts), `Motion` (a sprite's fade and slide), `PlayerColor` (a player's color by who they are), `Confirm` (the yes/no box over any scene); `MGQ_MpUi.wrap` breaks a text into lines that fit, at spaces and inside a word that is too long, for the chat and the windows; `MGQ_MpUi.cut` shortens a text to a width with an ellipsis, keeping a tail such as the overview's type and difficulty; `MGQ_MpUi.grouped` writes a whole number with its thousands grouped by commas; `MGQ_MpUi.within` keeps a value between two bounds; `MGQ_MpUi.draw_icon` draws an icon of the game's icon set at any side; `MGQ_MpUi.top_room` tells the pixels a strip of the mod takes at the top of the map's screen, such as a raid boss's bar, which the notification box and the party box move down by; `FRAMES_PER_SECOND` (60) converts frames and seconds; `Z` names the depth of every sprite of the mod on the map (`:arrows` 190 under `:lines`, `:confirm` 1050 over every box, `:typing` 1100 over it, `:layout` 1200 topmost); `Window_MpInfo` is the window of lines across the top of the world screen and the PvP battle screen, and the base of the save copy's notice; `Sprite_MpListBox` is the large box of headings and items (see The list box under [Worlds (DLL)](#worlds-dll)), `Sprite_MpMessageBox` the box in the middle of the screen that tells the player something. |
| `ui_theme.rbx` | `MGQ_MpUi::Theme`, `MGQ_MpUi::Theme::Skinned` | The interface presets, Monarch (the default) and Legacy, which set the style's box colors in place, and the window skin painted in them that the mod's windows wear (see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)). Loads right after `ui.rbx`. |
| `ui_layout.rbx` | `MGQ_MpUi::Layout` | The floating boxes the player moves and resizes with the left Alt and the mouse, each kept in `Player.ini`, never smaller than its least size (see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)). Loads right after `ui_theme.rbx`. |
| `ui_text_box.rbx` | `MGQ_MpUi::TextEdit`, `MGQ_MpUi::TextBox` | The text boxes: `TextEdit` is the editor of a text being typed, `TextBox` draws a box being typed into, and `MGQ_MpUi.wrap_spans` breaks a text into lines that keep every character's place (see Text boxes under [Worlds (DLL)](#worlds-dll)). |
| `core_actors.rbx` | `MGQ_MpActors`, `Game_MpActor` | Characters of another game: `Builds` writes and reads a character's build as plain numbers, `Items` its equipment, and `Game_MpActor` rebuilds it as a real character of this game (see [Team](#pvp-battles-battles_pvprbx) and Rebuilt characters); its `name` is "Alice (\<player\>)" for the battle's messages, `owner` the player and `base_name` the character alone, which the co-op status window draws in the owner's color. PvP battles build their enemy side on it, co-op battles the party members' characters. |
| `core_async.rbx` | `MGQ_MpAsync` | The world running on behind every other screen while a world is open, and the live map behind menus (see [The world never pauses](#the-world-never-pauses-core_asyncrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpWorld` at run time. |
| `overworld_sync.rbx` | `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` | The open world's messages: what each game tells the others, the other games by seat, and the registry the scripts after it join for their messages (see [On the map](#on-the-map)). Every script sends through `tell`, shows its notices through `notice`, and reads the player's id and seat from `Me.id` and `Me.seat`. Reaches `MGQ_MpWorld` at run time. |
| `ui_wheel.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWheel` | What the action wheel and the emote wheel share: the arrows held point like a joystick (`held`), one to its side, two to the diagonal between them, and nowhere while none is held, read past the capture with `Capture.press?`, and steadied for the open wheel (`steady`: one arrow left of a diagonal still points to the diagonal for `GRACE_FRAMES`, since two arrows are seldom let go in the same frame); and how a wheel spreads its choices over the eight directions (`places`: choice i of n on direction round(i × 8 / n), so every count is mirror-symmetric). A wheel keeps its pick while they point nowhere. |
| `ui_actions.rbx` | `MGQ_MpActions` | The action wheel on the map and what the scripts after it offer between players (see [Actions](#actions-ui_actionsrbx)). It names no later script: each adds its choice to the wheel's ring by order (`wheel_choice`: the party 10, duels 20, trades 30, leaving the party 40, the chat 50) or fills its middle (`wheel_center`: the overview), lists the choices of a player's menu for the World overview and the chat (`peer_options`, `own_options`), says the line above the player's own head (`own_line_from` for a line of its own, `own_doing_from` for what the line joins, with the moment it lapses for a countdown, see [Actions](#actions-ui_actionsrbx)), tells that its screen lies over the map (`cover`), and registers its `Offers` (`offer`): `peer_option` and `own_options` for the World overview's menus, `call_of` for its rows and `notice_of` for the notification box. `MGQ_MpCoop::Offers`, `MGQ_MpBattlesDuel::Offers` and `MGQ_MpTrade::Offers` are the three. Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `ui_chat.rbx` | `MGQ_MpChat` | Chat: the chat box, the bubble above the sender and the chat log, on the map and in battles (see [Chat](#chat-ui_chatrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `ui_emotes.rbx` | `MGQ_MpEmotes`, `Sprite_MpEmoteWheel` | The emote wheel on the map (see [Emotes](#emotes-ui_emotesrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions`. |
| `overworld.rbx` | `MGQ_MpOverworld` | What the player sees of the other players of the open world: the ghosts, their name labels in their `PlayerColor`, the own ping and the status line (see [On the map](#on-the-map)). Reads `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `coop.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoop` | Parties: `Party` (who is in the player's party, invites, the leader), and the gate that hands the party's messages to the scripts registered for them (see [Parties](#parties-cooprbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `coop_scope.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope` | Whom the player shares the map and the story with: the party in a Classic world, everyone on the map in a Raid World; the map's gate (`MGQ_MpCoop.route_map`) and its sends (`MGQ_MpCoop.tell_map`, `MGQ_MpCoop.tell_map_to`) (see [Party or map](#party-or-map-coop_scoperbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`; reaches `MGQ_MpCoopEvents`, `MGQ_MpCoopGather` and `MGQ_MpCoopStory` at run time. |
| `coop_squad.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopSquad` | A party's squads: each player's share of the Frontline and the Backline, the followers shown, the formation screens' cut and the Party Followers option (see [Squads](#squads-coop_squadrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `coop_events.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopEvents` | The events of a party: sorts every event page by what it does, and opens chests for the whole party (see [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Hands gathering messages to `MGQ_MpCoopGather`; reaches `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`, `MGQ_MpCoopCastle`, `MGQ_MpStoryState` and `MGQ_MpCoopStory` at run time. |
| `coop_gather.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopGather` | Gathering the party for the leader's story scenes, holding the scene until it came, and bringing a player over to a teleport they asked for (see Story scenes together under [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`, `MGQ_MpActions` and `MGQ_MpStoryState` at run time. |
| `coop_teleport.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopTeleport` | Teleports between a party's members: at once to the leader, to anyone else once they allow it (see Teleports between members under [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpCoop`, `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions`; reaches `MGQ_MpCoopGather` at run time. |
| `coop_scene.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopScene` | The leader's story pictures, screen effects and hidden character, shown to the members on the leader's map and put back once the story ended (see The story's scene under [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpCoopEvents`, `MGQ_MpCoopGather` and `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Wire` at run time. |
| `coop_npcs.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopNpcs` | The NPCs a party shares on a map: the Map Owner moves them and the other members follow (see [NPCs in a party](#npcs-in-a-party-coop_npcsrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions` at run time. |
| `coop_story_rewards.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopStoryRewards` | Generated by `story_rewards.rb` of MGQ-Paradox-Event-Checker (the developer tools next to this repository, which this repository's users have no use for) from the game's data: the skills Luka learns and the companions who join in events, the items the story's events give, and the events that reward Alice's and Ilias's side differently (see [The party's story](#the-partys-story-coop_storyrbx)). Library Flags (`LIBRARY_NAMES` in `story_rewards.rb`, which `raid_data.rb` shares) are never marks, since translations differ in setting them, so every translation gives the same marks. |
| `story_state.rbx` | `MGQ_MpStoryState` | The story's state model, which the party's story and a Raid World's story share: the switches, variables and self switches read and written as raw data, what is each player's own (`personal_switch?`, `personal_variable?`, the map's chests), a story joined with the player's own values (`mix`), written for a message and read back (`delta`, `full`, `story_lists`, `pack`, `unpack`, `decode_full`, `decode_delta`), and the story's skills, companions and items granted (`learn`, `join`, `bring`, `knows?`, `roster_ids`, `story_count`) (see [The party's story](#the-partys-story-coop_storyrbx)). Logs under the tag of `coop_story.rbx`; reaches `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope`, `MGQ_MpCoopEvents`, `MGQ_MpCoopChoices` and `MGQ_MpCoopStoryRewards` at run time. |
| `coop_story.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopStory` | The story a party plays in a Classic world: members borrow the leader's switches, variables and self switches and get their own back afterwards, built on `MGQ_MpStoryState` (see [The party's story](#the-partys-story-coop_storyrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions` at run time. |
| `coop_castle.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopCastle` | The Pocket Castle in a party: which pages are its residents, each player's own talks (see The Pocket Castle under [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Reads `MGQ_MpStoryState`. |
| `world.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorld` | Worlds: the Multiplayer command on the title screen, the world screen, each world's folder with its saves and system save, and whether the open world is a Raid World (`raid?`, `companion_sharing`) (see [Worlds](#worlds-worldrbx)). |
| `world_mods.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldMods` | A world's required mods: this game's copies against the relay's mod catalog and the creator's hashes, the download and restart, and the creator's mod settings while in the world (see [World mods](#world-mods-world_modsrbx)). |
| `world_mods_parking.rbx` | `MGQ_MpModParking` | A world that turns other mods off: which of the player's scripts it turns off, parking them and starting the game again into the world, putting them back on the way to the title screen and at the next start, and leaving a world whose rule changed (see [Turn off other mods](#turn-off-other-mods-world_mods_parkingrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`; `world.rbx` and `world_screen.rbx` call it at run time. |
| `world_catchup_data.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldCatchupData` | Generated by `raid_data.rb` of MGQ-Paradox-Event-Checker from the game's data of the reference install alone, the workspace's MGQ Paradox Translated with the updated translation (`ruby Tools\raid_data.rb "<that install>\Data"` in MGQ-Paradox-Event-Checker): the story's parts with their ending marks and level gates, every story join of a companion with its gate (the story's level floor at the join through any persona, else the start level on a first join, else for a companion the first playthrough took away the Level Cap's cap there, else the level kept; at most 300), the story's removals, the key items the story gives or takes, the chests that hold key items, and the story skills and items, each with its part, step, side and marks; joins, key items and rewards with `ran` (false where the marks only tell that the event is there to play, as for a town's companion who joins on a talk: the list sets nothing of its own, and a common event's callers or a non-talk page's transfer in set nothing either), joins and removals with their command's index in the list. Within one place a key item given comes before one taken. Scratch switches and variables are named as whole words (`SCRATCH_NAMES`), so a story value such as "Northern Undersea Temple Event" (1068) keeps its marks. Library Flags (`LIBRARY_NAMES`, "Library Flag: …" and the untranslated "図鑑フラグ…") are never marks, since translations differ in setting them (the 3.06 community translation sets no switch 5352 on the way into map 1813), so every translation gives the same marks and only command indexes may move, which keep their order within a list; where only a Library Flag told that an event ran, `RAN_MARKS` marks it by the story value it or the scene after it sets, and `MAP_MARKS` gives every event of a map a value that holds whenever they run, as Monte Carlo's evacuation (map 1979) its end (1305 = 6). The generator stops with an error when one of these pins names a command or map its scans never place, as after a game update. |
| `world_story.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldStory` | A Raid World's story at the relay: fetched and laid over the player's own values, written back, the Great Decision's routes and the return to it (see [In the game](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)). Builds on `MGQ_MpStoryState`; registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`; reaches `MGQ_MpCoopChoices`, `MGQ_MpCoopGather` and `MGQ_MpWorldCatchup` at run time. |
| `world_catchup.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldCatchup` | A Raid World's catch-up: a player behind on their part's checkpoint, their story held, the level gates, the story's rewards, removals, key items and story chests by story position, companions by level and the world's shared companions (see [Catch-up](#catch-up-world_catchuprbx)). Reads `MGQ_MpStoryState`, `MGQ_MpWorldStory` and `MGQ_MpWorldCatchupData`; `coop_events.rbx` asks it at run time. |
| `world_opening.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldOpening`, `Game_MpOpeningInterpreter` | A new game's opening in a Raid World whose story is past it: the start map's autorun event runs its setup in an interpreter of its own without its scene, the player goes where it ends, and only then is the world's story laid over. `world_story.rbx` and `coop_events.rbx` ask it at run time. |
| `world_difficulty.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldDifficulty` | A Raid World's difficulty: every game in it plays on it from entry, load, new game and the relay's push on, the game's own difficulty choice and the Reaper's change set it instead, and the fights with values of their own keep them (see [Raid World difficulty](#raid-world-difficulty-world_difficultyrbx)). Reads `MGQ_MpWorld`; registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `world_save_distribution.rbx` | `MGQ_MpSaveDistribution`, `Scene_MpStartSave` | A world's starting save: the creator picks one of their saves on the game's own save screen, and new players fetch it before entering the world (see [Starting save](#starting-save-world_save_distributionrbx)). Called by `world.rbx` at run time. |
| `world_text.rbx` | `Scene_MpText`, `Window_MpTextEdit`, `Window_MpTextInput` | The text screen: names and passwords, typed on the keyboard or picked from the game's letters with a gamepad. |
| `world_screen.rbx` | `Scene_MpWorlds`, `Window_MpWorldList`, `Window_MpWorldDetail`, `Window_MpChoice`, `Window_MpWorldForm` | The world screen and its windows: the list, a world's details, the forms that create a world, change one or add a hidden one to the list, the mod picker over the forms, and the choices in the middle. The picker lists MGQ_MpWorld::ModPick, which reads and writes the mods as the world keeps them (! required, ? essential), in the list box of ui.rbx. |
| `world_save_export.rbx` | `MGQ_MpSaveExport`, `Window_MpSaveExportNotice` | Copies a world's latest save, an autosave included, with its thumbnail into the first free slot of the player's own `Save` folder; the player's own system save stays as it is. The world screen offers it on a world played on this PC, the game's menu while a world is open (*Copy to my game*, after the game's own extra commands, greyed out until the player saved in the world, with a notice of the slot). It writes through `Files.unmapped`, since `Save/` stands for the world's folder while one is open. |
| `coop_choices.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopChoices`, `Window_MpStoryChoices`, `Scene_MpStoryChoices` | The story's choices a member makes for themselves, the leader's offer to sync the story and the screen of choices (see [The story's choices](#the-storys-choices-coop_choicesrbx)). Loads after `world_screen.rbx`, whose `Window_MpWorldForm` its screen draws with. |
| `battles.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattles` | The rules every multiplayer battle shares, PvP and co-op alike: no ero offers, no Give Up, and no swapping the Backline in where the battle has none (a PvP battle or duel whose host chose *Frontline Only*, a team duel); `begin(kind)` sets them and keeps the game's own settings, `finish` puts those back. `MGQ_MpBattles::Mode` is what a kind of live battle does differently from a duel between two players (whose party the host's is, who else plays, what happens before the start, at each command phase, when a player or the host leaves); `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop::Mode` and `MGQ_MpBattlesTeam::Mode` register theirs with `MGQ_MpBattles.mode`. |
| `battles_raid_bosses.rbx` | `MGQ_MpRaidBosses` | The story's boss troops by troop id: the story milestone each belongs to, the milestone's cap, and whether the troop is its fight's last phase (`at`, `boss?`, `last_phase?`), the one-time bosses outside the story that count too (`OPTIONAL`: the six of the Chaos Domain), each boss's name (`milestone_key`), the story's section it belongs to (`SECTIONS`, `OPTIONAL_SECTION`, `sections`, `section`), tier and the factors its tier gives its enemies' stats (`TIERS`, `TIER_BOSSES`, `tier`, `key_tier`, `factor`, `tier_stats`) and the title its HP bar shows (`TITLES`, `title`), and whether the battle running is a boss battle (`battle?`, the table or switch 22, which the game's events turn on around every story boss battle). Data only; loads before `battles_coop.rbx`. |
| `battles_coop.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop`, `Game_MpAlly` | Co-op battles: who joins, the party every game shares, the allies other players command, and the status window's names in their players' colors (see [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)). Drives `MGQ_MpBattlesSync`. |
| `battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin` | Hotjoining in a Raid World: a random encounter joins a co-op battle running on the map (see [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)). Loads after `battles_coop.rbx`, which calls it at run time, and before `battles_sync.rbx`, so its command phase hook wraps the live battle's. |
| `battles_coop_join.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesJoin`, `Sprite_MpJoinPrompt` | Joining a battle by choice in a Raid World: the prompt below the player's character near a battle that takes players in, and the request to its host (see [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)). Loads after `battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`, which takes the player in. |
| `battles_raid_pool.rbx` | `MGQ_MpRaidPool` | A Raid World's raid bosses in the game: every story boss battle fights the boss's pools at the relay, its enemies' stats raised by the boss's tier, reporting what it dealt as its actions end and at each command phase and taking the pools' HP at each command phase and every few seconds while it waits for commands, and a player near a boss battle that takes nobody in may fight the same boss in a side battle (see [In the game (`battles_raid_pool.rbx`)](#in-the-game-battles_raid_poolrbx)). Loads after `battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`. |
| `battles_coop_level_sync.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopLevelSync` | Level Sync in a co-op battle: a character above the battle's level, the highest of the party leader's characters in it, fights with the stats of that level, and its equipment loses half as much as the level takes; its stats come back once the battle ends. Every game syncs to the level the host sent. |
| `battles_sync.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync` | Live battles, for any mode: `join` makes the next battle live, `battle_started` marks it running, `finish` ends it and closes the link, `record_to_file` has the next battle record itself; `Waiting` is the box a battle waits in, with whom it waits for and the seconds (see [Live battles](#live-battles-battles_syncrbx)). Knows nothing of PvP battles, and names neither co-op battles nor team duels: it asks the running battle's mode (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.mode`, see `battles.rbx`). |
| `battles_sync_wire.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Wire` | How a live battle's messages are written and read: plain values as tab-separated tokens. |
| `battles_sync_recorder.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Recorder` | The host's side of the stream: what its battle shows, as events for the guests or a mirror match's file. |
| `battles_sync_playback.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Playback` | The guest's side of the stream: the host's events, played on the guest's battle. |
| `battles_sync_live.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Hooks`, `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Live` | The hooks into the game's battle, installed as the game starts running, and the steps host and guest differ in. |
| `battles_balance_pvp.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBalancePvp` | The balance of a PvP battle, between `begin(battlers)` and `finish`: skills use their formulas for monsters (`MONSTER_FORMULAS`), max HP times `HP_RATE` (damage formulas still read the game's max HP), one action's damage or healing on a target counted in full up to `DAMAGE_KNEE` of its max HP and kept below `DAMAGE_LIMIT`, at least `DAMAGE_RATE_FLOOR` of a hit taken by a character whose physical, magical or sure-hit damage rate is lower, evasion and reflection up to `AVOID_CEILING`, at least `ELEMENT_FLOOR` of an attack past a nullified or absorbed element, and a defense wall that takes `WALL_SHARE` of max HP off a hit. Constants, since both games must agree. |
| `battles_pvp.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp` | PvP battles: the PvP battle screen (F11), the team exchange, the rebuilt characters, the mirror match. Drives `MGQ_MpBattlesSync`. |
| `battles_pvp_backline.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Backline` | The Backline in a PvP battle between two players (see [Backline](#backline-battles_pvp_backlinerbx)): the *PvP Backline* option, the other side's characters outside its Frontline, the Frontline each game tells the other, and the commands a swap costs. Its `Mode` is the `:duel` mode of `MGQ_MpBattles`. |
| `battles_pvp_mirror.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::MirrorReport` | The mirror match's report: the player's team beside its rebuilt copy, turn by turn. |
| `battles_pvp_lobby.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Lobby`, `Scene_PvpLobby`, `Window_PvpLobbyCommand` | The PvP battle screen outside a world (F11): its lines and commands by how the exchange stands. |
| `battles_duel.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesDuel` | Duels: challenges like party invites, and `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp`'s battle over the world's room (see [Duels](#duels-battles_duelrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `battles_team.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesTeam` | Team duels: the sides once the duel starts, the other side's characters, and who commands the characters of a player who left (see [Team duels](#team-duels-battles_teamrbx)). Driven by `MGQ_MpBattlesDuel` and `MGQ_MpBattlesSync`. |
| `trade.rbx` | `MGQ_MpTrade` | Trades between two players on the same map: offers like party invites, both offers and their confirmations, the commit through the relay, applying and recovering a trade, and the save after it (see [Trades](#trades-traderbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions`. |
| `ui_trade.rbx` | `MGQ_MpTradeUi`, `Scene_MpTrade`, `Window_MpTradeOffer`, `Window_MpTradeCommand`, `Window_MpTradeBag`, `Window_MpTradeGold` | The trade screen: both offers as lists the Offers command scrolls, the bag by the game's categories, the gold's digits, confirming and cancelling. |
| `world_overview.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldOverview`, `Sprite_MpWorldOverview` | The World overview (F11 or the wheel's middle) with its Players and World pages, and the place, level and story every state tells (see [World overview](#world-overview-world_overviewrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`; reads `MGQ_MpRaidPool.pools` and `MGQ_MpRaidBosses` at run time for the World page. |
| `ui_party_box.rbx` | `MGQ_MpPartyBox`, `Sprite_MpPartyBox` | The party box at the top right of the map, built from `MGQ_MpWorldOverview.party_rows`, or in a Raid World the map box from `map_rows`, so it loads after the overview, and its size; each row with its state icon, its name in its `PlayerColor` (dim with the away icon while the connection is down), level and ping in fixed columns; it moves down by `MGQ_MpUi.top_room`. |
| `ui_edge_arrows.rbx` | `MGQ_MpEdgeArrows`, `Sprite_MpEdgeArrow` | The arrows at the screen's edge toward the players on the map whose ghost is off the screen (see [Edge arrows](#edge-arrows-ui_edge_arrowsrbx)). Hooks `Spriteset_Map` on its own; reads `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`, `MGQ_MpOverworld`, `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope` and the open screens at run time. |
| `ui_raid_bar.rbx` | `MGQ_MpRaidBar`, `Sprite_MpRaidBar` | The raid bosses' HP bars: the boss's title and, for each of its enemies, its name over a gauge, across the top of a battle that fights its pools, and on the map the title with the names over thin gauges above each event that starts a boss battle, or the battle's strip at the top while none shows (see [HP bars (`ui_raid_bar.rbx`)](#hp-bars-ui_raid_barrbx)). Loads after `ui_party_box.rbx` and before `ui_notices.rbx`. |
| `ui_notices.rbx` | `MGQ_MpNotices`, `Sprite_MpNoticeBox` | The notification box at the top left, on the map and in menus, below `MGQ_MpUi.top_room`. Invites: party invites and duel challenges from anywhere, read from the states every frame, so each stays exactly as long as it stands (a full party's invite drops out). Messages: what other scripts show for a moment (`message`, 3 s by default, `drop`), such as a party member meeting enemies, below the invites and without keys; outside a world too, such as a Discord invite taken during a game, while one shown in a world goes when the world closes. `MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:accept` (Y) accepts the first invite (a challenge only on the map), `:decline` (N) declines it: `MGQ_MpCoop::Party.decline` (`party_decline`, the inviter drops the player from `invite_to`) or `MGQ_MpBattlesDuel.decline` (`reason=no`, the challenger drops them from `challenge_to`), and the box hides a declined invite until the inviter sends a new one, an accepted one for 2 s (`@answered`, `ACCEPT_HOLD_FRAMES`), so a second press accepts nothing and an accept that did nothing, as in a menu, shows the invite again. Loaded last; registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |

**`MGQParadox.Multiplayer/`** builds `Multiplayer.dll`: C# compiled with NativeAOT into a native 32-bit DLL, because the game is a 32-bit process. Players need no .NET installed.

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `Exports/` | The functions the game scripts call, one partial class over ten files, which keep the namespace `MGQParadox.Multiplayer` (a namespace `Exports` would hide the class). `Exports.cs`: `mp_start`, `mp_keep_running`, `mp_check_for_update`, `mp_newer_version`, `mp_update_game` and `mp_dev_build` (1 for a development build, see [Raid World starting save](#raid-world-starting-save-development-builds-only)). `Exports.Pvp.cs`: `mp_set_player_name`, `mp_host`, `mp_join_invite`, `mp_join_clipboard`, `mp_receive_invite`, `mp_cancel`, `mp_copy_code`, `mp_state`, `mp_status`, `mp_send` and `mp_receive`. `Exports.World.cs`: `mp_new_id`, `mp_set_player` and `mp_player_id` for the player; `mp_world_id`, `mp_world_directory_id`, `mp_world_open`, `mp_world_close`, `mp_world_status`, `mp_world_send`, `mp_world_say` and `mp_world_receive` for worlds (see [Worlds](#worlds-dll)). `Exports.Directory.cs`: `mp_dir_refresh`, `mp_dir_watch`, `mp_dir_find`, `mp_dir_list`, `mp_dir_create`, `mp_dir_fetch_start`, `mp_dir_unlock`, `mp_dir_unlock_code`, `mp_dir_edit`, `mp_dir_set_settings`, `mp_dir_set_data`, `mp_dir_delete`, `mp_dir_ban`, `mp_dir_action` and `mp_dir_clear`. `Exports.Mods.cs`: `mp_mods_list`, `mp_mod_hash`, `mp_mods_install`, `mp_mods_options` and `mp_restart_game` (see [World mods](#world-mods-world_modsrbx)). `Exports.Parking.cs`: `mp_mods_park`, `mp_mods_restore`, `mp_mods_parked`, `mp_mods_scripts`, `mp_mods_restart_parked` and `mp_mods_recover` (see [Turn off other mods](#turn-off-other-mods-world_mods_parkingrbx)). `Exports.Trade.cs`: `mp_trade_commit`, `mp_trade_cancel`, `mp_trade_state`, `mp_trade_done`, `mp_trade_pending` and `mp_trade_pending_list` (see Trades under [Worlds](#worlds-dll)). `Exports.Raid.cs`: `mp_raid_story_fetch`, `mp_raid_story_post`, `mp_raid_story_state`, `mp_raid_story_headers`, `mp_raid_checkpoint_fetch`, `mp_raid_checkpoint_state`, `mp_raid_route_lock` and `mp_raid_companions_add` (see [World story on the relay](#world-story-on-the-relay-raid-worlds)). `Exports.RaidBosses.cs`: `mp_raid_bosses_fetch`, `mp_raid_boss_report` and `mp_raid_boss_state` (see [Raid boss pools on the relay](#raid-boss-pools-on-the-relay)). `Exports.Input.cs`: `mp_typing` and `mp_take_typed` for the keyboard, `mp_copy_text` for the clipboard. Nothing may throw out of them. |
| `UpdateCheck.cs` | Asks GitHub's API for the latest release once per session, on its own thread, and compares it with the DLL's version. Development builds (`0.0.0-dev`) never ask. Same shape as the Discord mod's, see its `docs/DEVELOPER.md`. |
| `Network/Transport/` | What every connection between games is made of: `Message` (header lines and a payload), `IFrameChannel` (whatever carries whole frames), `FrameCipher` (their encryption), and `Link` with `LinkState` (the connection that stays open after a PvP exchange). |
| `Network/Relay/` | The relay as the games reach it: `Relays` (the relays by id, and the rooms a token leads to), `RelayFrameChannel` (a PvP room), `RelayWorldChannel` (a world room), `WebSocketMessages`. |
| `Network/Pvp/` | PvP battles: `Session` with `SessionState` (hosting, joining, the first exchange, the state the script reads) and `JoinCode`. |
| `Network/World/` | Worlds: `WorldSession` with `WorldState` (the connection to a world room), `WorldCode`, `WorldCipher`, `WorldKeys` (auth key and player id), `Player`; the directory: `WorldDirectory` and `DirectoryClient` (the list and its actions), `WorldLock` (the token locked with the password), `StartingSave` (a world's starting save, zipped and encrypted), the last three sealing with `SealedBox` (an AES-GCM box with an HKDF key); `RaidStory` (a Raid World's story at the relay) seals with it too, through `StorySeal`; `RaidBosses` (a Raid World's boss pools at the relay, `DirectoryClient.Bosses.cs`). No folder is named `Link` or `Directory`, since a namespace of that name would hide the class `Link` and `System.IO.Directory`. |
| `../Relay/` | The relay server itself, see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md). |
| `Windows/GameWindow.cs` | Keeps the game running while another application is active, see [Background](#background), and hands typed keys to `Keyboard`. |
| `Windows/AltMenuGuard.cs` | When Alt may not open the game window's menu, in which the game would wait: for Alt pressed alone, while a text screen wants the keyboard, and for an Alt press that began while one did. |
| `Windows/Keyboard.cs` | What the player types while a text screen wants it, see [Keyboard](#keyboard). |
| `Windows/Clipboard.cs` | The join code and world ids on the clipboard. |
| `Windows/NativeMethods.cs` | The Windows functions the DLL needs to find itself. |
| `PlayerName.cs` | Cleans the name a player typed, for PvP and worlds alike. |
| `Mods/` | A world's mods: `CatalogMod` (an entry of the relay's catalog), `ModHash`, `ModInstaller`, `ReleaseDownload`, `ModOption` (a Mod Config option an admin's game read), `GameRestart` (starting the game again, the updater and the parked mods' watchdog) and `ModParking` (the parked mods, see [Turn off other mods](#turn-off-other-mods-world_mods_parkingrbx)). |
| `Log.cs`, `ModFolder.cs` | `Multiplayer <session start>.log` in the game folder's `Logs` folder, one per game session named after the game process's start, which the DLL finds two folders above its own. |

**`package/Multiplayer/`:** the player `README.txt`, and the updater: `Update.bat` runs `Update.ps1` (Windows PowerShell 5.1), which downloads the latest release's zip and extracts it over the game folder. The player starts it, or the game does: once a newer release is out, *Multiplayer* on the title screen offers *update and install* (`MGQ_MpWorld::UpdateOffer`), `mp_update_game` starts `Update.bat -GameProcess <pid>` in its own console and the game quits (under Wine it answers 2 and starts nothing, since Wine's cmd has no PowerShell, and the box tells to update by hand, `WINE_TEXT`); the updater takes that process once as it starts, holding its handle (`Get-StartingGame`), so a later wait never waits on another program Windows gave the same id, waits for it, updates, and starts `Game.exe` again, with `MGQMP_OPEN_WORLDS=1` set when it succeeded, which makes the title screen open the world screen once (`MGQ_MpWorld.open_after_update`). Only an install that already has this updater offers it, since the installed scripts run it. It reads the installed version from the DLL's `ProductVersion`, so it needs no version file, and prints the release's notes to the console (see [Releasing](#releasing)). Unlike the Discord mod's updater, it also **cleans up after refactors**: the `ShippedFile` item in `MGQParadox.Multiplayer.csproj` is built from the same `ModFile`/`PatchFile` globs the package itself is, so every publish writes a `Patch/Multiplayer/Manifest.txt` that always lists exactly what that release ships, no matter how many scripts get renamed, merged or split later. `Update.ps1` reads the *old* `Manifest.txt` before extracting and the *new* one after, and deletes whatever dropped out (a manifest lists only files a release shipped, so `Player.ini`, `Favourites.ini`, the worlds and the logs, which share `Patch/Multiplayer/` with them, are never at risk). An install with no `Manifest.txt` yet (from before the updater existed, or a hand-extracted zip) simply skips cleanup instead of deleting anything. Every path goes to cmdlets through `-LiteralPath`, and the download, the extraction (`Expand-Release`) and the game's start through .NET, since Windows PowerShell takes `[` and `]` in a path as wildcards and a game folder such as `MGQ Paradox [EN]` would break them. The cleanup only reaches `Patch` and `Patch/Multiplayer`, never the game folder's `Parked mods`, and the game puts parked mods back once the updater started, before it quits; the updater only extracts once the game closed. `Patch/Multiplayer/Parking.lock`, the parked mods' lock, is in no manifest, and exists only while a game or the watchdog holds it, so it is gone by the time the updater runs. `ModParking.ps1` is the parked mods' watchdog, which the game starts itself (see [Turn off other mods](#turn-off-other-mods-world_mods_parkingrbx)).

## Build and test

You need the .NET 10 SDK and the Visual Studio workload **Desktop development with C++**, whose linker NativeAOT uses.

Publishing the DLL project assembles the complete release layout in `Shipping/` at the repository root. Copy its content into a game folder to install or update the mod there:

```
Patch/                      Multiplayer.rb  0_ModConfigRemake.rb
Patch/Multiplayer/          Multiplayer.dll  Manifest.txt  README.txt  Update.bat  Update.ps1  ModParking.ps1
Patch/Multiplayer/Scripts/  *.rbx
```

- **Mod Config Remake ships with the mod.** A publish on this PC copies it from the Mod Collection checked out beside this repository (`..\MGQ-Paradox-Mod-Collection`) into `package/Patch/` (ignored by Git); the release workflow downloads it from the Mod Collection's latest release instead. Without it the publish stops with an error.

- **Publish, not build:** only a publish runs NativeAOT, so a plain build gives no usable DLL.

  ```powershell
  dotnet publish MGQParadox.Multiplayer -c Release -p:Version=0.1.0
  ```

- **Release publishes** also zip it: `bin/Release/MGQ-Online-<Version>.zip`.
- **Every publish replaces `Shipping/`.** Close the game before copying it over an install: `Multiplayer.dll` is locked while the game runs.
- **Logs:** `Multiplayer <session start>.log` (the DLL) and `Multiplayer InGame <session start>.log` (the scripts: what the mod did, decided and refused, and errors), both in the game folder's `Logs` folder, which every mod writes its logs to. Each game session writes its own pair, stamped with the time the game started (`YYYY-MM-DD HH-MM-SS`, local time), without a line or size limit; as a session starts, the scripts delete the logs of every session older than the last five (`Log::KEPT_SESSIONS`, `Log.prune`). Only the scripts' logs count the sessions, since the DLL names its log after its own start when Windows cannot tell the game's, as under Wine; a log stamped up to a minute before the oldest kept session stays (`SESSION_SLACK`), and only the files actually deleted are counted in the log. A game the World Admin tool starts for the options dump deletes nothing, and names its scripts' log `Multiplayer InGame <session start> options dump.log`, which counts no session but goes with the sessions older than the kept ones. The DLL logs the world's messages as one line about every minute (`WorldTraffic`: messages and kilobytes in and out, by kind, and the seats they came from), plus each message it could not send, and the scripts log their hooks as one line per script with how many methods it follows and wraps (`MGQ_MpHooks.report`, after loading and every frame), plus each one registered again or failing.
- **Two games on one PC** can play each other: both go through the real relay as they would between two homes.
- **A relay on this PC:** run `node Relay/node/server.js` (port 8080, `ADMINS` for admin ids, `MGQ_RELAY_DB=<file>` to keep worlds, saves, mods and trades in a SQLite database instead of memory) and start the game with the environment variable `MGQMP_RELAY=ws://127.0.0.1:8080`; the DLL then talks to that relay instead of the live one, which lets a build with relay changes be tried before the relay is deployed.

### Tests

`MGQParadox.Multiplayer.Tests` covers the DLL without the game: the join code, its relay room and codes of other versions, messages, their encryption (another token, a changed, repeated, missing or returned frame), the link (messages both ways, pings keeping a quiet link open, goodbye, silence, oversized messages) and whole exchanges between two sessions, including the party both sides name and what hosting and joining say when the relay finds no host, cannot be reached or is unknown. Everything meets at a relay inside the test process (`TestRelay`), which never reaches the real one. It builds into an executable that runs itself:

```powershell
dotnet run --project MGQParadox.Multiplayer.Tests
```

The tests run 32-bit like the game and need the x86 .NET 10 runtime (`C:\Program Files (x86)\dotnet`); with `DOTNET_ROOT` pointing at the x64 install they need `DOTNET_ROOT_X86` pointing at the x86 one.

`GameScript/Tests` covers the scripts outside the game, one `*_test.rb` file per area (the loader, the shared hooks, the open world, parties, battles, worlds, the hotkeys and the boxes on screen), with stand-ins for the parts of RGSS and the game each script touches. Each file runs in a Ruby process of its own, since each brings its own stand-ins; `support.rb` gives them `check` and `load_script`, `world_support.rb` the open world's stand-ins that the overworld, duel and overview tests share, and `battle_support.rb` the live battle's that the co-op, hotjoining and team duel tests share. With Ruby 3.3 or later:

```powershell
ruby GameScript/Tests/run.rb        # -v shows every check
```

The *Scripts* workflow runs them on every push, and the *Release* workflow before it builds a release. They run on a newer Ruby than the game's (1.9), so they do not catch what only that one lacks. Drawing, input and the game's own battle and event engine stay untested: those need the game.

## Conventions

- **File headers.** Every `.cs`, `.rb` and `.rbx` file starts with a header block listing the file name and a changelog, newest entry first. Entries by the same author on the same day are grouped. Only changes after the first commit are recorded; before that, a file carries just `Created`.
- **Documentation comments.** Every type and member is documented: XML comments in C#, YARD-style comments in Ruby. The summary is mandatory, parameters and return values whenever they apply, and remarks only where they add something. Keep them short, and always describe the current state.
- **Inline comments.** Kept to a minimum, only where the code cannot say something itself: a constraint, a trap, or the failure a line prevents.
- **Names.** Magic strings and numbers get a named constant.
- **Language.** Code, comments and documentation are English.

## Releasing

1. Take out the development builds' Raid World starting save first, see [Raid World starting save](#raid-world-starting-save-development-builds-only).
2. Push your changes.
3. On GitHub, go to **Releases → Draft a new release**, create a tag like `v0.1.0`, write the notes, and click **Publish**. `Update.ps1` prints these notes to the console as they stand, so write them as something a player reads there, not as a commit log.
4. The *Release* workflow runs the tests, builds the mod and attaches `MGQ-Online-0.1.0.zip` to that release. Keep that zip name: `Update.ps1` of every install looks for `MGQ-Online-*.zip`. If the run failed, fix the cause on `main`, then start **Actions → Release → Run workflow** with the release's tag.

## Runtime

- **Nothing blocks the game.** The DLL's threads run the network; every export returns at once.
- **Nothing throws into the game.** Every export and the DLL's threads catch everything; an exception escaping them would end the game.
- **The connection.** Hosting waits in a room of the relay, and joining enters it; the first frames swap what each game hands over (a PvP battle's team) and then stay open as the link, which carries the modes' own messages (`mp_send`, `mp_receive`). The link pings after 2 s without another frame, counts as `closed` after a goodbye and as `dropped` after 10 s of silence, which a running game never lets happen. `mp_state` describes it: `state`, `code`, `invite`, `error`, `opponent`, `link`, `role`, `party`, `ignored`, then the payload. `mp_status` describes it without the payload, for the checks the scripts make many times a second.
- **Update check:** `MGQ_Multiplayer.start` calls `mp_check_for_update` once per session, and the `Graphics.update` hook polls `mp_newer_version` every 30 frames until it answers (`MGQ_Multiplayer::UpdateCheck`). Once a version comes back, `MGQ_Multiplayer.outdated?` is true, which `MGQ_MpWorld.available?` and `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp.available?` both require to be false: the title screen's Multiplayer command greys out (`MGQ_MpWorld.add_title_command`) and `Scene_Title` shows `MGQ_MpWorld::UpdateNotice` (two lines below the translation's version and Discord's own notice, which takes up to two lines from y=24), and F11 on the map answers with `MGQ_Multiplayer::UPDATE_MESSAGE` instead of opening the PvP lobby (`MGQ_MpBattlesPvp.on_map`). Different mod versions never speak the same protocol on purpose, so this keeps an outdated game from confusing a friend's newer one rather than failing mid-exchange.

### Worlds (DLL)

A world is a lasting place several games play in at once, up to 32. Its **world code** (`mgqmp2;<token>;<relay>;<seats>`) never changes, and whoever holds it may enter the world; the game keeps it in the world's folder, which `mp_world_id` names by a hash of the token, so the folder never gives the code away.

- **The directory.** The relay lists every public world, and the hidden ones the asking player joined (see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md), world directory). `mp_dir_refresh` fetches the list with the player's key on a thread of the DLL's own, and `mp_dir_list` hands it out as it stands: `state` and `error`, then one tab-separated line per world (`world`, id, seats, players online, creator's id, when last active, creator's name, name, starting save `none`, `pending` or `ready`, 1 when hidden, 1 when players choose their start, 1 without a password, 1 when featured, 1 when only games with the same data may enter, the creator's game data, the mods it needs, its description, the creator's hashes of required mods outside the catalog, its mod settings, its type `classic` or `raid`, how a Raid World shares companions `off`, `story` or `all`, a Raid World's difficulty from -2 to 4, empty when it sets none, and 1 when it turns other mods off, else 0, by its type when the relay does not tell (1 for a Raid World); fields added later go at the end, and the game script reads a line without the last four as a Classic world sharing none that allows other mods) and per player (`member`, id, 1 when online, name). The DLL reads the relay's JSON by hand, since NativeAOT leaves out what serializing by reflection needs. Every request of the DLL to the relay, and its world room's WebSocket, names what the game plays in the header `X-MGQ-Features` (`raid`, `DirectoryClient.Features`); the relay keeps games that do not name `raid`, as released ones never do, out of Raid Worlds. A development build names `dev` too (`raid,dev`, `DirectoryClient.DevFeature`), which lets it give a Raid World a starting save, see [Raid World starting save](#raid-world-starting-save-development-builds-only).
- **One action at a time.** `mp_dir_create` (name, password, seats, 1 when hidden, 1 when players choose their start, starting save, description, mods, the creator's game data, 1 when only games with the same data may enter, the creator's mod hashes, the mod settings, the type `classic` or `raid`, how a Raid World shares companions `off`, `story` or `all`, both empty for the defaults, the difficulty, which only a Raid World sends and which must then be -2 to 4, and 1 when it turns other mods off, 0 when not, anything else for the relay's default by type), `mp_dir_unlock` (world, password), `mp_dir_delete` and `mp_dir_ban` (world, player), and `mp_dir_fetch_start` (world code, folder) run on a thread and return at once, refusing while one runs; `mp_dir_action` gives `state` (`idle`, `busy`, `done`, `failed`), `kind`, and `code` with the world's `world` id, after `mp_dir_unlock` also its `name`, `start` and `choose`, or `error`; `mp_dir_clear` makes room for the next. `mp_copy_text` puts a hidden world's id on the clipboard for its creator.
- **Game data.** `MGQ_MpWorld::GameData.fingerprint` is a format number and one CRC32 per part of the database (actors, classes, skills, items, weapons, armors, enemies, states, troops, common events, maps) over the ids that exist: entries with a name, troops with members, common events with commands, and the map ids. It reads whether an entry has a name, never the name itself, so the translated and the untranslated game give the same one as long as both name the same entries. The creator's game sends it as `data` with a new world, with `strict` when only games with the same data may enter; the relay only keeps both. The world screen compares it with its own, from the list, before a world is entered; a world this PC has saves of is not entered while the list has not arrived. A differing game is asked first (`data_allows?`), or refused in a `strict` world. A world without `data`, or with another format, is not compared. The creator replaces a world's `data` with their game's as it is now from the details' *Your game* row (`mp_dir_set_data`, the relay's `edit` route, which takes `data` from the creator only), for when a mod they play with changed.
- **Cancel.** Every screen of the mod that closes asks `MGQ_MpUi.cancel?`: the game's cancel button past the capture, or the numpad's 0 read from Windows with Num Lock on and off (`NUMPAD_CANCEL_KEYS`, 0x60 and Insert), since the game takes it only with Num Lock on; `Key.triggered?` reads those keys at every `Input.update` (`Key.read_watched`), watched from the start (`Key.watch`). A text box asks `numpad_cancel?` alone, as the game's cancel button is X too. `Key.pressed?` takes a key nobody asked about in the frame before as let go, counting frames at `Graphics.update` (`Key.next_frame`), since a game window's cancel runs `Input.update` twice in a frame; so the numpad's 0 that closed one screen still closes the next.
- **Text boxes.** `MGQ_MpUi::TextEdit` (ui_text_box.rbx) is the editor every text box of the mod types through: the text, the cursor in it, the keys that move it (left, right, Home, End) and remove characters (Backspace, Delete), the longest text and the characters it takes, and the blinking. Its owner feeds it the typed characters (`type`, which answers `:enter` and `:escape` for the owner to act on) and calls `update_keys` once a frame, then draws: `visible` picks the part of a long text a box of one line shows around the cursor, and `MGQ_MpUi.wrap_spans` breaks a text into lines that keep every character's place, for a box of several lines, where up and down move by line (`update_keys` with the lines). `MGQ_MpUi::TextBox` draws a box being typed into on any picture, a sprite's or a window's: `draw_line` a box of one line, `draw_area` one of several, each with the cursor in the color of the text; the owner draws the background and what the box shows while nobody types. The chat box and the world screen's forms use both; a new box should too, instead of typing or drawing logic of its own.
- **The list box.** `Sprite_MpListBox` (ui.rbx) is a large box of headings and items drawn like the World overview, above every window; `Sprite_MpListBox::View` holds its lines, the item picked and the scroll. The world screen opens it with a world's players (online, then offline), its mods (`MGQ_MpWorld.mods_of` splits them at semicolons) or its whole description, from the world's choices, from the details (`Window_MpWorldDetail#targets`: the right arrow moves the cursor onto them, `update_focus`) or with a click (`target_at`).
- **The left side.** The worlds scroll in `Window_MpWorldList`, which draws a scrollbar on a sprite of its own; the commands sit in `Window_MpWorldCommands` below it. `MpWorldListPane` stands for both towards the screen and lets the player pick one of the two as a whole first (`pick`, the cursor drawn around the window through `MGQ_MpBoxCursor`), so the commands are reached without moving past every world: up and down pick, confirm moves into the picked window onto the row the cursor left there (`enter`), cancel moves back out (`back_out`), and cancel while a window is picked, or the commands' *Back*, leaves the screen. The pane reads those keys itself (`update`), a frame after it took the input.
- **Required and essential mods.** A mod written as `?Name` in a world's mods is essential (`MGQ_MpWorld.essential_mods`): no script is looked for, which suits mods of data files; it shows in green while the game data check finds this game matching the world's, and in gold while it differs or cannot be compared. A mod written as `!Name` is required (`required_mods`). `installed_mod?` looks for a script of that name among the `.rb` files of the `Patch` folder, read once per session and compared without case, `.rb`, spaces, underscores and hyphens; `on_enter` checks them before the game data check, by their files' hashes where the relay's catalog or the creator knows them (see [World mods](#world-mods-world_modsrbx)), else by file name only.
- **Hidden worlds by id.** *Add a hidden world* looks the id up (`mp_dir_find`, the world's lock, which names it) and keeps it in `Added.ini` in the mod folder (`MGQ_MpWorld::Added`). Every refresh first hands those ids to the DLL (`mp_dir_watch`), which asks the relay for them with the list (`ids`), so the world shows with its details before the player ever entered it; entering then goes the way of any listed world. *Remove from my list* forgets an id whose world was never entered, and only the 50 ids added last are kept (`Added::MAX`, the relay's `maxListedIds`), so the ids of deleted worlds do not crowd new ones out. Whoever holds a hidden world's id therefore sees its details, but still needs its password to enter.
- **The password locks the token.** Making a world makes a new token and locks it with the password (`WorldLock`: PBKDF2-SHA256, 600,000 iterations, then AES-GCM; the DLL derives PBKDF2 itself (`Pbkdf2Sha256`), since .NET's goes through a BCrypt pseudo-handle no Proton release supports; locks of released versions have 200,000), so the relay only ever keeps the lock. An empty password locks it too, and marks the world `open` in the directory, so the games open its lock with the empty password without asking. Opening a world fetches its lock, which comes with the world's name, seats, starting save state and whether players choose their start, so a hidden world opens by its id alone, and opens it with the password; a wrong password fails the tag. The world's id is `Relays.WorldRoomOf(token)`, which the opened token must match.
- **Proving the token.** Every connection sends the auth key (`WorldKeys.AuthKeyOf`: HKDF of the token, apart from the frames' keys and the room id), whose hash the directory keeps. It also sends the player's key and name, which `mp_set_player` sets from `Patch/Multiplayer/Player.ini`; the relay turns the key into the id everyone sees (`WorldKeys.PlayerIdOf`, which `mp_player_id` hands out), and only the creator's key may delete the world or remove a player.
- **The starting save.** `mp_dir_create` takes the starting save's files as lines of `<name>=<path>`, paths relative to the game's folder (`ModFolder.GamePathOf`). `StartingSave.Seal` zips them and encrypts the zip with AES-GCM under a key from the token (HKDF, apart from the auth key) before the world is made, so a save that cannot be read, or sealed is larger than the relay's 8 MB (`StartingSave.MaxSealedBytes`), leaves no world behind; the relay deletes a world whose save has not come 10 minutes after it was made. The world is made with `start: true`, which keeps everyone out until the upload arrived; a failed upload deletes the world again. `mp_dir_fetch_start` downloads it with the auth key and unpacks it into the folder, plain file names only, at most 8 files and 64 MB.
- **No host.** `mp_world_open` takes a seat in the world's room at the relay, whose room id is the world's id. Once every seat is taken, the next game waits and tries again, with `error` saying the world is full; it waits the same while the starting save is still on its way or the relay took too many tries from its address, which the relay names in the `X-MGQ-Refusal` header. The player's key goes in the `X-MGQ-Player` header, never in an address. A world the directory lacks, a token that no longer fits, a player the creator removed, a world with as many players as it may, or a Raid World the relay keeps the game out of (400 `raid_unsupported`, which needs a newer mod) ends the session for good (`failed`), as does a close code 4009 (removed, or with the reason `replaced` when the same player entered from another game) or 4010 (deleted) from the relay.
- **Coming back by itself.** A cut connection, a silent relay (60 s) or the relay's 2-hour limit per connection leads back into the room, after 2, 5, 10, then every 30 s, the delays starting over after a connection that lasted 30 s. Meanwhile `state` is `reconnecting`, and the game plays on. The seat may be another one afterwards.
- **The ping.** The writer sends the relay a text `ping` on connecting and every 5 s (`PingInterval`), which also keeps the connection open, and the relay answers `pong` without waking the room. The time from the last ping to its pong is the ping, `ping` in milliseconds in `mp_world_status` while the connection is open; a ping whose pong never came is given up at the next.
- **One key per sender.** `WorldCipher` derives each game's key from the token and a salt the game picks per connection and sends in front of every frame; the sender's seat and the target seat, which follows the counter, are authenticated, so a frame passed on under another seat or to a game it was not meant for fails. A sender's counters only have to grow, since frames it sends to one seat skip the others. Salts of games that left, or whose seat another game took, are refused from then on. One cipher lasts for every connection of an open world (`Renew` picks a new salt per connection), so frames read before a break are refused again after it.
- **Only a carrier.** The DLL never reads the messages and knows no players, only seats: who plays on a seat is for the game scripts to tell each other. `mp_world_receive` hands out one inbox entry at a time: `kind=seat` with `seat` and `others` after every (re)connect, `kind=in` or `kind=out` with the `seat` that came or went, `kind=message` with the sender's `seat` and the text, `kind=chat` with the `name` of an admin and the line the relay said for them, `kind=story` with the `rev` a Raid World's story has now (see [World story on the relay](#world-story-on-the-relay-raid-worlds)), `kind=difficulty` and `kind=boss` (see below), and `kind=mods_rule` with `value` 0 or 1 and the world's `mods` (see [Turn off other mods](#turn-off-other-mods-world_mods_parkingrbx)). `mp_world_send` sends to one seat, or to every other game with -1, and refuses while the game holds no seat. `mp_world_status` gives `state` (`idle`, `connecting`, `open`, `reconnecting`, `failed`), `seat`, `others`, `ping` and `error`.
- **The relay reads the chat.** `mp_world_say` mirrors a line of the world's chat to the relay as the text frame `chat <line>` (`WorldSession.Say`, through the writer's queue like a frame, `RelayWorldChannel.SendText`), which the relay keeps for the world's admins and passes to nobody; the line itself still goes to the others sealed through `mp_world_send`. The relay's `chat <name>` + tab + `<line>` text frame, what an admin said in the World Admin tool, becomes the `chat` inbox entry (`TakeChat`); a DLL that does not know the frame logs and ignores it, as it does any text it does not know.
- **Trades.** `mp_trade_commit` (world, trade id, partner's id, both offers as one line) hands a trade to the relay on a thread of its own (`WorldTrades`): the SHA-256 of the offers, and the offers sealed (`TradeSeal`: AES-GCM with a key derived from the world's token, the trade id bound in), so the relay compares hashes and never reads the offers; then it asks the relay about once a second until the trade is committed or cancelled, 150 s at most. `mp_trade_state` reports it (`trade`, `state`: sending, waiting, committed, cancelled or failed, `reason`, `error`), `mp_trade_cancel` cancels a trade the relay has not committed, and `mp_trade_done` marks it applied. `mp_trade_pending` fetches the committed trades this player has not marked done, which `mp_trade_pending_list` hands out unsealed, one `trade=<id>	<offers>` line each after the state. Sealing needs the open world's token, which `WorldSession` keeps for the world it opened (`OpenWorld`), also while it reconnects.
- **A world code in the wrong place.** A world code given to a PvP join says where worlds are entered.

### Keyboard

RGSS only knows buttons, so the text screens get what is typed from the DLL. While `mp_typing` is on, `GameWindow` hands the window's `WM_CHAR` characters and `WM_KEYDOWN` presses to `Keyboard`, and `mp_take_typed` hands out what came since the last call: `keys=`, how many keys went down, then the characters, Enter, Backspace and Escape among them as `\r`, `\b` and `\e`, and the clipboard's text in place of Ctrl+V. Windows makes `WM_CHAR` only when the message loop translates key presses; until the first one arrives, `Keyboard` works the characters out from the presses itself (`ToUnicodeEx`, which leaves the keyboard's state alone), which combines no accents typed as dead keys. The in-game log does not tell which way it went, the DLL's `Multiplayer` log does ("the game window gets typed characters"). While typing is on, `Link.typing` also sets `$mgq_text_input`: mods whose hotkeys read Windows directly, as Party Sheet of the MGQ-Paradox-Mod-Collection does, check it, since `Capture` only reaches the game's `Input`.

### Background

RGSS pauses a game whose window is not in front: RGSS301.dll keeps the last `WM_ACTIVATEAPP`'s flag and its frame loop waits while it says inactive. A live battle cannot wait for either player, so `GameWindow` subclasses the game's window (class `RGSS Player`) and tells RGSS the game is always active; `Multiplayer.rb` turns it on at start (`mp_keep_running`). The keyboard only reaches the window in front, but gamepads reach every game, so `Background.guard_input` has `Input` report no buttons while another window is in front. It wraps `Input` through `core_hooks.rbx` once the first scene starts, since the game's plugins, the gamepad one among them, load after the Patch folder, and `Capture` reads the buttons past it while it holds them (`Capture.reading?`). Once the last screen gives the buttons back, `Input` reports none until the next `Input.update` (`Capture.released?`), so the press that closed it, such as the Escape that closed the chat box, never reaches the game too, as when a battle's wait took it for leaving the battle. Each `Input.update` asks Windows once whether the game is in front, since the game's `Graphics.frame_count` raises until the game first sets it.

When the game closes, the in-game log's last line says how (`closing_text`): "the game closes", "it was quit" for `exit`, or the error that ended it, so a log that stops without one tells a crash. A second `SceneManager.run`, which only F12 starts, is logged as a reset.

## Discord (optional)

The [Discord Rich Presence](https://github.com/Pauliinchen/MGQ-Paradox-Discord-Rich-Presence) mod, when installed, shows the connection on the player's profile and brings Discord's invites. The two mods only meet in its `MGQ_Discord::Bridge` (version 1), which `MGQ_Multiplayer::Discord` checks for (`available?`) and otherwise leaves alone; the join code on the clipboard works without it.

- **Every 30 frames** (`Discord.tick`, a `Graphics.update` hook) the script hands over the connection when it changed: `Bridge.hosting(party, join_code)`, `Bridge.connected(party, friend)` or `Bridge.idle`. The Discord mod then shows `Waiting for a friend (1 of 2)` with the join code as the invite's join secret, or `Playing with <Friend> (2 of 2)`, in a party both games name after a hash of the join code's token (`party` in `mp_status`).
- **Invites** the player accepted in Discord come back through `Bridge.take_invite`. A world code goes to `MGQ_MpWorld::Invite`: the title screen opens the world screen, which adds the world to the list by its id (`mp_world_directory_id`), waits for it like an entry after a restart for mods, and opens its lock with the code (`mp_dir_unlock_code`) instead of the password; during a game the notification box says the title screen opens it. Anything else goes to `mp_receive_invite`, where they wait until the game joins with them (see [Flow](#pvp-battles-battles_pvprbx)). While the game hosts, the DLL ignores them and counts them (`ignored` in `mp_status`), and the PvP battle screen or the map says so once each: Discord also hands over joins the player did not mean, such as after a friend's request to join, and joining would end the hosting that the player's own invites lead to.
- **The player's name** on Discord (`Bridge.player_name`) goes to `mp_set_player_name` before every host or join, so the friend sees it; without the Discord mod it is "A friend".
- **Status fields:** `battles_pvp.rbx` registers `Bridge.add_status`, which adds `pvp_battle_with=<Friend>` or `pvp_battle=mirror` for the Discord mod's first line; `overworld_sync.rbx` adds `mp_world`, `mp_world_id`, `mp_world_size`, `mp_world_max` and `mp_world_invite` while a world is open (the seats from the world code's last field; the world code as the invites' join secret, left out for a world from before the directory), and `coop.rbx` adds `mp_party`, `mp_party_size` and `mp_party_max` while the player is in a world's party of two or more. The Discord mod shows `Playing on World <World> (3 of 8)`, taking turns with `Currently in a Party! (2 of 4)`.

**Dialogue boxes.** The [Battle Dialogue](https://github.com/Pauliinchen/MGQ-Paradox-Mod-Collection/tree/main/Battle_Dialogue) mod of the MGQ-Paradox-Mod-Collection, when installed, shows every battle's messages in boxes at the screen's sides instead of the message window, so neither side of a live battle waits for a key. The guest's playback never calls it: it adds each message the host's battle showed to the game's message and names its speaker from the stream in `$game_message.speaker`, which that mod reads; before a new message starts it lets the game move the earlier one on (`Scene_Battle#wait_for_message`), unless the guest has to catch up (`Playback.message`). Without it, a live battle's messages stay in the message window, which moves on by itself after 90 frames on both sides.

## Worlds (`world.rbx`)

**Flow.** *Multiplayer*, below *Continue* on the title screen (`Window_TitleCommand#make_command_list`), opens `Scene_MpWorlds`, laid out like Mod Config Remake: the worlds at the left, the chosen one's details at the right (who made it, players online of its seats, whether it is hidden, and everyone who ever joined, those online marked), then *Create new world*, *Add a hidden world*, *Change your name* and *Back*. The list is every world the directory shows the player plus the worlds on this PC it no longer has, which come last once the directory's list arrived (`Entry#gone`); among either, Raid Worlds first (drawn in red, `MGQ_MpWorld::RAID_COLOR`, also above favourites and in place of a featured world's gold; one the directory no longer lists keeps its colour by its `world.ini`), then favourites (marked `*`), then the featured worlds (drawn in gold, `MGQ_MpWorld::FEATURED_COLOR`; only the relay's admins make them, with the admin tool outside this repository), then those played last, then by name (`MGQ_MpWorld.entries`); it is fetched when the screen opens and every 10 s, and keeps the cursor on the world or command it was on. A world offers *Enter the world*, *Mark as a favourite*, *Delete my saves of it*, and for its creator *Remove a player*, *Delete the world for everyone* and, for a hidden world, *Copy the world id*. *Enter* asks for the password only while the world has one and this PC's folder of the world does not hold its world code (`Entry#password_known?`; the details' *Password* row then says *Known* instead of *Needed*, `Window_MpWorldDetail#password_text`). While a directory action runs, the screen says what it waits for and holds the input (`@resume_form` false while a form's action runs, so its button cannot send it twice). After *Create the world* or *Add the world* the cursor goes onto that world as soon as the fetched list shows it (`MpWorldListPane#select_world`, `select_asked`: into the worlds box, unless the pane no longer takes the input because the player moved on), and the info line says "\<name\> was created. Confirm to enter it." (`ENTER_HINT`).

**Forms.** While the list points at *Create new world* or *Add a hidden world*, the right side shows its form (`MGQ_MpWorld::Form`, drawn by `Window_MpWorldForm`), and confirming moves the cursor into it; Cancel goes back to the list and keeps what was filled in. Up and down move between rows, left and right between the two fields on one row, and the lines at the top show the hint of the field the cursor is on. A switch (`:switch`, drawn and turned by `Window_MpWorldForm` for the world screen and the story's choices alike, `draw_switch`, `turn_or_move`) shows its outcome between arrows; confirm turns it onward, an arrow turns it unless it points at the other field of the switch's row, and one that cannot be used buzzes. *Create new world* has *Name*, *Password* (may be empty, as in the other form), *Max Players* (digits only, 2 to 32, 4 at first), *Hidden*, the switch *World type* (Classic or Raid, fixed once created), for a Raid World the switch *Sharing companions* (Off, Story companions, or Story companions and battle recruits) and below it the switch *Difficulty* (the game's seven names, NORMAL at first), for a Classic world on the same two rows the checkboxes *Shared save* and *Player's choice* and below them *Save* (*Save* is greyed out until *Shared save* is ticked, whose `needs` is a block; opens the save screen, see [Starting save](#starting-save-world_save_distributionrbx); a Raid World starts from the beginning and never sends them, `Form#from_save?` and `Form#choose?`, except a development build's Raid World, whose optional *Save* shares the Difficulty's row, see [Raid World starting save](#raid-world-starting-save-development-builds-only)), each field shown only for its type (a field's `shows` block takes it out of `Form#fields` while it is not part of the form), all in the panel *World*, which keeps the form on one page; *Mods*, the checkboxes *Allow data mismatch* and *Shared Mod Settings*, and the checkbox *Turn off other mods* in *Game data* (ticked for a Raid World and unticked for a Classic one as the type changes, until the player ticks or unticks it, `Form#tick`, `follow_type`); the description, a box of several lines, in *Description*; and *Create the world*. A field's `group` names its panel, and `Window_MpWorldForm#places` lays the panels out below each other, as `Window_MpWorldDetail` draws a world's details; both share `MGQ_MpWorldPanels`. *Edit the world*, offered to a world's creator and the relay's admins, opens a third form in place of the details (`Form.edit`, kept as `@forms[:edit_world]` only while it is open) with what may change after a world was made: *Max Players*, for a Raid World the switch *Difficulty* below it (first *Per player* for one that sets none, which sends nothing), *Mods*, *Turn off other mods* and the description. `mp_dir_edit` (world, seats, description, mods, the creator's mod hashes and whether they go along, the difficulty and whether it goes along, only for a Raid World and only when the form changed it, since the relay moves every player onto a difficulty it is sent, and 1 or 0 for whether it turns other mods off and whether that goes along, only when the form changed it) sends them to the relay's `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/edit`; a world's folder takes the new seats into its code the next time the world is entered from the list (`World#describe`). *Add a hidden world* has *World id* (32 characters of 0-9 and a-f, pasted with Ctrl+V) and *Add the world*, which adds the world to the list; Enter in the id box sends the form like the password form's (`SEND_ON_ENTER`, `sends_on_enter?`: only while the form has one text box, so a form that gains another is sent from its button). Sending a form that is not complete names the first field that is wrong and moves the cursor there. The scene is the same object when the text or save screen returns, so the forms keep their values and the cursor goes back to the field it left from.
