**Typing.** A text box is typed into in place: confirming it takes the keyboard (see [Keyboard](#keyboard)) and shows a caret; Enter keeps the text once it passes the field's check (`Form#check`: not empty, Max Players in range, a whole world id), otherwise the reason shows and the typing goes on; Escape puts the text back; characters the field does not take buzz. The form takes the cursor again only once OK and Cancel are let go, so the Enter that ended the typing does not start it again. A button or direction pressed in a frame no key went down, as only a gamepad does (directions by their first frame, since a held arrow key reports its direction without going down again), moves the typing to `Scene_MpText` with the game's letters shown; so does confirming a text box while the game's window is not hooked (`Background.running?`), since the keyboard cannot reach the game then. What the text screen hands back passes the same check.

**Names and passwords.** The first visit asks for the player's name unless the Discord mod knows it (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Player`: the name chosen for Multiplayer, else the one on Discord; PvP battles pass it on too). `Scene_MpText` shows the text in a box, as stars for a password, and takes the keyboard: Enter confirms, Escape leaves, Backspace removes the last character. The game's own letters stay hidden until a button is pressed in a frame no key went down, and show from the start when the game's window is not hooked or a form's text box asked for them. A key that types also moves the game's own buttons, such as Z for OK, so the letters ignore the buttons in a frame the keyboard was used. Cancel on the letters with nothing typed leaves.

**On disk.** `Patch/Multiplayer/Player.ini` keeps the player's key (`mp_new_id`), chosen name and bound hotkeys (`MGQ_MpHotkeys`); `Patch/Multiplayer/Favourites.ini` the favourite worlds' ids. Each world entered has `Patch/Multiplayer/Worlds/<id>/` (`mp_world_id`), with `world.ini` (name, world code, the directory's id of the world, its `type` and `share` once the directory told them, when it was made and last played) and `Save/`. *Delete my saves of it* deletes the folder, and with it that this PC knows the password.

**Raid Worlds.** A world is Classic or a Raid World, which its creator picks once (`type`, see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md)). `MGQ_MpWorld.raid?` is the one question every script asks (false outside worlds), and `MGQ_MpWorld.companion_sharing` tells how the open Raid World shares companions (`:off`, `:story` or `:all`; `:off` outside Raid Worlds), both from the open world's `World#raid?` and `World#companion_sharing`, which `world.ini` keeps. The details name the type, and for a Raid World how it shares companions.

**Its own saves.** Entering a world writes the player's own system save, then redirects every path below the game's `Save` folder to the world's (`Files`: the path-taking methods of `File` and `Dir`, and `Bitmap.new` for thumbnails), and loads the world's system save, or makes one (`System`): the Library, the system switches and the global system with the affection, which the game otherwise shares between all saves and loads once per start. The game names `Save/` in many places, its backups, thumbnails and temporary file included, so the paths are changed where they reach the files. Paths only move while a world is open, and a path such as `SaveData.ini` never does. The game's options live in each save, so they are unaffected.

- **Entering** loads the world's latest save, an autosave included, with the game's own `Scene_Load#on_load_success`. A world without saves goes back to the title screen, whose own `command_new_game` starts the new game, so everything the game and other mods do for one happens. Entering also takes a seat in the world's room (`mp_world_open`), which tells the directory the player is online.
- **Leaving** happens whenever the title screen starts other than for that new game: after *To Title*, a reset or a game over. It leaves the room, writes the world's system save and puts the player's own back.
- **PvP battles** through the PvP battle screen are off while a world is open (`MGQ_MpBattlesPvp.available?`): a world keeps a connection of its own, and both would claim the Discord status. Its players duel instead (see [Duels](#duels-battles_duelrbx)), and the World overview's hotkey (F11) opens the World overview.

## World story on the relay (Raid Worlds)

A Raid World plays one story, which the relay keeps: the counters it reads to tell which story is further, and the rest of the story sealed with a key from the world's token, which it never reads. The relay side is `Relay/core/story.js`, the DLL side `RaidStory` (`Network/World/RaidStory.cs`, `StorySeal.cs`, `DirectoryClient.Story.cs`) behind the `mp_raid_*` exports of `Exports/Exports.Raid.cs`. Classic worlds keep no story.

**Where it is kept.** On Cloudflare in the world room's own Durable Object (`World` in `worker.js`), never in the directory object: the Worker sends `/v1/worlds/<id>/story…` straight to the world room, which answers it. One storage value holds the counters and the sealed story (one row written per write), and one value per part holds its checkpoint. The world room asks the directory whether a player holds the world's token (`/internal/member`), keeps the answer (the world's type, the hash of its auth key, the players let in, each with when) and checks later requests itself, asking again for a player let in over 10 minutes ago; a removed player is forgotten, an answer that came while a removal or the deletion ran is not kept, and deleting the world deletes its story, again after a request that was under way. The directory refuses the question while the world's starting save is still being uploaded (409 `pending`), since the sweep of a stale world removes only its entry. The Node relay asks its directory in-process, removes a deleted world's story and pools again after a request that was under way and keeps the stories in memory or in the SQLite tables `stories` and `story_checkpoints`.

### Routes

Every route takes the player's key in `X-MGQ-Player` and the auth key in `X-MGQ-Auth`, as the world room does. 404 for a world the directory lacks, 400 for a missing key, 401 for a wrong auth key, 403 for a removed player, 404 with code `classic` for a Classic world. Each answers JSON.

| Request | Body | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /v1/worlds/<id>/story` | | 200 *state* with `blob` |
| `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/story` | `{"base": <rev>, "counters": {...}, "blob": "<sealed>"}`, at most 300,000 characters | 200 *state* without `blob`, with `"accepted": true`; 409 *state* with `blob`, `error` and `code`; 400 for wrong fields, 413 for a longer body, 429 with code `rate` past 20 writes at once, then one per 3 s, per player |
| `GET /v1/worlds/<id>/story/checkpoint/<part>` | | 200 the checkpoint as *state* with `blob` and `part`; 404 with code `none` when the world keeps none of that part; 400 for another part |
| `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/story/route` | `{"route": "ad" \| "mr" \| "chaos"}` | 200 *state* with `blob` and `"locked": "<route>"`; 409 *state* with `blob` and `code` |
| `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/story/companions` | `{"ids": [<actor id>, ...]}`, 1 to 500 ids of 1 to 99,999 | 200 *state* with `blob`; 413 past 2000 shared companions |

**State** (every answer): `rev` (counts every change: a write, a route lock, a grown companions list), `wrev` (the revision of the last write, whose sealed story `blob` is), `p`, `r1141`, `r1142`, `r1143`, `clear` (the finished routes the last write named), `part` (`1`, `2`, `3`, `ad`, `mr` or `chaos`), `route` (locked: `none`, `ad`, `mr` or `chaos`), `done` (the routes the world finished, in the order it did), `end` (`{"map", "x", "y"}` or `null`), `comps` (shared companions' actor ids, ascending), `checkpoints` (the parts with a checkpoint), `at` (when the last write came, milliseconds since 1970), and `blob` (the sealed story in base64, empty before the first write) where the table says so. A world nobody wrote for answers `rev` 0, `part` `1` and an empty `blob`.

**Counters** of a write: `p` (variable 1001), `r1141`, `r1142`, `r1143` (the route variables; at most one above 0), whole numbers of 0 to 1,000,000; `clear`, the routes whose clear switch is on in the story (7096 `ad`, 7097 `mr`, 7039 `chaos`), each once; `end`, the story teleport's endpoint `{"map": 1-9999, "x", "y"}`, `null` to forget it, left out to keep the one the relay has.

### Writes

- **Accepted** when `wrev <= base <= rev` (the write built on the last sealed story; a route lock or a companion since does not turn it away) and the counters are not behind the story's. Else 409 with the world's state and sealed story, and `code` `rev` (built on an older story), `behind` or `route` (see Route lock); the game takes the world's story, and for `behind` and `route` drops its own step.
- **The story's order** (`storyKey`): `[returned, p, step, cleared]`, compared left to right, where `cleared` is how many routes `clear` names, `returned` how many of them are not the route under way, and `step` the counter of the route under way (the route whose variable is above 0), 0 for none. A finished route returns the world to the Great Decision with 1001 at 39 and the route variables at 0 (CE 154, 267/268), which `returned` counts as further. So the order grows through `p` 39 → AD 1 … 76 → AD 76 with `clear` `ad` → `p` 39 with `clear` `ad` → MR 1 … Once the world finished every route (as a starting save that cleared them all makes it), a route under way no longer counts as returned from and ranks below the decision, so the locked route's first step from the decision (the story's counters with no route under way) is taken whatever its key (`refusalOf`). A write with the same counters is accepted: most events move no counter, and the revision keeps writers apart.
- **Parts** (`partOf`): the route under way if any, else Part 1 while 1001 < 19 (Tartarus Escape sets 19), Part 2 while 1001 < 34 (the Middle Chapter's epilogue sets 34), else Part 3, the Great Decision included (1001 is 40 there before the route variable is set, and 39 after a return).
- **Checkpoints**: a write that first leaves a part keeps the story before it (its last write) as that part's checkpoint, and a later return to the part never replaces it (Part 3's is the first Great Decision, before any route, so the parts stay in the order `world_parts` climbs them). So a part's checkpoint is the story as the last write before the part's first ending left it; a game should write the story before an ending's first step. Nothing is kept from a world without a sealed story yet.
- **Route lock**: the first route locked wins (`POST …/route`, the same route again answers 200). Refused with 409 and `code` `route` while another route is locked or under way (the story's counters on another route, with or without a lock), `finished` for a route in `done` while not every route is (`everyRouteDone`: once the world finished all three, each may be locked again), and `closed` for `chaos` until both `ad` and `mr` are in `done`. While a route is locked, until the world returned from it, a write must be on it or return from it (no route under way, the locked route in `clear`) and may finish no other route the world did not play, else 409 `route`: so a further story from another route (a loaded save) cannot throw the route's progress away or finish a route nobody played. Until the world's own story is on the locked route, a write with no route under way goes on too, since the player who locked it writes the route's first step only once their scene ended. A write with a route variable above 0 locks that route; the lock ends once a write shows no route under way and the locked route is in `done`, newly or after the world's story was on it (the return to the Great Decision), so in a world that finished every route a write at the decision keeps the lock.
- **Finished routes**: every route a write names in `clear` joins `done`, which only grows.
- **Shared companions** only grow; adding ids the list holds writes nothing.

### Push

After every change the relay sends the text frame `story <rev>` to every game in the world room (`storyText`). Games never send it. Released DLLs log it as an unknown text and drop it. This DLL (`WorldSession.TakeStory`) hands it to the game script as the inbox entry `kind=story` with `rev=<rev>` (`mp_world_receive`); the game script fetches the story when that revision is above the newest it knows (`rev` of `mp_raid_story_state`), so its own changes do not make it fetch again.

### Exports

Each starts a request on a thread of its own and returns at once; one request of each kind runs at a time. Text arguments are UTF-8 and null-terminated; `world` is the world's id (`mp_world_id`), and the world must be the open one, whose token seals the story. Win32API notation for `Link::EXPORTS`:

| Export | Arguments | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `mp_raid_story_fetch` | `'p'` world | 1 started; 0 while a fetch runs, without a player, outside that world |
| `mp_raid_story_post` | `'plpp'` world, base revision, counters, story | 1 started; 0 while a write runs, without a player, outside that world, or for counters, a base below 0 or a story (empty or over 200 KB in UTF-8) that are not as they should be |
| `mp_raid_story_state` | `'pl'` (READ_ARGUMENTS) buffer, size | the length, negated when the buffer is too small; 0 before anything was asked |
| `mp_raid_story_headers` | `'pl'` (READ_ARGUMENTS) | as `mp_raid_story_state` without the story's text, for polling while a request runs |
| `mp_raid_checkpoint_fetch` | `'pp'` world, part (`1`, `2`, `3`, `ad`, `mr`, `chaos`) | 1 started; 0 while one runs, without a player, outside that world, for another part |
| `mp_raid_checkpoint_state` | `'pl'` (READ_ARGUMENTS) | as `mp_raid_story_state`, 0 before the first checkpoint fetch |
| `mp_raid_route_lock` | `'pp'` world, route (`ad`, `mr`, `chaos`) | 1 started; 0 while a lock runs, without a player, outside that world, for another route |
| `mp_raid_companions_add` | `'pp'` world, actor ids separated by commas | 1 started; 0 while an add runs, without a player, outside that world, for ids that are not whole numbers above 0 |

**Counters** for `mp_raid_story_post`: `key=value` pairs separated by semicolons, `p`, `r1141`, `r1142` and `r1143` required (whole numbers of 0 or more, at most one route variable above 0), `clear` optional (finished routes separated by commas: `ad` for switch 7096, `mr` for 7097, `chaos` for 7039), `end` optional (`map,x,y`; empty to forget the endpoint; left out to keep it), e.g. `p=40;r1141=12;r1142=0;r1143=0;clear=mr;end=1650,30,51`. **Story**: the text the game script packed (`story_state.rbx` `pack`: deflated, base64, one line); `StorySeal` seals its UTF-8 bytes (AES-GCM, HKDF key from the token with info `mgqmp world story v1`) and unseals the relay's `blob` back into it.

**`mp_raid_story_state`** is a message (header lines, a blank line, the text) like the other states:

- The newest story the relay told of the world the last request started for, from any answer, a newer revision never replaced by an older one; a request for another world forgets the story before, and a late answer of the world before is not kept: `world`, `rev`, `wrev`, `p`, `r1141`, `r1142`, `r1143`, `clear`, `part`, `route`, `done`, `end` (`map,x,y`, missing for none), `comps`, `checkpoints` (lists separated by commas, missing when empty), and the story's unsealed text after the headers (empty before the first write, and for a story that does not open with the world's token, which is kept without it so the game can write over it). A write the relay took leaves the text that was written.
- One header per kind of request that ran, with its revision, code and error: `fetch` (`busy`, `done`, `failed`), `post` (`busy`, `accepted`, `conflict`, `failed`), `lock` (`busy`, `locked`, `taken`, `failed`), `companions` (`busy`, `done`, `failed`); each with `<kind>_rev` (the revision the answer told: an accepted write's own), `<kind>_code` (the relay's code: `rev`, `behind`, `route`, `finished`, `closed`, `rate`, …; the story's `started` answers only an admin's route clear, never a game) and `<kind>_error` (why it failed, for the player). A `conflict` or `taken` already holds the world's story in the headers and the text.
- A request that cannot reach the relay tries again after 2, 5 and 10 s, then fails. A write whose answer was lost comes back on its next try as 409 `rev` with the world's story; when that story's text is the write's own, it counts as `accepted`.

**`mp_raid_checkpoint_state`**: `state` (`busy`, `done`, `none`, `failed`), `part`, `error` when failed, and when done the checkpoint's story headers (as above, `rev` and `wrev` both the revision of its write) and its unsealed text after them.

### In the game (`world_story.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpWorldStory` follows the story after the map's update (`tick`), behind menus and battles too, while a Raid World the directory knows is open (`active?`); the world's id is its `directory_id`. Classic worlds never reach it.

- **Fetching** (`start_fetch`): as the world opens (`note_world`), after a loaded save or a new game (`loaded`, after `load_game_without_rescue` and `setup_new_game`; not after `extract_save_contents`, which a duel's end calls to put the game back), on the relay's push and once a minute (`POLL_FRAMES`) in case a push was lost. The push is the inbox's `kind=story`, which `overworld_sync.rbx` hands to the route `MGQ_MpOverworldSync::STORY_FIELD` marked `:relay` like an admin's chat line (`pushed` ignores a message without it); it fetches only a revision above the newest the game knows. While a request runs, the DLL's headers are read every `STATE_FRAMES` (`mp_raid_story_headers`), and the whole state with the story only once a request's header ended (`ended?`), which takes it; a state whose `world` is not the open world's is never taken, and a request that ended in it is asked again (`other_world`).
- **Laying it over** (`take_pending`, `take_world`): a newer story (`wrev` above the last taken) waits until the player's own map event, battle, transfer or duel ended (`quiet?`). A world without a story, or with one this game cannot open (`wrev` above 0, no text), takes the player's (`force_post`, on the world's `wrev`), unless another route is locked there or the relay refused the player's step: then the game waits for the world's story (`wait_for_world`, mode nil). Until the world's story first came after entering, a load or a new game, the player's story events wait, ten seconds at most (`awaits_world?`, `AWAIT_WORLD_FRAMES`; `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.awaits_world_story`), so their own story never plays before the world's is laid over it. A new game in a world whose story is past its start runs the opening's setup first without its scene (`world_opening.rbx`, `MGQ_MpWorldOpening.before_take`: `:wait` until the player is where the opening ends, then `:opened`), since the opening puts the story back to its start; the story it set up is the player's `entry_story`, so its values count as no own change. Judged afresh (after a load, a new game or entering): a player further than the world (`key_of`, the relay's `storyKey`, or a route chosen at the decision the world stands at that it may take, `decision_step?`) whose story the relay would take (`continues?`, the relay's route rule) writes their story as the world's, the furthest story winning (`start_ahead`), but never again after the relay refused that step (`pending[:drop]`), which takes the world's story instead; a player behind the world and in an earlier part (`part_of`, the relay's `partOf`) is behind (`fall_behind`, mode `:behind`, notice once per world, the story they stood on kept as `@base`, see `base` and `stand_on`): they write nothing, and `world_catchup.rbx` puts them on their part's checkpoint and moves them on by their level (see [Catch-up](#catch-up-world_catchuprbx)); a player the catch-up moves into the world's story (`MGQ_MpWorldCatchup.joins_world?`, after `refetch`) takes it whatever their part, and one whose own progress reaches the world's part (`check_progress`) fetches again, unless the catch-up keeps them behind below their gate (`kept_behind?`, `MGQ_MpWorldCatchup.keeps_behind?`, whatever the mode), as when the world returns from a route to the player's part, and after a load or a new entry into the world, whose `forget` leaves no mode, while the save notes them behind. Otherwise the world's story is joined with the player's own values (`MGQ_MpStoryState.mix` with every chest of `MGQ_MpCoopStoryRewards::CHESTS` and the map's, `chest_list`); variables no story carries, such as lists, stay the player's (`keep_unsent`). What the player changed since the story they built on (`@base`; before the first story taken, the one they brought in, `entry_story`, so progress made while the relay could not be reached is not lost) stays where the world's story did not change it (`keep_own`), so a step being written is not lost.
- **What is the player's own** in a Raid World: `PERSONAL_SWITCHES` and `PERSONAL_VARIABLES`, affection, awakening, the roster, every chest, the side (switches 4/5, after the Great Decision too, `personal_switch?`) and every choice's outcome (`MGQ_MpCoopChoices.personal?`: `raid_keys`, the switches and variables of every branch, 7002/7003, 7007/7008, 7016/7017, 1029, 1056, 1061, 1065 among them). The chests that hold the story's key items (`MGQ_MpWorldCatchupData::CHESTS`, `world_chest?`) are the world's: their self switches go with the story, so each opens once for the world; one the player opened stays open under the world's story or a checkpoint (`keep_open_chests`) and goes with their next write.
- **Writing** (`post_due?`, `send_post`): only in mode `:world`, never while a newer story waits or another write or lock runs. A change of the story's progress (`PROGRESS_VARIABLES`: 1001, 1140-1143 and the areas' progress; `PROGRESS_SWITCHES`: the castle's bosses and the route clears 7096/7097/7039; `check_progress` every `CHECK_FRAMES`) goes out after `SETTLE_FRAMES`; other changes of the shared story, compared with `@base` (`scan`, `scan_due?`) every `SCAN_FRAMES` after an event of the player's own or their telling ended, after progress, kept changes or an accepted write (`@watch`), else every `IDLE_SCAN_FRAMES` while no event of theirs runs (for parallel events), never during the player's telling, go `DEBOUNCE_FRAMES` (5 s) after the last one, at most `LONGEST_WAIT_FRAMES` (30 s) after the first; changes of the player's own values write nothing. While the player tells the story nothing is written but the route lock, a write going again included, since other games would take the scene half done; when the telling ends, what it changed goes out after `SETTLE_FRAMES` (`told`). A story transfer of the player's telling (`story_transfer`, after `Game_Player#perform_transfer`) is the story's endpoint, written then; the place a telling ended is the endpoint of the next write (`note_telling`). The write holds the counters (`counters_text`: the route under way alone, the clears, `end` only when it moved and until a write carrying it is accepted) and the story without the player's own values and chests (`story_text`, `story_lists` and `pack`; without what it turned off when larger than `MAX_FULL_BYTES`), on `@wrev`. Accepted, the story written is the new `@base`; refused (`conflict`), the world's story comes back to lay over the player's, dropping every change of the player's since their base for the codes `behind`, `route`, `finished` and `closed` (`DROP_CODES`), keeping those the world lacks for `rev`, after which what was kept goes again on the new revision; failed, it goes again after `RETRY_FRAMES`, `RATE_FRAMES` for `rate`.
- **The Great Decision**: a route the world has not taken (`route` `none`) is locked (`mp_raid_route_lock`) before the write that starts it, when the relay would lock it (`lock_route`, `open_routes`: not finished, the third way once both others are, every route once the world finished all of them; else the write goes alone and the relay judges it); refused, or a write refused with code `route` as another player locked a route after the player's choice offered every route (`ROUTE_REFUSAL`), the world's story comes back without the player's step, the companions the player's own branch of common event 380 took away come back (`restore_roster`, noted in `decision_list`) before the world's route plays its own half, the notice names the world's route and the player teleports to the story. The game's own choice of the routes (common event 380, `decision_choice?`) offers only `decision_routes` (`before_command`, a `Game_Interpreter#execute_command` hook, which gives the interpreter a copy of the list with the other branches left out, `decision_list`): the locked route while the world has not returned from it, else those not finished, the third way only once both others are and never before, every route once the world finished all three, so nobody reaches its bad end. `Game_Interpreter#ssw` reads `:ed1` and `:ed2` from the world's clear switches 7096 and 7097 (`world_ending`), which the third way's dialogue asks. A player the world's story carries past the decision (`followed`) gets `MGQ_MpCoopChoices.raid_decision`, after `story_passed` for the earlier choices the same story carried them past: the screen of choices offers the world's route alone and plays the player's own half (`raid_decision_commands`): the outcome's part of common event 380 without its transfer (the companions a different side changes), the side switches, and the screen faded in.
- **The return**: the Reaper's reset menu (common event 265, which drops companions) never runs. At a finished route's credits (`route_credits?`: the Angelic Dominion or Monster Realm route under way with its clear switch on, which the credits on map 1021 set) `before_command` puts the game's own return of the player's side in its place (`return_list`, `return_commands`: common event 267 or 268 without its scripts, so without the companions it adds: the Final Chapter's reset 154, 1001 = 39, the warps, variable 57 = 407 and the transfer through map 544), so the players on the credits' map come along as the telling's transfer takes them. At the Reaper's offer in Hades (common event 149) the call is replaced by a fade-in with a notice (`reset_list`); anywhere else, such as the Chaos route's bad end on map 1889, whose event runs by itself and only left the map through the menu, also by Erase Event, and the player teleports to the story: one player must not take the whole world back. The next write names the cleared route, which the relay marks done and whose lock ends. Every other game whose route under way the world finished and left (`wait_for_return`: in its `done` or `clear`, the world's route under way another, and the story this game built on, `@base`, already on that route) plays its own half first (`returned`, `play_queued`: common event 154, which takes the route's passes, resets its maps' chests and the route's progress, variable 1002, and the transfer to the Great Decision's hall through map 544), with a notice, and then takes the world's story, wherever the world went since. A route the player started themselves since the story they built on is no return: a starting save that cleared every route names every route in `done` and `clear` from the first write on, so without that condition any fetch while the player is on a route read as a return.
- **Watching a telling**: once a player on the map whose scene the player watched (`Scope.watches?`) stops telling (`note_teller`), the player's own story events wait (`awaits_teller?`, through `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.hand_over`) until the world's story of another game's later write was taken, at most `AWAIT_TELLER_FRAMES` (10 s), since the teller's write takes seconds to arrive and the scene would play again; the player's own accepted write ends no wait (`took_post` raises `@teller[:wrev]` past it).
- **Saves** hold the story as it is, the world's laid over the player's own (`MGQ_MpCoopStory.save_contents` returns the contents unchanged in a Raid World).

### Catch-up (`world_catchup.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpWorldCatchup` carries a player behind a Raid World's story along, on the tables of `world_catchup_data.rbx` (`MGQ_MpWorldCatchupData`, generated by `raid_data.rb` of MGQ-Paradox-Event-Checker). It runs after the map's update (`tick`), every `CHECK_FRAMES` while the player is free (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.quiet?`), and whenever `world_story.rbx` lays a story over the player's (`carried`).

- **Behind** (`behind`, from `MGQ_MpWorldStory.fall_behind`): the player gets the checkpoint of their own part (`mp_raid_checkpoint_fetch`, `mp_raid_checkpoint_state`), laid over their story with their own values (`take_checkpoint`, `MGQ_MpStoryState.mix` with `chest_list`, `keep_open_chests`) while they are still behind, once per save (the notes' `checkpoint`), so side quests played while behind stay: what the player changed since the story they stood on (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.keep_own` against its `base`) stays where the checkpoint did not change it, and the checkpoint becomes the story they stand on (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.stand_on`), so their changes while behind also survive the move into the world's story, where only what the world's story changed since overrides them. `fall_behind` keeps the story the player stood on as that base rather than clearing it. A part the world keeps no checkpoint of (the world's first story was further), or whose checkpoint this game cannot read, leaves the player on their own story there (`without_checkpoint`), or moves a player moving on to it into the world's story. A failed fetch, which may name no part, is tried again after `RETRY_FRAMES`. A player on a route the world never took moves into the world's story at once.
- **Story held** (`holds_story?`, asked by `coop_events.rbx`): while behind, and from a save's load or the world's entry until the world's story came when the save notes the player behind in their part (`noted_behind?`: the ledger's `checkpoint` is their part, cleared once the world's story is laid over theirs), a story event the player starts does not start and tells them "Reach level N to continue the story." (`refusal_text`, once per event in `REFUSE_FRAMES`, `held_behind`); story that runs by itself (a map event's autorun, a common event's) waits silently, so a teleport onto its map cannot loop; a talk turning into story ends there (`guard`). Side events, talks, shops, chests and battles run.
- **Level gate** (`gate_reached?`, `reached?`): the highest base level among the player's characters against their part's gate (`PARTS`); a character at their own `max_level(:base)` below the gate (another mod's cap) counts as reaching it. Reached, the player moves on (`move_on`, notice "Level N reached: catching up to ..."): to the next of Parts 1 to 3 the world played (`world_parts`) that has a checkpoint (`next_part`), else, and always from the world's own part, into the world's own story (`join_world`, `MGQ_MpWorldStory.refetch`). A route the world finished is never a next part, since the routes stand side by side: a player in Part 3 or earlier moves straight into the world's current story, and the world's story laid over theirs gives the finished route's skills, key items and rewards too (`passed?`), its companions by their gates. They never play the part's ending. A player leaving a route the world finished first gets the personal half of the world's return to the Great Decision (`return_from_route`, `MGQ_MpWorldStory.returned`). Then the rule repeats, one part per gate.
- **Rows by story position** (`review`, `passed?`): a row counts once the story is in the row's part or a later one and the row's marks hold, so an optional event nobody played is never past; a route's rows also once the route is finished (its clear switch), whatever part the story is in, since the return clears its marks; side content by its marks alone. The row's side (before the Great Decision the side the player chose then, noted in the save's notes, `note_side`) and the choices' outcomes among its marks (`MGQ_MpCoopChoices.personal?`) are always read from the player's own story. Each row counts once per save (`ledger`, `$game_system`'s `@mgq_mp_catchup`, per world). A row the player's own game played gives nothing, since the game gave it; one a story laid over theirs carried them past (`carried`: what was passed before is noted done first) gives what it gives, as long as its marks tell that its event ran (`carries?`, the row's `ran`; one only there to play stays the player's own to play, and is noted done once their own story is past it): the story's skills and items (`REWARDS`); the companions a first playthrough loses for good (`REMOVALS` with `first`, variable 912 at 0) leave, unless a join of the same look comes after each of those removals or at the same place (`after?`: a later place, or later in the same event's list); temporary leaves only order the joins. Choices the story carried the player past go through the screen of `coop_choices.rbx` as before (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.followed`).
- **Key items and story chests for everyone** (`world_items`): every key item row (`KEY_ITEMS`, given or taken) and story chest (`CHESTS`) the world's last story is past (`@world_told`) comes to every player once, whatever their part; a chest the world opened shows open for them too (`open_chest`). A key item row with `ran` false, whose event a global flag may use up for the world, gives what the player lacks of its amount (`top_up`), unless the world's story took the item since (`taken_since?`). A player in the world's story who then holds every orb of Part 2 (`ORBS`) below 1001 = 32 plays common event 330 once they are free (`check_orbs`, `play_orb_event`), which only an orb's own event calls.
- **Companions by level** (`companions`, `note_join`, `bring_waiting`): a join the player's story is past waits for its gate (notes `c` carried, `o` held back) unless its companion is there or the player's own game left them out. Once the player's level reaches the gate the companion joins as the game's own join does (`bring_in`: `persona_change`, `recover_all`, `add_actor`, standby by the game's own cut when the team is full, `add_stand_actor` while a story's temporary party plays), raised to the gate unless the join is a first one at the start level; a companion there already is raised to a carried join's gate. Never twice (`in_roster?`; the roster is read once per look, `roster_view`). A removal passed after a join waited drops it. A story event of the player's own that hands out a companion during their telling before the gate of that companion's join in the player's part (`hold_join?`, around `Game_Party#add_actor` and `add_stand_actor`) is held back, notice "<name> joins you once you reach level N.", and the join waits for its gate.
- **Shared companions** (`MGQ_MpWorld.companion_sharing`): every companion new in the player's roster (`note_roster`) goes to the world's list (`mp_raid_companions_add`, at most one write per `SHARE_FRAMES`): the story's companions with *Story companions*, every one with *battle recruits* too. Each companion on the list (`comps`) joins a player who lacks it on reaching its gate (`share_in`): a story companion at its first join's gate and persona, a battle recruit at its database start level; never one the player had (in the roster, or past one of their joins in the player's story, `joined?`), nor one their story took away for good (a lasting removal passed on a first playthrough, `taken_for_good`).
- Notices go to the notification box (`MGQ_MpNotices.message`). A loaded save or new game forgets the session's requests (`loaded`); the notes stay in the save.

## Raid World difficulty (`world_difficulty.rbx`)

A Raid World has no starting point: its create form leaves out *Shared save*, *Player's choice* and *Save*, `Directory.create` sends neither a starting save nor the choice, the relay refuses both (400, code `raid_start`) and a starting save's upload, and `MGQ_MpSaveDistribution.ask_start?` and `fetch?` say no for a Raid World even when its directory entry still names one, so a new player starts a new game. A development build may give one a starting save for testing, see [Raid World starting save](#raid-world-starting-save-development-builds-only). Instead the world sets a **difficulty** for every player, the game's own value of variable 902 (-2 VERY EASY, -1 EASY, 0 NORMAL, 1 HARD, 2 VERY HARD, 3 HELL, 4 PARADOX; `MGQ_MpWorld::DIFFICULTIES` and `DIFFICULTY_NAMES`). The relay keeps it as the world's `difficulty`, set at create and changed by the creator or an admin with an edit; a Raid World made without one, and every Classic world, sets none, and its players keep their own. The list tells it at the end of the world's line, `World#describe` keeps it in world.ini as the world is entered, and `MGQ_MpWorld.difficulty` tells it for the open Raid World.

**Applying it.** Common event 112 works the enemies' rates (variables 41-49) out from variable 902, and the start of every battle runs it again (common event 4), after the troop's enemies were made, so `apply` sets 902 and runs 112 at once in an interpreter of its own, as the game's own choice (common event 110) does. It runs after `DataManager.load_game_without_rescue` and `setup_new_game`, and `tick` (after `Game_Map#update`, every 30 frames) puts the game back on the world's difficulty whenever 902 differs while the map is quiet and no battle runs: after the relay's push, and after a fight put back the difficulty it kept.

**The fights with values of their own.** The game sets values of its own for some fights, the same for every player, and keeps the difficulty they replace in variable 908: the Colosseum during a match (switches 28 and 87, common events 158 and 159: 908 = 902, 902 = NORMAL, and back; a defeat there, common event 3000, puts 902 back and turns 87 off but leaves 28 on until the next win or leave, so 28 counts only with 87), the special bosses on EASY and below (switch 23, common events 10 and 11), the Labyrinth of Chaos (switch 41: common event 112 calls 900, which works the rates out from the Labyrinth's level in 149 alone) and the final battles' fixed NORMAL (switch 507). While any of them is on, nothing here writes 902 or 908 or runs common event 112; the game's own restore (common events 11, 159, 3000) puts the difficulty back, and the next look then finds the world's. The Colosseum's survival course writes the rates itself between rounds, also under switch 28.

**The game's own change.** `before_command` replaces a call of common event 110 (the new game's choice, the choice after a defeat, and the Reaper's difficulty change in Hades, common event 146, which calls it and then erases the Reaper's picture 5, so it runs as the game has it) in a Raid World with a difficulty by setting 902 to the world's and calling 112, with the notice *The world sets the difficulty: HARD.*; while a fight has its own values set, by a comment only. A Raid World without a difficulty and every Classic world keep the game's choice. The Ruler Ruler (common event 100, variables 92-99, EASY and below only) stays each player's own.

**Live after an edit.** The relay answers an edit that names a difficulty with the world room's text frame `difficulty <n>` (`difficultyText`); this DLL (`WorldSession.TakeDifficulty`) hands it to the game script as the inbox entry `kind=difficulty` with `value=<n>`, released DLLs log it as unknown and drop it. `overworld_sync.rbx` hands it to the route of `DIFFICULTY_FIELD`, marked `:relay`; `pushed` keeps it in world.ini, says *The world plays on HELL now.* and the next look applies it. Players who are not in the world read it from the list as they enter.

**The story.** A Raid World's story would carry one player's values to everyone, so `MGQ_MpStoryState` makes them each Raid World player's own (`RAID_PERSONAL_SWITCHES`, `RAID_PERSONAL_VARIABLES`): switches 23, 28, 41, 87 and 507, and variables 41-45 and 49 (the rates), 92-99 (the Ruler Ruler's), 121, 123, 149 and 151 (the Labyrinth's floors, area, level and tier), 902, 903 and 906 (the difficulty, the highest cleared and its check) and 908. Switch 506, the eased scaling the story turns on and off, stays the world's. A Classic party's story is unchanged.

**Battles a player joins.** A guest builds the host's troop in its own game, whose enemies read the rates 41-45 and 48 (HP, attack, defense, agility, instant death resistance, MP) as their parameters are asked, so in a Raid World the host sends its own with the invite, the late invite and the answer `hot_ok` (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.rates_text`, field `rates`), and the guest fights with them (`borrow_rates`, before the troop is set up, also for a rival's or a followed battle) until the battle ends: `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.restore` gives them back (`give_back_rates`), with the level sync's stats, in an after hook on `BattleManager.battle_end`, since the game's victory saves its autosave right after `battle_end` and before `Scene_Battle#terminate`; `ended` calls it again, which puts nothing back twice, and `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.fight_own` gives them back when its own encounter starts alone, also when the wait for the running battle's roster ended as its host left or broke it off (`own_ending?`: `:late`, `:gone`, `:broken`). A special boss's NORMAL, the Colosseum's and the host's Labyrinth level are thus the same in every game of the battle. The rates of experience, job experience and gold (46, 49, 47) stay each player's own.

## Raid boss pools on the relay

Each story boss of a Raid World is one fight the whole world shares. Its pool at the relay keeps how much of the boss's HP is left, as a share of its raid max HP; every battle against the boss reports what it dealt and takes its HP from the pool, so the boss falls only once the world wore the pool down. The pool fills up again by 3% of its max a minute, also while battles fight it, and the battle that empties it defeats the boss for the world until an admin resets it. The relay side is `Relay/core/bosses.js` (the admin routes `Relay/core/raid_admin.js`), the DLL side `RaidBosses` (`Network/World/RaidBosses.cs`, `DirectoryClient.Bosses.cs`) behind the `mp_raid_boss*` exports (`Exports/Exports.RaidBosses.cs`). Classic worlds keep none.

This replaces the earlier pools in kills, where a won battle took a share of a kill off and the boss fell back, its event and the story put back, until the pool was empty. Nothing of that is left: no story, scene or party is put back, no event or autosave waits for the relay, and the relay answers an old body with 400.

**Where it is kept.** Beside the story in the world room's own Durable Object: one storage value per boss (`boss:<key>`), written only by a report that changes the pool and by an admin's reset; regeneration costs no writes, since every read computes the share left from the last report. Access is checked as for the story (`/internal/member`, kept by the world room, whose answer also names the player, `name`, for `by`), the admin routes ask the directory (`/internal/raid-admin`); deleting the world deletes its pools. The Node relay keeps them in memory or in the SQLite table `boss_pools`, as JSON.

**Pool.** Kept as `key`; `max`, the raid max HP the first report named, rounded to a whole number of at least 1 (each game's difficulty and mods set the boss's own max HP, so the first report's stands for the world); `left`, the share of `max` left at the last report, 0 to 1; `at`, that report's time; `defeated`; `by`, the name the world's members knew the player whose report emptied it by, and `byPlayer`, their id, both null while the boss stands; `battles`, the last 32 battles with each one's last `seq`, newest last; and `reports`. The share left now is `defeated ? 0 : min(1, left + 0.03 * (now - at) / 60000)` (`leftOf`, `BOSS_LIMITS.regenPerMinute`). Every answer's `left` is that share at the moment of the answer, so a game adds the regeneration from when the answer came and never reads `at`, which keeps the clocks of relay and game out of it. The key is the one the game sends (see *Keys* below), 1 to 72 characters without control characters or spaces at either end, `#` allowed, percent-encoded in the address (`#` as `%23`). A world keeps at most 500 pools; a record left from the pools in kills reads as never reported, counts toward none and is overwritten by the next report.

### Routes

The player routes take the player's key in `X-MGQ-Player` and the auth key in `X-MGQ-Auth`, with the story routes' refusals (404 no world, 400 missing key, 401 wrong auth key, 403 removed player, 404 code `classic`). Each answers JSON.

| Request | Body | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /v1/worlds/<id>/bosses` | | 200 `{"bosses": [*pool*, ...], "regenPerMinute": 0.03}`, the pools a report touched, by key |
| `GET /v1/worlds/<id>/bosses/<key>` | | 200 *pool*; a pool never reported is full, `{"key": <key>, "left": 1, "max": null, "defeated": false, "at": null, "by": null}`; 400 for a key that is none |
| `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/bosses/<key>/report` | `{"battle": "<id>", "seq": <n>, "delta": <share>, "max": <hp>, "killed": <bool>}`, at most 4 KB; `battle` 1 to 64 letters, digits, `_` or `-`; `seq` a safe whole number of 0 or more, rising within the battle; `delta` the share of the max lost since the battle's last report, -1 to 1, negative for healing; `max` the reporting game's raid max HP, above 0 and at most 1e18; `killed` true, false, 0, 1 or left out | 200 `{"boss": *pool*, "repeat": <bool>}`; 400 for wrong fields, an old `{battle, dealt}` body too; 413 for a longer body and with code `full` past 500 pools; 429 with code `rate` past 30 reports at once, then two a second, per player |

**Pool** as answered: `key`, `left` (the share left now), `max`, `defeated`, `at` (the last report) and `by`.

**A report.** A `seq` at or below the battle's last one is a repeat: it answers the pool with `repeat: true` and writes nothing, so a report sent again after a lost answer counts once; once more than 32 newer battles reported on the pool, a resend from an older battle counts again. A report on a defeated boss, and one on an existing pool that takes nothing off (`delta` 0, no kill), change and write nothing either. Otherwise the first report makes the pool, full, with its `max`; `killed` empties it, any other report takes `delta` off the share left now, clamped to 0 to 1. A pool whose HP shows as 0 (`left * max` below 0.5) is empty: the report that empties it sets `defeated`, `by` and `byPlayer`, and the boss stays defeated until an admin resets it.

**Admin routes** (`ADMINS`): the admin's key in `X-MGQ-Player`, else `player` in the address (GET) or the JSON body (POST, at most 4 KB); 403 for anyone else, the creator included, 404 for no world, 404 code `classic` for a Classic world.

| Request | Answer |
|---|---|
| `GET /v1/worlds/<id>/raid` | 200 `{"story": <state without blob>, "bosses": [*pool* with "hp", "reports", "byPlayer"], "regenPerMinute": 0.03}` (see the story's *state*; `hp` is the HP left now, `round(left * max)`) |
| `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/raid/bosses/<key>/reset` | 200 the full *pool* (`left` 1, `max` null) with `reset` (whether there was one); the pool is deleted, so it is full and undefeated, and the next report sets its max again |
| `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/raid/route/clear` | 200 the story's *state* without `blob`, with `cleared` (whether a route was locked); a cleared lock counts the revision up and pushes `story <rev>`. 409 with the *state* (no `blob`) and `code` `started` while the world's story is on the locked route (`activeRoute(counters)` is the route); that lock ends by itself once the world returns from the route |

### Push

After every report that writes and every reset of an existing pool the relay sends the text frame `boss <left> <max> <defeated> <key>` to every game in the world room (`bossText`): the share left now with up to 6 decimals and no trailing zeros, at least `0.000001` for a pool that stands, so its last HP never read as empty; `max` as a whole number; `1` when defeated, else `0`; and the key last, since it may hold spaces and `#`. A reset sends `boss 1 <the old max> 0 <key>`. Released DLLs drop it as an unknown text. This DLL (`WorldSession.TakeBoss`) hands it to the game script as the inbox entry `kind=boss` with `key`, `left`, `max` and `defeated` (`mp_world_receive`), and to `RaidBosses.Heard`, which keeps it as the pool's newest word and keeps the name of a defeater it knew, since the frame names none; a malformed frame is logged and dropped.

### Exports

Text arguments are UTF-8 and null-terminated; `world` is the world's id (`mp_world_id`), and the world must be the open one.

| Export | Arguments | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `mp_raid_bosses_fetch` | `'p'` world | 1 started; 0 while a fetch runs, without a player, outside that world |
| `mp_raid_boss_report` | `'pppp'` world, pool key, battle id, the report as `seq\tdelta\tmax\tkilled`, such as `3\t0.0125\t5000000\t0` (a whole number from 0, the share lost from -1 to 1 with a point for decimals, the raid max HP above 0 and at most 1e18, `0` or `1`) | 1 queued; 0 without a player, outside that world, while 1000 reports wait, or for a key (1 to 64 characters, `#` allowed), battle id or report the relay would refuse |
| `mp_raid_boss_state` | `'pl'` (READ_ARGUMENTS) buffer, size | the length, negated when the buffer is too small; 0 before anything was asked or heard |

- **Fetching** runs on a thread of its own, one at a time; a relay that cannot be reached, answers 429 or fails itself (5xx) is asked again after 2, 5 and 10 s, then the fetch fails. It takes `regenPerMinute` (0.03 when missing) and the pools: a pool the game knew before the fetch started and the relay no longer lists is forgotten, one the world room told while it ran is kept.
- **Reporting** queues the report and returns; one thread sends the queue in order. A relay that cannot be reached, answers 429 or fails itself gets the same report, with the same `seq`, again after 2, 5 and 10 s, then every 10 s, up to `RaidBosses.MaxReportRetries` (13) tries again, about two minutes. After them the report is dropped with a log line naming its battle and `seq`, and the next one goes out, since the queue goes in order and one the relay never takes would hold back every later one; a dropped report's damage never reaches the pool. Any other refusal (4xx) drops that report at once (`report=failed` with its code and error). Reports are never merged: each is a request of its own.
- **The newest word wins.** The relay tells the world room of a change before it answers, and the two travel apart, so every pool the DLL keeps carries a rising stamp, and an answer to a request sent before the pool was last heard is not taken; it only adds a defeater's name the world room's word lacked. A defeat is thus lifted only by the world room telling the pool stands again (a reset) or by an answer to a request sent after the defeat was heard.

**`mp_raid_boss_state`** is a message (header lines, a blank line, the text) of the world the last request or word was for, empty before either; a request for another world forgets the pools before:

- `world` and `regen` (the share a minute).
- Once a fetch ran: `fetch` (`busy`, `done`, `failed`), `fetch_code` (the relay's code: `classic`, `rate`, `full`, …) and `fetch_error` (why it failed, for the player).
- Once a report was queued: `report` (`busy` while any report waits, else `done` or `failed` of the last), `report_queued` (how many wait), `report_code` and `report_error`; and `report_key`, `report_battle` and `report_seq` of the last report the relay answered.
- After the headers one line per pool, by key: `key`, tab, the share left now (filled up at `regen` since the DLL received it, at most 1, up to 6 decimals, 0 when defeated), tab, `max` as a whole number, tab, `1` when defeated else `0`, tab, who defeated it (empty while it stands).

### In the game (`battles_raid_pool.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpRaidPool` makes the story boss battles of a Raid World one fight against their pools. Classic worlds, and a player whose story is in an earlier part than the world's (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.mode` other than `:world`, `active?`), are never touched.

- **Which battles fight a pool** (`set_up`, after `BattleManager.setup`; `kind_of`, `refusal`): an event's battle (`:event`): the map's interpreter runs a map event (`in_map`, around `Game_Map#update_interpreter`; an event id above 0, so a common event run on its own never counts) whose battle command started the battle (`at_command`, around `command_301`), the troop is a boss's (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.boss?`: a story boss of the table or one of `OPTIONAL`, the one-time Chaos Domain bosses) and the last phase of its fight (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.last_phase?`; an `OPTIONAL` boss has one phase) and not in `UNPOOLED` (the two Great Decision fights, which set the world's route), neither a Hades rematch (switch 97, or anything on the rematch hall's map 576, `hades?`) nor the Colosseum (switch 87) runs, and the story has not beaten the milestone (`BEATEN`: the variables and switches the story writes after each milestone's last fight, alternatives split by `|`, conditions by `&`). The event may have moved the story or put the party together anew before its battle; nothing of it is put back. A co-op guest's battle fights the pools its host's invite names (`:guest`, see *Invites*), a side battle those of its troop (`:side`, see *Side battles*), and the game's retry of a lost battle (`retrying`, around `BattleManager.retry_battle`; `retried`) fights as the lost one did, under a new battle id, its HP taken from the pool, not restored. A random encounter never fights one.
- **Keys** (`key_of`, `member_keys`, `troop_keys`): a fight a milestone lets the player take in any order has a pool of its own under its boss's name (`FIGHTS`: Sisel, EX-Kyubi and the other Chaos route quartet, the five Deadly Sins vessels, Goddess, Demon and Fiend); every other pool is its milestone's (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.milestone_key`), with ` (cap <n>)` where milestones of several caps share a name, such as `Tamamo (cap 61)`, or an `OPTIONAL` boss's name. A boss's key holds no `#` and at most 60 characters (`MAX_KEY_LENGTH`). A troop whose setup makes several enemies (`data_own`: its members whose enemy the database has, such as Kagetsumugi and her dolls) has a pool per enemy, `<key>#<index>` by the enemy's place in the troop from 0; enemies another mod adds mid-battle come after them and fight no pool (`own_count`, `own_enemies`). The two routes' Doppel Lukas share one, so on the second route its pool is defeated already.
- **Tiers** (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses::TIERS`, `TIER_BOSSES`, `tier`, `factor`): every boss has a tier from 0, the strongest, to 9, set by hand per milestone name in `TIER_BOSSES` (the level caps play no part) or with its `OPTIONAL` entry: 0 is Chaos alone, 1 and 2 the Chaos route with its Phenomena of Ruin, 3 the two routes' final bosses, 4 the routes' other bosses (the Archangels and the Six Ancestors among them), 5 to 9 the story before them, the first bosses last. A tier names a factor per stat of the game's (`:mhp`, `:mmp`, `:atk`, `:def`, `:mat`, `:mdf`, `:agi`, `:luk`); a stat it leaves out stays. Now max HP 50 times at tier 0, 5 less per tier down to 5 times at tier 9, and defense and magic defense 5 times at tier 0, falling evenly to the game's own from tier 6 on.
- **Raid stats** (`stat`, `scale_enemies`): the battle's own enemies have their tier's factors on the stats the game computes, through a wrap of `Game_Enemy#mhp` and of each other stat a tier names (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.tier_stats`, wrapped as the game starts), applied on every call and never stored, since the game computes them anew from the difficulty and the states: so a factor always comes after the world's difficulty. So the game's own rules on a share of HP, such as an enemy's HP conditions and the ero offer, read the raised max. An enemy keeps its share of HP as its max is raised, then takes its pool's (`take_on`); a troop a guest builds anew from its host's (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.take_troop`) is raised at once (`rescale`), since the enemies' own max would cut the host's HP. An enemy's HP for a share is `hp_of`: `ceil(share * max)`, at least 1 while any is left.
- **Each command phase** (`command_phase`, around `Scene_Battle#start_party_command_selection`; by load order inside `battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`'s wrap and outside `battles_sync_live.rbx`'s, so it runs before the host records what it sends the guests): the host, or a player who fights alone or took the battle over (`reporter?`: `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.role` not `:guest`), reports first and then sets each own enemy's HP from its pool (`sync`): the share left now (`left_now`, with the regeneration since the pool was told) of the enemy's raised max. An empty pool kills the enemy (`kill`: HP 0 and its collapse), and once every enemy is dead `BattleManager.judge_win_loss` wins the battle as the game's own battles win. Reporting first keeps what the turn's end and the battle's start dealt by themselves (slip damage, automatic skills, troop events). A dead enemy keeps its death, one whose pool the relay told nothing of keeps its HP. A co-op guest never reports and never sets HP from the pools (`Fight#guest`, `reporting?`), not even a kill, since the stream gives it the host's values; once it takes the battle over (`reporting?` finds it the reporter), its `base` starts from its enemies' HP at that moment, so the turn its host reported already is not reported again. As a turn ends (`turn_ended`, before `Scene_Battle#turn_end`) only the enemies whose pool is empty die, which the game's turn end then counts.
- **While the battle waits for commands** (`waiting_for_commands`, after `Scene_Battle#update_basic` while `BattleManager`'s phase is `:input`): every `INPUT_SYNC_SECONDS` (5) the own enemies take their pools' HP again (`sync`), for the host and the guests alike, so the bar shows the other battles' blows and the regeneration while the players choose; nothing of the battle's own runs then, so it undoes none of its damage. A pool emptied meanwhile kills its enemy, and the host wins once all are dead, as at the command phase.
- **Reports** (`report`, at each command phase and as the battle ends, `battle_ended` before `BattleManager.battle_end`, and as an action ends, `action_done` after `Scene_Battle#process_action`, at most every `REPORT_SECONDS` (2) so a long turn's damage reaches the other games while the relay's two reports a second per player hold for a boss of up to four enemies; the turn's own enemies take the pools' HP only at the command phase, never mid-turn): per own enemy not reported dead, the share of its raised max HP it lost since its HP was last taken or reported (`base`; negative for healing, at most 1 either way), as `seq\tdelta\tmax\tkilled` with the battle's next `seq`, under the battle's id (`new_battle_id`), through `mp_raid_boss_report`. An enemy whose HP did not change sends nothing; one that died reports `killed` 1, once. Nothing waits for the relay: a local kill is a kill, the battle wins and its event runs on at once while the DLL sends the queue in the background. Each report is taken off the game's own copy of the pool at once (`note_own`), so a command phase before the relay's answer keeps the damage. A report the DLL refuses leaves the enemy `held`: its HP is not set from the pool until a report goes, which then carries what both turns dealt. A report's `base` counts at most the enemy's max HP now, so a max HP the battle lowered reads as no damage. A guest that takes the battle over reports from its next command phase on, from the HP its enemies had as it took over (see *Each command phase*).
- **What the game knows of the pools** (`@pools`, `Pool`): the push (`pushed`, through `overworld_sync.rbx`'s route `MGQ_MpOverworldSync::BOSS_FIELD`, marked `:relay`, with `left`, `max`, `defeated` and `by`) and the DLL's state lines, read every `STATE_FRAMES` (`read_state`, `take_line`) and only while its `world` header is the open world's. A line replaces the game's copy only when it tells something new: the pool's first line, or one that differs from the pool's last line taken (`@lines`) by more than `LINE_TOLERANCE` beyond the regeneration since, so small changes in a row count once they add up, since a line the DLL keeps giving unchanged would undo the player's own reports meanwhile. Word of more HP than the game counts while a report of that pool waits (`@pending`, the pools of the reports the DLL took, cut to the DLL's `report_queued` at each read, at most `MAX_PENDING`) is older than that report and ignored (`behind?`); a defeat, a reset (a share of 1) and a pool that was defeated are always taken. A standing pool's share is at least `MIN_LEFT` (`share_of`), since the relay and the DLL round it to 6 decimals and the last HP of a large pool would arrive as 0.
- **Fetching** (`fetch_on_move`, from `tick`): `mp_raid_bosses_fetch` as the player enters the world's story and on each map change; a fetch the DLL did not start is tried again after `FETCH_RETRY_FRAMES`. Entering another world forgets the pools first.
- **A pool empty already**: a battle against it starts with its enemies dead and is won at once, and its host invites nobody (`won_already?`, from `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.invitees` and `pools_won?`), not even the host of a waiting invite (`with_waiting_host`).
- **Defeat** is the game's own, and what the battle dealt stays in the pool; the lost battle is kept for the game's retry (`@lost`).
- **The boss falls for everyone**: once a pool is empty, every battle fighting it kills that enemy at its next command phase or turn end and wins once all its own enemies are dead, with the game's own rewards. Once every pool of a boss is empty, the chat log and the notification box say "\<boss> is defeated for the whole world." once (`announce`, from the push; `back_up` allows it again after a reset).
- **The story**: the teller's event runs on after its battle as after any battle, and the story moves on for the world once, through the story's normal write (see [In the game (`world_story.rbx`)](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)).
- **Invites** (`invite_fields`, `note_invite`): a battle that fights pools adds `pool`, how many own enemies its troop has, and for a side battle `side` = 1, to its invite, its late invite (`MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.invite_fields`) and its retry's invite (`retry_of?`). The guest notes it through `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.note_invite`, also as it follows or yields to a rival's battle (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.take_pool`, which runs `set_up` for the troop taken), and derives the keys from the troop as its host did, so it fights the same raised max HP and can report should it take the battle over. A boss battle also invites the players watching the story its host tells (see *Who joins* under [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)).
- **Side battles** (`state_fields`, `side_of`, `start_side`): the host of a boss battle, or a player who fights one alone, tells `rpb` in its state (`STATE_FIELD`): the troop and its enemy rates (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.rates_text`), such as `71;100,100,0`, while its battle scene runs and the boss's pools are not all empty, else empty. Near such a battle that takes the player in no more, `battles_coop_join.rbx` offers a side battle on the same hotkey (see *Joining by choice* under [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)). `start_side` borrows the host's enemy rates (`borrow_rates`), sets the same troop up with escaping off and losing allowed (`BattleManager.setup(troop, false, true)`) and calls the battle scene: a battle of the player's own (`:side`) against the same pools, which starts at once and invites and holds nobody (`side_battle?(troop)`, the public check `battles_coop.rbx` asks to skip gathering and invites), though other players near it join it by choice; it runs no map event, none of its troop's battle events (`without_pages`, which empties the troop's pages while the battle runs) and writes no story. It wins once its own enemies die, by its own blows or as the pool empties; a defeat returns to the map without a game over. Its EXP, gold and drops come only with the first side battle won against that boss (`note_rewarded`); a later one gives none (`withhold_rewards?`, around `BattleManager.enable_get_exp?`, `enable_get_gold?` and `enable_get_drop?`, `REWARD_CHECKS`), for the players who join it too, whose invite tells `side`; the boss's own story battle always gives them. The save keeps the bosses rewarded in `$game_system`'s `@mgq_mp_raid_rewards` (`REWARDS_FIELD`): `{"world" => <world id>, "keys" => {<boss key> => true}}`, started anew in another world. It never starts for a player behind the world's story, against a boss their story beat, or on an empty pool.

### HP bars (`ui_raid_bar.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpRaidBar` and `Sprite_MpRaidBar` draw the raid bosses' HP.

- **The bar** (`draw_bar`): the boss's title in the preset's title color (`TITLE_COLOR`, gold in Monarch; `MGQ_MpRaidBosses.title`: the group the game's enemy library names it in, such as *Archangel*, *Six Ancestors*, *Phenomenon of Ruin* or, from the Chaos Domain's events, *Chaos' Phantom*; else *Tier \<n> Raid Boss*) centered across the bar, below it a row of gauges, one per enemy of the boss (`Bar#gauges`, each a `Gauge` with the enemy's name, HP and max), centered: the row's width (on the strip 65% of the screen's width, at most the strip's less its margins, `strip_row_width`; the overhead bar's width inside its frame) is shared among them with `GAUGE_GAP` (6) between, and each enemy's name in white is centered over its gauge; each line of text on the style's plate (`Style::PLATE`) as wide as its text plus `Style::PAD` on either side (`draw_plate`), the title's capped to the bar's width and a name's to its gauge's, the plates stacked onto each other and onto the gauges' frames without a gap. The names of a boss of several enemies share one font size, the largest from the layout's down to `MIN_NAME_SIZE` (14) at which every name fits its gauge (`name_size`), a name still too long cut with an ellipsis, a shrunken name drawn in its row's full height so it stays centered over its gauge; a boss of one enemy draws as a single gauge the row wide, its name and plate centered across the bar as the title (`name_box`). Every text is drawn through the shared style (`MGQ_MpUi::Style.use_font`, `draw_outlined`, `draw_plate`): the game's outline in solid black (`Style::OUTLINE`, the game's own is half see-through) widened by a copy above, below and beside it (`Style::OUTLINE_REACH` 1, since more fills the small letters in), never bold, which smears VL Gothic; the strip's title line names the party box hotkey that makes it small at its right (`BARS_HINT`). The gauge sits in a black frame with a thin edge in the preset's `EDGE` (gold in Monarch); its filled part is a maroon gradient (`FILL_TOP`, `FILL_MIDDLE`, `FILL_BOTTOM`) over the preset's empty part (`EMPTY`, `Style::WELL`). The HP inside the gauge (`draw_numbers`) is shortened by `MGQ_MpRaidBar.short_numbers`: the largest unit of `UNITS` (K, M, B, T, Qa, Qi) that leaves the max at least `MIN_DIGITS` (7) digits, the HP rounded up so a boss with HP left never reads 0, such as "236,167 B / 1,009,500 B", but never past the max, which rounds down; then the share left ("68.3%", "<0.1%" while any is left) when it fits, else the HP alone, else the share alone.
- **The damage trail** (`follow_trails`, a `Trail` per gauge): every change of a gauge's share left is noted with until when the enemy had the HP before it; the gauge shows, past its filled part, the most of the max HP the enemy had within the last `TRAIL_FRAMES` (120) in a darker maroon (`TRAIL_TOP`, `TRAIL_BOTTOM`), which then drains down to the HP left by `TRAIL_EASE` of the way a frame (at least `TRAIL_MIN_STEP`), drawn every `TRAIL_REDRAW_FRAMES` (2) meanwhile; healing and the regeneration rise at once, a hit on one gauge leaves the others' trails alone, and a bar whose names differ from the last shown starts every trail anew. A bar is drawn again only when its title, a gauge's name or numbers or a trail changed, numbers at most every `REDRAW_FRAMES` (6).
- **In battle** (`update_battle`, after `Spriteset_Battle#update`): while the battle fights pools, the strip, across the top of the screen until the player moves or narrows it (`place_strip`, the layout box `:raid_bar`, see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)), 640x86 at most (`STRIP_HEIGHT`, `STRIP_LAYOUT`: title 20, name 24, a 30 px gauge 65% of the screen wide with its numbers in 21) at z 90 (`BATTLE_Z`: under the game's windows, which start at 100, over the battlers and pictures). It has a gauge per own enemy of the battle (`battle_bar`), named as the game names it (`Game_Enemy#name`, which letters enemies of one name *A*, *B*, ...), with the enemy's share of its raised max times its pool's max once the relay told one, else its raised max. It hides while the game's message at the top shows (`top_message?`), which draws over it. The party box's hotkey (`MGQ_MpRaidBar.on_battle`, Tab unless bound) crops it until only its gauges show (`crop`: its `src_rect` steps down over `Style::SLIDE_FRAMES` (8) by `STRIP_HEIGHT - SMALL_STRIP_HEIGHT`, the strip staying where the player placed it) and back again, kept in `Player.ini` (`raid_bar_small`), a new battle's strip starting where it stands. While it shows, the game's own HP bar of a boss (Basic Enemy HP Bars Lite: the sprite's `@hp_bar`, the `MGQ_MpGame` field `hp_bar`) moves down below it, following the crop (`push_down`, after `Sprite_Battler#update_hp_bar`, by `battle_bottom`), only while the strip lies at the top of the screen.
- **On the map** (`MapBars`, after `Spriteset_Map#update`): every `SCAN_FRAMES` (30) and on each new map the map's events are looked through (`boss_events`): an event whose page starts a battle by troop id (command 301, designation 0), directly or in the common events it calls (command 117, followed into common events that call others, each once; `troops_of`, kept per page), against a troop that shows a bar (`shown_troop?`: a last phase not in `UNPOOLED` with pool keys, never during a Hades rematch or in its hall, `MGQ_MpRaidPool.hades?`, whose pool the game knows or whose milestone the player's story has not beaten). Above each such event that shows (`shows?`: a character's or a tile's picture, markers such as `!Flame` included, neither transparent nor hidden) a 52 px high bar, 240 wide (`OVERHEAD_WIDTH`) for a boss of one enemy and 480 (`OVERHEAD_WIDE_WIDTH`) for one of several, so the names still read, its picture remade when the width it needs changes (`fit`; `OVERHEAD_LAYOUT`: title 16, names 18 and thin 8 px gauges without numbers), under the event's feet when the screen has no room above it (`place_over`). While no such event shows, the first boss's bar is the battle's strip, numbers included, where the player placed it (the same `:raid_bar` box as in battle), and while it lies at the top of the screen `MGQ_MpUi.top_room` tells its bottom, which the notification box and the party box move down by while at their default places. The bar (`map_bar`) has a gauge per pool key of the troop (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.troop_keys`, in the troop's order), named by the database's enemy, a name that repeats within the troop lettered as in battle (`map_names`, with `Game_Troop#letter_table`), each pool's share left now times its max; a pool the relay told no max of counts the enemy's own (`raid_max`: the enemy's max HP at the player's difficulty, `Game_Enemy.new(0, id).mhp`, kept per enemy and variable 41, times its tier's max HP factor), full before any report. A side battle's prompt also counts the steps to these events (`distance_to_boss`).

## Raid World starting save (development builds only)

> **TODO: remove before a release.** This exists only for testing: a release must not let anyone give a Raid World a starting save. Before the next release, take out every part listed here: `mp_dev_build` (`Exports.cs`, `Link::EXPORTS`, the `ParkingExports_AreRegistered` assertion), `UpdateCheck.DevelopmentBuild` with `IsDevelopmentVersion` and `DevelopmentVersion`, `DirectoryClient.DevFeature` and `FeaturesOf` (`Features` back to `raid`), `FEATURE.dev` with the `features` of `Directory#create`, `Directory#putStart` and `handleDirectoryRequest`'s `client` (the servers' `client.features`), the TestRelay's `DevGame`, `MGQ_MpWorld.dev_build?`, `Form#raid_save?` with the Raid World's *Save* and its row-sharing *Difficulty* (`RAID_SAVE_LABEL_WIDTH`, `RAID_SAVE_HINT`), the `dev_build?` in `Directory.create`, `MGQ_MpSaveDistribution.fetch?` and `Window_MpWorldDetail#raid_start_text`, and the tests and test plan checks named *development build* or *dev feature*. Until then a release already behaves as before, since `release.yml` stamps the tag's version, which is never `0.0.0-dev`, pre-release tags like `v0.6.0-beta` included.

A development build (version exactly `0.0.0-dev`, the project's own stamp, which `UpdateCheck.IsDevelopmentVersion` checks; any other version, a pre-release one included, is not; `UpdateCheck.DevelopmentBuild`, told to the game script by `mp_dev_build` and asked through `MGQ_MpWorld.dev_build?`, read once) may give a Raid World a shared starting save, as a Classic world's *Shared save*. Never *Player's choice*.

- **DLL.** `DirectoryClient.Features` is `raid,dev` in a development build, `raid` in a release, on every request and the world room's WebSocket.
- **Relay.** `Directory#create` takes a Raid World's `start: true` and `Directory#putStart` its upload only when the request's `X-MGQ-Features` names `dev` (`FEATURE.dev`; `handleDirectoryRequest` reads it from `client.features`, which `server.js` and `worker.js` fill with `parseFeatures`), else 400 with code `raid_start`; `choose: true` stays refused for every Raid World. Getting the save (`getStart`) and entering (`admit`, which refuses `pending`) are as for a Classic world.
- **Form.** In a development build a Raid World's *Create new world* form shows *Save* (`:optional`, label width `RAID_SAVE_LABEL_WIDTH`) on the left of row 4 and *Difficulty* on its right, in place of the Difficulty across the row, so the form keeps its height. There is no checkbox: the save chosen is the shared save (`Form#from_save?`), *The beginning* shows while none is, and leaving the save screen without a save sets it back to none (`take_start_save`). `Directory.create` then sends the starting save as for a Classic world, `create_world` places it in the creator's folder, and a new player of the world fetches it (`MGQ_MpSaveDistribution.fetch?`); `ask_start?` still never asks. The details' *Start* says *The creator's save* for a Raid World whose save is `ready` in a development build (`raid_start_text`), *The beginning* in a release, which never fetches it.
- **Story.** Nothing in the story is special to a world from a save: the relay's story is empty until the first write, and `MGQ_MpWorldStory.take_pending` writes the player's own story as the world's whenever the world has none (`wrev` 0) and nothing locks a route, so the first game in the world writes the save's story on revision 0 instead of being taken back to the beginning, and the relay's story starts in the save's part. Every later player from the same save is in the world's part, so the world's story is laid over theirs (`take_world`), never behind; a player whose save is further writes theirs (`start_ahead`, the furthest story wins). The catch-up's notes start empty in a save from outside the world, and `carried` reviews the player's own story first, so the rows the save played give nothing and take no companion. A new game in such a world is behind the world's part (Part 1 against the save's), with no checkpoint of the parts before the save's, so it keeps its own story there, its story events held, until its level reaches the part's gate, as for any part the world keeps no checkpoint of.

## Starting save (`world_save_distribution.rbx`)

A world's creator may start every new player from one of their own saves instead of the opening, or let each new player choose (*Player's choice*, the directory's `choose` flag). Both are chosen once, when the world is made, and never change.

- **Picking.** `Scene_MpStartSave` is the game's own `Scene_Load`, whose choice is handed back instead of loaded. `files_of` writes the system save first, then names the save, its thumbnail when it has one, and the newer of the game's two system saves (`SaveSystemData.load_filename`), under the names a new player gets them by: `Save01.rvdata2`, `Save01.png` and `SystemSave.rvdata2`. So the starting save is the world's first save, and the world's system save (Library, medals, affection) is the creator's.
- **The creator** gets the files copied into their folder of the new world (`place`) and enters it from them, with nothing fetched back.
- **A new player** fetches them (`fetch`, a directory action of kind `start`) when entering a world whose list entry says `ready` and whose folder holds no save yet. `check` then loads every object of the save and the system save before the world opens, since the game quits when a system save fails to load. A class the game lacks, from a mod only the creator has, is named in the message, and the files are thrown away, so the next entry fetches them again.
- **Player's choice.** Entering a `choose` world whose folder holds no save yet (`ask_start?`) asks first: *From the creator's save* (only when it is `ready`) fetches as above, *At the beginning* starts a new game, and *From one of my saves* opens `Scene_MpStartSave` again and copies that save with `files_of` and `place`, as the creator's own copy is made. The copy waits for the world screen's next frame (`@own_start`), since entering loads the map, which must not happen while the scene starts. The creator is asked too when they made the world without their own save. Once the world holds a save, nobody is asked again.
- A world whose creator is still uploading (`pending`) cannot be entered.

## World mods (`world_mods.rbx`)

A world's required (`!`) mods must be the same in every game that enters it. The relay keeps a catalog of the admins' mods, which only they fill in the World Admin tool (see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md), mod catalog): each mod's current version, the versions seen before, and a SHA-256 per file, `.rb` files without carriage returns (`ModHash`), so a checkout with Windows line ends and the release asset hash the same.

- **Kinds.** `link`: a script of a release on the admins' GitHub, which the game downloads from GitHub and installs where its copy is, else as `Patch/<file>`. `zip`: a link to a zip of a release, laid out as in `Patch`. `upload`: a mod of several files an admin uploaded, which the relay hands out as a zip. A zip's files go to their paths inside `Patch` (`ModInstaller`, which refuses any path that leaves it); the DLL tells the script `zip` for a link whose release file is one (`CatalogMod.ScriptKind`).
- **The check.** The catalog comes with the list (`mp_mods_list`). `differing` hashes this game's copy of each required mod (`mp_mod_hash`) and names the version the hashes are, "unknown" or "not installed"; a mod outside the catalog is compared with the creator's hash (`modHashes` of the world, sent with create and edit), and a copy that differs can only be fetched from its author. Without a catalog or a creator's hash, being installed is enough, as before.
