While the list is fetched again the DLL still hands out the catalog it had (state `loading`), which the script keeps using; a link the relay could not read yet (no version, no files) is listed for admins only and left out by the script, so it counts as a mod outside the catalog. The creator's hashes leave out names longer than 100 characters, as many as the relay takes.
- **Download and restart.** *Download and restart* installs every differing catalog mod (`mp_mods_install`, all files checked and staged before any is written, every replaced file put back when one cannot be written, and a zip mod's files that only an older version shipped deleted while they are still that version's), stores the world in Player.ini (`rejoin`), starts `Game.exe` again once this game has ended (`mp_restart_game`) and quits; the title screen then opens the world screen and enters that world again, checking anew. Under Wine or Proton `mp_restart_game` answers 2 and starts nothing: the game stays open, keeps `rejoin` and tells the player to restart it by hand, which then enters the world. An outdated game started again offers the update on the title screen instead (`MGQ_MpWorld.offer_update`), as a Discord invite into a world does. Updates thus reach a player only on their next entry.
- **Settings.** The world's `settings`, Mod Config values of every mod it names, required or listed (no key bindings, no `:personal` rows), are applied whenever a save of the world is loaded or started (`load_game_without_rescue`, which the newest translation's replaced `load_game` still calls), and Mod Config Remake shows them greyed out (`ModConfigRemake.world_keys`) until the player leaves the world. The creator's own stay unlocked: in their own world (`own_world`), the button *Use My Settings for This World* that `world_mods.rbx` adds to Mod Config Remake's rows (pressed through the handler of its key, set on every `Window_ModConfig`) sends their current values through `mp_dir_set_settings`, at most 2000 characters (options past them are left out, logged and named in the help line and the notice), and reports the answer as a notice. Creating and editing a world send no settings; the relay lets the creator and its admins replace them. A game applies only the settings of options a world may set (`world_option?`) in a mod's group of its own Mod Config, never the game's own options, key bindings or personal rows (`apply_values`). The relay's latest settings win: before the creator's game sends settings, and before it tells another player whose state names other settings than its own (unless it read them less than `CHECK_FRAMES` ago), it reads the world's settings on the relay through a fresh list (`check_relay`, `mp_dir_refresh`, started only while no list is being fetched and no request of its own runs). Settings someone else set there, such as an admin, become the world's, the creator's options and every player's (`take_relay`); settings the creator's game wants meanwhile keep only what the creator changed, joined to the relay's (`merged_text`), and only the creator's own switch turns sharing on or off. The DLL keeps one directory action's answer for the world screen and the world's settings alike, so each claims the action it starts (`Directory.claim`) and takes only its own answer (`Directory.mine?`); a request starts only once nobody waits for the last answer (`Directory.free?`), and settings whose answer gave way to another request are sent again. A world's settings are noted only as the world opens (`start_world`), so an entry refused for its data, a full world or a password leaves nothing behind, and the edit form starts from the settings the relay took from this game last while the list may be older (`settings_of`).
- **Options for the catalog.** Mods build their Mod Config rows as they load, often from constants and method calls, so only a running game knows them. Whenever the world screen reads the list and the player is an admin, `report_options` sends, once a session per mod, the options of each catalog mod this game has installed, whatever its version, unless the relay holds the current version's options already or those of the version this copy is (`opts` line of `mp_mods_list`): the version `row_for` found by the copy's hashes, or an empty one for a copy that matches no version the catalog knows, such as a build of the admin's own; the relay keeps any version's options until the current version's arrive. The options are the same rows a world may set (`world_entries`), each with its name without the `[Mod]` prefix, its type and default from `NWConst::Config::DEFAULT`, and its choices from its `:values` (an array or a proc) or else `DATA`, named by `DATA_TEXT`, as tab-separated lines through `mp_mods_options`. A mod that offers no such rows sends an empty list, so the relay knows it offers none. The DLL posts them on a thread of their own (`SendModOptions`), outside the actions, and only logs the answer. The World Admin tool lists them in its mod settings dialog.

## Turn off other mods (`world_mods_parking.rbx`)

A world whose `otherModsOff` is true is played with the mods it names and nothing else: while a player is in it, every script of their `Patch` folder whose mod the world's mods do not name (required `!`, essential `?` or only listed) and the relay's catalog does not mark personal is **parked**, moved out of `Patch` into the game folder's `Parked mods`, below which it keeps its path (`Patch/Invasions.rb` goes to `Parked mods/Patch/Invasions.rb`). The mod loader loads every `.rb` below `Patch`, subfolders included, so a script belongs to the mod named by the folder right under `Patch` it sits in (`Patch/Luka_Replacer/heroes.rb` to Luka Replacer), and a script right in `Patch` to the mod its own name names. The folder lies outside `Patch`, so the mod loader never sees it, and on the same volume, so each move is a rename; a script behind a junction or link to another drive is never parked, since a move there copies and a crash would leave half a script (see `mp_mods_scripts`). Only `.rb` files move: a mod's data folder, such as `Patch/Luka_Replacer/`, stays, and so does everything outside `Patch`. The mod loader reads the folder's files once as the game starts, so the scripts a game runs only change with a restart: every park and every restore goes with one. The relay does not check it: a build without it enters such a world with every mod on, but once a newer release is out an outdated game cannot enter worlds at all (see the update check under [Runtime](#runtime)).

**The setting.** The relay keeps it as the world's `otherModsOff` (see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md)): sent with create (`mp_dir_create`'s last argument; the create form's *Turn off other mods* follows the type, on for a Raid World, until the player ticks it), changed by the creator or an admin with an edit (`mp_dir_edit`'s last two arguments, value and whether it goes along), listed as field 22 of the DLL's world line, which the DLL fills by type when the relay leaves it out, as a world kept before the field is listed (`ListedWorld#other_mods_off`). The details show *Other mods: turned off (2 of yours)* or *Other mods: allowed* (`other_mods_text`), and the mods box adds *Your mods turned off here* with the player's mods the world turns off, one line per mod with its scripts counted when it has several (`mod_lines`, `MGQ_MpModParking.mods_of`); the details count mods the same way. The relay refuses a mods text past 300 characters (400) instead of cutting it, so the mods a game parks by are the mods the world keeps.

**Personal mods.** The catalog may mark any mod personal, and may hold mods by their name alone (kind `name`, nothing to download), which exist only to be personal, such as someone else's cosmetic mod. The relay's list body carries every personal mod in `personal` (key and name, checked or not); the DLL keeps them in `ModCatalog.Personal` (`CatalogMod.Personal`, the last positional parameter, which the World Admin tool compiles too) and adds one line `personal`, key, name per mod to `mp_mods_list`'s text, never offering a `name` entry for download. `MGQ_MpWorldMods.personal_keys` keeps the keys and the keys of the names, nil while the catalog is unknown (a catalog `loading` that still carries the last payload counts as known).

**Which scripts.** `MGQ_MpModParking.scripts` reads the scripts in `Patch` and those parked already from the DLL (`mp_mods_scripts`, UTF-8, since the game's Ruby lists file names in the system's code page), nil when the DLL cannot list them; the paths go back to `mp_mods_park` exactly as they came. `to_turn_off(mods)` takes them less those every player has (`shared?`: the keys `patch`, `multiplayer` and `0modconfigremake`, compared by `MGQ_MpWorld.mod_key`, and anything below `Patch/Multiplayer/`, which the DLL leaves out too), less those whose mod (`owner_key`: `mod_key` of the folder right under `Patch`, or of the file's own name right in it) the world names (`MGQ_MpWorld.mods_of`) or the catalog marks personal; nil while the scripts or the catalog are unknown and a script might be personal. `plan_for(listed)` is none for a world that allows other mods. Scripts the DLL lists as `elsewhere` (behind a link to another drive, `MGQ_MpModParking.elsewhere`) are never parked and count as on in an open world. `change_for(listed)` tells what entering needs: nil when the scripts parked are those already, the scripts to have parked (none puts every parked one back), `:elsewhere` when the world would turn off a script behind such a link (`stuck_for`, `elsewhere_text`), `:unlisted` when the DLL cannot list the scripts, `:unknown` while the catalog is unknown (unless the parked ones are this world's, which stay), or `:restart` when this session moved scripts since it started (`note_start`, `moved_here?`), since the scripts it runs then are not those the folder holds.

**Entering.** `Scene_MpWorlds#on_enter` asks, in this order, whether the world is open now (`open_now?`: no upload pending, a seat free), whether its required mods match (`mods_allow?`), whether the parked scripts fit it (`parking_allows?`) and whether the game data matches (`data_allows?`). `parking_allows?` refuses while the catalog is unknown ("The mod catalog has not loaded yet. Try again in a moment."), while the DLL cannot list the scripts ("Your Patch folder could not be read"), while a script it turns off lies behind a link to another drive ("2 mod scripts lie on another drive behind a link in Patch and cannot be turned off: ...", naming the first) and after a move in this session ("Restart it first"). When the scripts parked are those the world turns off but the journal names another world, `claim` parks the same list for this world (`mp_mods_park`, which moves nothing and only rewrites the journal's world), so the start after any restart into this world keeps them parked; `restart_for_mods` does the same before it restarts for downloads alone, and then restarts through `rejoin_parked` (`mp_mods_restart_parked(world, 1)`), whose new watch id makes the watchdog that started this game stand down, so it cannot put the scripts back while the next game starts. A plain `mp_restart_game` would leave that watchdog to put them back during the next game's load, so while scripts stay parked and could not be parked, claimed or kept for the world (`:not_kept`), the game stays on the world screen with "The mods were installed, but your other mods could not be turned off or kept off for \<world>. Try again." (`keep_open_for_mods`, `NOT_KEPT_TEXT`); only with nothing parked does a failed park fall back to `mp_restart_game` for the downloads alone. A world this PC never entered has its lock opened first (`unlock_first`), since a password or a Discord invite's code does not survive the restart. Then it asks "These mods are turned off while you play in \<world>: ... Restart and join?" (*Restart and join*, *See the mods*, *Back*), or, when the world allows the scripts parked for another, whether to put them back and restart. *Restart and join* runs `park_and_rejoin`: `mp_mods_park` with the plan, which counts as done only when `mp_mods_parked` then lists exactly the plan (a partial park is put back at once), `rejoin=<world>` in Player.ini, and `mp_mods_restart_parked(world, 1)`; the game quits and the watchdog starts it again. Answer 2 (Wine) keeps the scripts parked and tells the player to restart by hand, which enters the world; 0 clears `rejoin`, puts the scripts back and refuses. Putting back the scripts parked for another world answers `:held` when `mp_mods_restore` says -2, and the screen says "Another game of this folder has mods turned off. They come back when it closes." When required mods are downloaded too, `restart_for_mods` installs them first and then parks, so one restart covers both, and the download offer names the scripts it turns off. A creator who enters their own world with its other mods off and finds the data differing is asked to take the game's data as it is now (`parked_for?`), since the world's scan may have been made with those mods on.

**The start after the restart.** The first `Scene_Title#start` of a session (`title_started`, a `before` block that runs before `MGQ_MpWorld` closes the world) calls `mp_mods_recover` with the `rejoin` world (`startup`): 2 holds the parked scripts for that world (`holding?`) until it is entered (`entered`, from `MGQ_MpWorld`); 1 means an earlier game closed with scripts parked for another world and they came back, so the title says "The game closed while mods were turned off. Your mods are back after a restart." and starts the game again; -1 tells the player some stayed parked; -2 (another game of this folder holds the lock) is that game's business, so the start only logs it. After 2, -1 or -2 the first title screen leaves the parked scripts alone (`kept_at_start`). Player.ini's `mods_back_restart` is the loop guard: a start right after such a restart that has to put scripts back again only tells the player to restart. Every way the held entry ends without entering the world (the list lacks it, it is full or its upload pending, a data or parking question or the password answered with Back, the lock not opened, an outdated game, the screen closed) calls `give_up`: the scripts come back, and the title starts the game again with the reason.

**Leaving.** `MGQ_MpWorld.on_title_start`, after it left the world (not for the world's own new game, nor the held start's first title screen), calls `on_title_start` with whether a world was open, which puts the parked scripts back (`mp_mods_restore(1)`, `leave_parked`) when this session parked or kept them (`park`, recover answer 2 or -1) or the player left a world, and otherwise leaves them to the game that parked them; the title screen then shows "Your mods are back after a restart." for `RESTART_FRAMES` (3 s), its commands held (`title_busy?`, which also keeps the world screen from opening), and from its update, never at load time, calls `mp_restart_game` and `SceneManager.exit`. Where the game cannot restart itself, the box tells the player to. When `mp_mods_restore` answers -2, another game of this folder holds the scripts, so the box says "Another game of this folder has mods turned off. They come back when it closes." in place of the restore-failed text and nothing restarts. A notice waiting for the title, such as why the world was left, goes in front of the text. After a restore, here or at the start's recover, the `conflicts` header of `mp_mods_parked` adds "2 of your mods went to Parked mods/Conflicts because another file took their place in Patch." to the title's text (`with_conflicts`, `CONFLICTS_TEXT`). `mp_update_game` marks the journal `update` for the updater's run, so the watchdog never starts a second game, and only once it answered that the updater started does `UpdateOffer.update_and_install` put parked scripts back (`restore_for_update`), before `SceneManager.exit`; an updater that did not start leaves them parked until the next start.

**No saves without the mods.** `world_mods_parking.rbx` wraps `DataManager.save_game` and `auto_save_game` (`save_allowed?`): outside a world, while the session started with scripts parked (`@start_parked`) or scripts are parked, a save is refused with "Saving is off until your other mods are back. Start the game again to bring them back." in the notification box and the autosave is skipped silently (`saving_off?`), so a single-player save never holds a game run without the player's mods. Saving inside a world works as usual.

**A journal the DLL cannot read.** When `mp_mods_parked` answers with the `error` header, `parked_state` keeps the state it read last and logs once (`PARKED_FAILED`), so a moment the journal is locked never reads as nothing parked.

**The rule changing.** An edit that changes `otherModsOff` or the mods makes the relay send the world room `mods_rule <0|1>` + tab + the mods (`modsRuleText`; Cloudflare through the world room's `/internal/mods-rule`, Node through `tellWorld`). The DLL (`WorldSession.TakeModsRule`) hands it to the script as the inbox entry `kind=mods_rule` with `value` and `mods` (left out when empty), ignoring and logging a value other than 0 or 1 or mods past 4096 characters; released DLLs drop it as an unknown text. `overworld_sync.rbx` routes it to `MODS_RULE_FIELD`, marked `:relay`, and `pushed` takes the mods (`MGQ_MpWorldMods.take_mods`) and runs `check_rule`: when the world now turns off scripts this game runs (`to_turn_off` less the parked ones), the notification box says "\<world> now turns off other mods. Enter it again to play.", and after `KICK_FRAMES` (5 s), once the map is free (`map_free?`), the game fades out to the title, which says it again; entering again parks them. While the catalog is unknown the game stays and checks again against a list it fetches (`recheck`, looked at every `RECHECK_STEP` frames, given up after `RECHECK_FRAMES`). `entered` does the same check against a fresh list, since the list the world was entered from may be older than a frame the room sent before the game joined it.

### The parked mods (DLL)

`ModParking` (`Mods/ModParking.cs`) lists the scripts, moves the files and keeps a journal of them; the script decides which to park and when they come back. The folder is `<game>/Parked mods`, made by the first park and removed by a restore that brought every script back and left no conflicts, so an install that never parks never has it:

- **`Parked.txt`**, the journal, written to `Parked.txt.tmp`, flushed and moved over the old one (`File.Move` with overwrite), before the first move and after each one, so a crash leaves the old journal or the new one, never half of one; its writes and its deletion are tried again for a moment while another program reads it. Lines, separated by tabs: `world`, the world's id; `after`, `none` or `title` (the watchdog starts the game to the title once the mods are back); `update 1` while the updater was asked for; `watch`, 16 hexadecimal characters naming the watchdog the journal belongs to; then `file`, the script's path relative to the game folder with forward slashes (`Patch/Invasions.rb`), its `ModHash`, and 1 once moved, else 0.
- **The lock**, not in this folder but `Patch/Multiplayer/Parking.lock`, which `mp_start` opens exclusively (`FileShare.None`, not inherited, `DeleteOnClose`) and holds for the game's lifetime, outside the options dump. Another game of the same folder cannot take it, so it never moves scripts under the first: park answers 0, restore and recover `HeldElsewhere` (-2) while scripts are parked, and each logs why. The operating system frees and deletes the lock on any exit, a crash included. Every call tries to take the lock again before it refuses. The World Admin tool's options sandbox leaves it out of its copy of `Patch`, and `Parked mods` too.
- **`Conflicts/<yyyy-MM-dd HH-mm-ss>/`**: nothing is ever overwritten. A restore checks each parked file against its journal hash first: one that differs, changed while parked, comes here instead of going back into `Patch`. A restore whose script's place in `Patch` is taken again drops the parked copy when both hash the same, and otherwise moves the parked one here, keeping its relative path. A park that finds a leftover file in a script's parking place moves that one here first. Every move clears a read-only attribute first, so a read-only copy never stays in the journal for good; the watchdog does the same, and reaches paths over 260 characters through `\\?\` paths.

| Export | Arguments | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `mp_mods_park` | `'pp'` the scripts, relative paths in UTF-8 separated by line breaks, as `mp_mods_scripts` lists them; the world's id | How many scripts are parked now. Each must be a `.rb` file inside `Patch`, not below `Patch/Multiplayer/` and other than the three every player has, else it is skipped and logged. Parked scripts the new list leaves out come back first; if one cannot, nothing moves and the answer is 0. Each move is `File.Move` without overwrite; once one fails, this call's moves are put back, newest first, and the answer is 0. A script listed and parked already stays; one put back into `Patch` meanwhile is parked anew. The same list for another world moves nothing and only writes the new world into the journal, its `watch` kept. 0 too in the options dump (`MGQMP_OPTIONS_DUMP`) and without the lock. |
| `mp_mods_restore` | `'l'` 1 when the game goes to the title afterwards, which only the log tells | How many scripts were handled, conflicts included; 0 with none parked, whoever holds the lock; -1 when one could not be moved (the journal keeps it, parked); -2 while another game holds the lock and scripts are parked. Deletes the journal once every script is handled, then the empty folders, `Parked mods` itself when no conflicts are left in it. |
| `mp_mods_parked` | `'pl'` (READ_ARGUMENTS) buffer, size | A message: `world`, `files` (how many are parked), `conflicts` (of the last restore), a blank line, then one parked path per line. Once nothing is parked but the last restore had conflicts, `files=0` and `conflicts=N` alone; empty when neither. Only the header `error=<message>` (`ModParking.DescribeError`) when the journal could not be read after a few tries. |
| `mp_mods_scripts` | `'pl'` (READ_ARGUMENTS) buffer, size | The scripts a world may park, in UTF-8, since the game's Ruby cannot list file names outside the system's code page: one line per script, first `elsewhere` + tab + the path for every script behind a junction or link that leads to another volume (`ModParking.ScriptElsewhere`, never parkable, and `mp_mods_park` skips them too), then `patch` + tab + the path relative to the game folder with forward slashes for every other `.rb` below `Patch` (subfolders included, each kind sorted), then `parked` + tab + the original path for every script the journal holds parked. Leaves out everything below `Patch/Multiplayer/`, the scripts whose key (`KeyOf`, as `MGQ_MpWorld.mod_key`) is `patch`, `multiplayer` or `0modconfigremake`, and names and folders that start with a dot, as the mod loader does. It follows junctions and links to folders, since the game's Ruby takes them for folders and loads the scripts behind them; one that leads to a folder listed already is listed once, one to a missing folder holds none. Empty when there is none; only the line `error` (`ModParking.ScriptsFailed`) when a folder or the journal could not be read, so a failed or partial listing never reads as no script. |
| `mp_mods_restart_parked` | `'pl'` the world's id; 1 to start the game again into the world (Player.ini's `rejoin` is stored already), 0 to start it to the title once the mods are back | As `mp_restart_game`: 1 when the watchdog waits for the game to close, 0 when it failed (no `Game.exe` or watchdog, the journal not written), 2 under Wine, where it starts nothing and the journal stays. Writes a new `watch` id and `after` into the journal first. |
| `mp_mods_recover` | `'p'` the world the game rejoins as it starts, empty for none | 0 with no journal, in the options dump, or for a journal of a park that moved nothing (deleted); 2 when the journal is that world's (the scripts stay parked); 1 when they came back for another; -1 when that failed; -2 while another game holds the lock. |

**The watchdog.** `package/Multiplayer/ModParking.ps1`, shipped like `Update.ps1` into `Patch/Multiplayer/`, is the safety net for every way the game ends. `mp_mods_restart_parked` starts it through the shell, so it inherits none of the game's handles, the lock among them: `powershell.exe -NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -WindowStyle Hidden -File <ps1> -GameProcess <pid> -GameFolder <game> -Mode <rejoin|title> -Watch <id>`. It runs on Windows PowerShell 5.1 and passes every path through .NET, like `Update.ps1`. It takes the game by its process id once and holds the handle (`Get-StartingGame`, as `Update.ps1` does), and waits for it to close. In `rejoin` mode it then starts `Game.exe` in the game folder, keeps that process and waits for it too, unless an update was asked for (the journal's `update` line, or `Update.ps1` running, found by a CIM process query), a game of the folder runs already, or the journal names another watchdog. Then, once no `Game.exe` of the folder runs and it can take the lock (`Patch/Multiplayer/Parking.lock`, the game's), it reads the journal again and, while the journal still names its own `watch` id, puts every script back with the DLL's rules (the same hash, the conflicts folder, no overwrite), deletes the journal and removes `Parked mods` when it is left empty; in `title` mode it then starts the game again if the journal said `after title` and no update was asked for. The `watch` id keeps a watchdog of an earlier restart from putting back what a later park just moved, in the moment between one game closing and the next starting. Its log is `Logs/Multiplayer Parking.log`, moved to `Multiplayer Parking.old.log` past 256 KB. Leaving a world puts the scripts back in the game itself and restarts through `mp_restart_game`, so the watchdog mostly covers crashes and closed windows; under Wine it never runs, and the next start's `mp_mods_recover` puts them back.

**Tests.** `ModParkingTests` covers the scripts listed (non-ASCII names included), park and restore, conflicts, the scripts never parked, parking again for another world and the same scripts for another world, the rollback, recover, journals a crash left, a script put back while parked, the lock and the folder made only while parked, the options dump and the journal's lines; `ModParkingWatchdogTests` runs the real `ModParking.ps1` in Windows PowerShell 5.1 against a game that closed already, and one that holds the lock for a moment. `GameScript/Tests/mod_parking_test.rb` covers the script's side. Neither covers a real crash or Wine.

## On the map

`overworld_sync.rbx` carries the world's messages; `overworld.rbx` shows what the player sees of them. While a world is open, the sync's `Graphics.update` hook runs every frame in every scene: it reads the world room's inbox (`mp_world_receive`, at most 64 entries a frame) and tells the others what changed about the player, so nothing piles up during a battle or in a menu.

- **What each game tells** (`Me`): one message of `key=value` lines with the player's id and name, the party leader's sprite and index (the vehicle's while in one), map, tile, facing, speed (`real_move_speed`, dashing included), whether the player is hidden, and what the player does (`Me.scene`): `battle`; `event` (a message or a running event on the map, or a novel scene, which requests and defeat scenes are); `away` while the game's window is not in front, unless in a battle or an event; a menu screen by its class name (`SCREENS`: `items`, `equip`, `shop`, `casino`, `library`, else `menu`); `sailing` or `flying` in a vehicle; or `map`; and the player's ping (`Ping.told`). It goes to everyone whenever any of it changed, and only then, so a player standing still sends nothing: every incoming message counts against the relay's free plan.
- **Newcomers** (`Inbox`): after every (re)connect (`kind=seat`) the game tells everyone everything, and keeps each of the others for 15 s (`REJOIN_FRAMES`, `Peers.wait_for_all`), in which they tell back; a game that went (`kind=out`) is kept as long (`Peers.wait_for`, its ghost hidden) and is the same player when its id tells again, on any seat, even while the relay still holds its old seat (`Peers.moved`, which forgets the old seat without a notice), so a party and a battle outlast the relay's limit per connection, and only who stays away has left (`Peers.tick`, `Peers.remove`; `Peers.present` lists those whose connection stands, `Peers.named` and `Peers.with_id` find one, `Peers.on_this_map?` tells one on the player's map); when another game comes in (`kind=in`), it tells that seat everything, and that game does the same, so both know each other at once. A message marked by a field a script registered (`route`: `chat`, `npcs`, `story`, `chest`, `pevent`, `coop` or `battle`, and through the map's gate of a Raid World `map_npcs`, `map_pevent`, `map_pscene` or `map_coop`) goes to that script, even when it names a map, as the leader's place does; any other message with a `map` is a state, which makes or updates the sender's `Peer` by seat; `kind=out` forgets it. The relay's push of a Raid World's story (`kind=story`) goes to the route `story_rev` (`STORY_FIELD`) without a sender, marked `:relay` as an admin's chat line is. Joining and leaving show as notices.
- **Ghosts** (`Game_MpGhost`, a `Game_Character` with `@through`): one per player on the current map, made where they stand. Every frame on the map (`Game_Map#update`) it takes their sprite, speed and visibility, and walks one tile toward where they stand, along the longer axis first, at their speed; more than `CATCH_UP_TILES` (3) away, or after a map change, it moves there at once. It triggers nothing, since only the player and events check triggers.
- **Sprites** (`Spriteset_Map#update`): a `Sprite_Character` and a `Sprite_MpGhostLabel` per ghost, made and freed as ghosts come and go. The label shows the name, and before it an icon of the game's icon set for what the player does (`STATE_ICONS`): battle 451 (crossed swords), event 4 (speech bubble), typing 4 (the same; `MGQ_MpActions.scene` says so while the chat box is open), menu 183 (open book), items 3059 (potion), equip 3905 (hammer), shop 3874 (coin), casino 220 (cards), library 3240 (red book), sailing 4069 (anchor), flying 3836 (wing), away 6 (Zzz); none while walking. `Sprite_MpWorldStatus` at the bottom left shows the last notices for 4 s each and, from `mp_world_status` every 30 frames, "Reconnecting . . .", "Connecting . . ." or why the connection failed.
- **Pings** (`Ping`): every 30 frames the status's `ping` becomes the player's own, shown right above their head (`Sprite_MpOwnPing`), and after the name on each ghost's label the ping its player told. The others are only told a ping that moved by 20 ms (`PING_STEP`) and a quarter (`PING_SHARE`) of the one they know, so jitter sends nothing. Colors: up to 100 ms green, up to 200 ms yellow, then red (`PING_COLORS`). `ui_actions.rbx` keeps `HEAD_ROOM` free above the head for it. Labels of characters on one tile stack upwards (`Sprite_MpGhostLabel#show` takes the room the labels before it took and returns its own), above the player's own ping on the player's tile, so two players standing together no longer draw their names over each other.
- **Registry**: the scripts that load after the sync join it at load time, each in a `begin` block before its hooks: `route(field)` for their messages, `state_fields` to add to the state message, `busy_scene` for a state of their own (the chat's `typing`), `on_tick` for every frame, `on_observe` and `on_leave` to hear of every state and every player leaving, and `label_line` for the line above a ghost's name. A failing block is logged once and left out; the others still run. Every state carries the marker field `state=1` (`STATE_FIELD`), which no route may be named like: a message with it is a state, any other goes to its route, so a state field may share a route's name. No message carries two marker fields, so the order of the routes decides nothing.

## The world never pauses (`core_async.rbx`)

In single player the map stops whenever another screen is in front: a menu, a shop, a battle or a story (novel) scene. In a world it goes on, so what others see of the player and what the player comes back to never waits for them.

- **Behind which screens** (`behind?`): every screen but the map itself, the title and the game over, while a world is open and a map waits on `SceneManager`'s stack. The map clears that stack whenever it starts, so a map on it is the one the player left and returns to; screens reached from the title never qualify.
- **What runs** (`tick`, from a `Scene_Base#update_basic` hook, which every screen's frame calls, battles and novel scenes included): `$game_map.update(false)`, so events move, background (parallel) events and common events run, ghosts walk (`overworld.rbx` hooks the same method), and the map's screen effects go on; and `$game_timer`, except in a battle, which runs it itself. The player does not move, and the map's main event, which drives whatever the player is in, never runs here.
- **What waits** (`hold?`, a `Game_Interpreter#execute_command` hook): while the tick runs, a command in `HELD_CODES` yields its interpreter's fiber until the map runs it again: messages, choices and inputs (101-105), party changes (129), transfers and vehicles (201, 206), music, sounds and movies (241-246, 249, 261), everything in 301-399 (battles, shops, name input, menu, save, game over, title, and actors' changes that would reach into a running battle), and scripts (355), which may do any of these. An event that starts by touching the player, or autoruns, only marks itself as starting; the main event picks it up once the player is back on the map. Events outside the tick, such as a battle's own, never wait.
- **Field music**: the game's map refresh plays the field's music when a switch changed it (`need_refresh_autoplay_field`), which would cut into a battle's, so the tick leaves that refresh to the map.
- **The live map** (`Spriteset_MpLiveMap`): a menu whose background is the picture the map took on leaving (`SceneManager.background_bitmap`) shows a `Spriteset_Map` instead, updated every frame, its viewports below everything the menu draws (z -300 to -200) and dimmed like the picture. Menus that make their background themselves, such as the casino and the warp screen, keep theirs.
- **No freeze between the map and menus** (`instant_switch?`, `leave`): leaving a screen freezes its picture (`Graphics.freeze` in `Scene_Base#terminate`), and the next screen fades in from it (`Graphics.transition` in `post_start`, 10 frames, the map 15). Nothing updates meanwhile, and even a transition of no frames takes about 117 ms (measured in game), so the world stood still for a moment at every menu. While a world is open, switching between the map and menus skips both: the screen keeps its last picture until the next screen draws its first. Battles, story scenes and the title keep their fades.

## Parties (`coop.rbx`)

**The party** (`Party`). Every state message also carries the player's party id (`party`) and whether they invite (`invite`), so a party needs no message of its own. Inviting lasts 15 s (`INVITE_FRAMES`, from the wheel in `ui_actions.rbx`), or a minute once it names a player from afar (`NAMED_INVITE_FRAMES`, from the World overview), who sees it in their notification box (`ui_notices.rbx`), and makes a party of one with a new id, which the invite line above the player's own head (`Sprite_MpOwnLine`) and above their ghost on the others' screens shows. Accepting takes the inviter's id. Anyone can read that id in a member's state, so naming it is not enough: the leader admits a player who names it only while the invite reaches them (`Party.admit`, `invite_reached?`, which still holds for 3 s, `LATE_FRAMES`, after a join ended the invite), tells whom it admitted in its state (`party_members`), and every member takes that list from the leader (`Party.adopt`). A member is who names the party and either made it or is on the list (`Party.member?`); a player who only names it is sent `kick=late`. A game sees another join its party when they become a member (`Party.update`), which also ends its invite. An invite nobody took forgets its party of one, and leaving the world leaves the party. Ghosts outside the party are see-through in a Classic world (`STRANGER_OPACITY`) and solid in a Raid World, where everyone on the map shares it (`ghost_opacity`, `SOLID_OPACITY`); party members' names are green in both. `Party.members` and `Party.leader` are what the party scripts below build on.

**The party's messages** (`route`, `take`, `tell`). The party scripts (`coop_events.rbx`, `coop_npcs.rbx`, `coop_story.rbx` and `battles_coop.rbx`) register their fields with `MGQ_MpCoop.route`, which registers the same field with `overworld_sync.rbx`. A message is handed on only from a member of the party (`Party.member?`) and with the party's own id; the scripts keep only checks of their own, such as who the Map Owner or the leader is. `MGQ_MpCoop.tell(seat, field, value, fields)` adds the party's id to what they send.

A party holds at most four players (`Party::MAX_PLAYERS`), one per place of the Frontline; the wheel refuses to invite into a full party or to accept the invite of one (`Party.full?`), and so does `Party.join`. Two players may still accept before either sees the other join, so the leader sends `kick=full` to whoever joins one too many (`Party.admit`, `turn_away`). The invite's frames and targets live in `MGQ_MpCoop::Invite`, which duels share; `Invite.reaches_me?` is the near-or-named check of both. Only the leader invites more players (`Party.may_invite?`), and only the leader removes a member (`Party.remove`, from the World overview): it sends `kick=1` through the party's gate, and the member's game leaves the party when the message comes from its leader (`Party.removed_by`).

## Party or map (`coop_scope.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpCoop::Scope` tells `coop_npcs.rbx`, `coop_castle.rbx`, `coop_events.rbx`, `coop_gather.rbx` and `coop_scene.rbx` whom the player shares the map and the story with. Each question goes to `Scope::Classic` or `Scope::Raid`, by `MGQ_MpWorld.raid?` (`Scope.raid?`, false while `world.rbx` cannot tell, outside worlds and in Classic worlds), so Classic worlds behave as before.

| | Classic | Raid |
|---|---|---|
| `peers_here` | party members on the player's map | everyone on the player's map |
| `sync_host` (Map Owner of `coop_npcs.rbx`) | lowest `[since, id]` of the player and `peers_here`; the castle's maps: the party's leader (`MGQ_MpCoopCastle.owner`) | lowest `[since, id]` of the player and `peers_here`, the castle's maps too |
| `teller` | the party's leader | the player while telling; else of the players on the map (or the map the story takes the player to) whose state says `telling=1`, those whose connection is down included (`Raid.tellers`, from `Peers.all`, as the Classic leader is), the one whose story takes the player along (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.followed_id`) or whose pages the player follows (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.heard_from`); else the one whose story the player follows to where it moved them, on that map, whether or not their state says they tell (`Raid.followed_on_the_way`, from `MGQ_MpCoopGather.follow_taken`); else the lowest `[since, id]` |
| `viewers` | the synced members (`MGQ_MpCoopStory.synced_members`) | the players on the map, and those the story took along who have not arrived yet (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.taken_along`), whose `sm` equals the player's story progress as their telling started |
| `story_from?(peer)` | `peer` is the leader | `peer` is the teller, or nobody's state says `telling=1` yet (the teller's state and pages travel apart) |
| `watches?(peer)` | `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.following?` | the teller's `tsm` (or `sm` before it came) equals the player's own progress |
| `follows_teller?` / `leads_story?` | `follows_leader?` / `leading_synced?` of `coop_story.rbx` | another player is the teller / nobody else is |
| `sharing?` | `MGQ_MpCoop.in_party?` | a world is open |
| message gate | `route` (same party) | `route_map` (same map) |

- **The map's gate** (`MGQ_MpCoop.route_map`, `take_map`, `map_drop_reason`): a script registers its field, and the gate listens on `map_<field>`, so the party's gate and the map's never take each other's messages. A message is handed on (under its plain field) only from a known player who is on the player's map by their state, or whose message names the player's map, or the map the story takes the player to (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.story_map?`), in `mmap`; a message whose `mto` names other players' ids alone is dropped. `npcs`, `pevent` and `pscene` are registered with both gates; the party chat, `kick`, invites, `chest`, `story`, co-op battles and duels keep the party's gate alone.
- **Sends** (`Scope.tell`, `Scope.tell_each`, `MGQ_MpCoop.tell_map`, `MGQ_MpCoop.tell_map_to`): in a Raid World a message to everyone (`-1`) goes out once to the whole world while anyone else is on the player's map, and the map's gate of every game sorts it; a message to several players (the story's `say`, `done`, `pscene` and `follow`) goes out once too, naming their ids in `mto`, to one player to their seat. The relay ends a connection that sends more than 60 messages a second, which one send per seat ran past on a busy map. It carries `map_<field>=<value>`, `mmap=<map id>` (the player's map unless the sender names another, as the story's `follow` does with the map it left) and no party id. Classic sends stay `MGQ_MpCoop.tell`.
- **The story in a Raid World** (`coop_events.rbx`): a story event starts only while no other player on the map tells the story (`hand_over`, notice "\<name> is telling the story here.") and never while the player follows a teller's story to where it moved them (`held_following`, see *Gathering in a Raid World* below); a talk or travel turning into story ends the same way (`guard`). A player telling the story says `telling=1` in their state, alone on the map too, and `tsm`, their progress (`sm` values) as the telling started (`told_markers`), which decides who sees it: their pages (`say`, `done`) and scene changes (`pscene`) go to `viewers` alone, and only a player whose own progress matches takes them (`watches?`) and stands still while the scene plays on their map (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.blocked?`), but not while the teller fights the story's battle (their state's `scene` is `battle`): a player who came after its invites would otherwise neither move nor join it by choice until it ended. A player whose story differs sees the NPCs move (the Map Owner's events on other pages stay their own) but not the scene. Two players who start telling at the same moment both say `telling=1` for a moment: the one with the lower `[since, id]` goes on (`Scope::Raid.first_teller_here`), and the other's event ends at its next command (`yield_story`, before every command of the map's interpreter). So that it ends before it changed anything, a story event whose map holds someone who entered it first waits at its first command for one second (`hold_for_tie`, `tie_holding?`, `TIE_HOLD_FRAMES` = 60), which covers a usual relay delay of the earlier player's state with room to spare. This tie holds only within 3 s of the telling's start (`TIE_FRAMES`), on the map it started on (`note_telling_start`) and never once the telling moved its teller (`@telling_moved`), since `since` starts anew with every transfer: a story that moves its teller onto a map where another player tells one goes on. A telling goes on at the story's next event in two cases, with its `tsm`, start and `taken_along`, since the world's story of its changes goes out only once the telling ends and the viewers' `sm` stays where it started: an event that starts in the frame its last one ended (`note_told_frame`, after the map's update), and, when the telling moved its teller onto another map (`moving`, before `Game_Player#perform_transfer`), an event there that runs by itself (an autorun map event or a common event a switch starts, `by_itself?`) within `CONTINUE_FRAMES` (5 s) of the move (`moved_by_story?`, `continue_telling`), since the game's transfer process, the player's own, runs between them and the next event may wait for the screen to fade in; a story event the player starts there is a new telling. `world_story.rbx` writes nothing while the telling may still go on (`telling_or_moved?`), so nobody takes the scene's state half done. Any other telling is new. While telling, `state_fields` also says `twb`, whether warping is banned where the player stands. Chests stay personal (`shares_chests?`): a Raid World's parties share no loot, and a `chest` message is ignored there. The pages a player follows are forgotten once another player tells (`forget_former_leader`, by `page_source_id`), and a page ends once its teller left or stopped telling 2 s ago (`page_teller`, `page_done?`).
- **Gathering in a Raid World** (`coop_gather.rbx`): nobody is gathered (`gathered?` is always true): whoever is on the map watches. A transfer of the teller's story calls everyone who was on the map, and those an earlier transfer of the same telling took who have not arrived yet (`after_transfer`: `pevent=follow` with `mmap` the map it left; `take_raid` takes only `follow`). A receiver follows at once only while it watches that story (`Scope.watches?(teller)`) and is not behind the world's story (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.behind?`); every other receiver gets an offer in the notification box (`MGQ_MpCoopGather::Offers`, key `[:story_follow, seat]`): "\<name>'s story moved on to \<place or map id>. Follow?", which Y takes through `followed` and N dismisses. The offer drops once the teller's telling ends, after `FOLLOW_OFFER_FRAMES` (60 s), once the player is on that map, or when the world closes. A follow, taken at once or accepted, waits `ASKED_LAPSE_FRAMES` (60 s) for a busy player, since the story calls once and the teller's pages, a battle or a menu keep a player busy longer than `CALL_LAPSE_FRAMES`. While the player is on the way, and for `CALL_LAPSE_FRAMES` after they arrived (`@taken_follow`), `follow_taken` names the follow: `followed_id` and `story_map?` read it, so the teller's pages from the new map reach the player, `Scope::Raid.teller` keeps that teller while their state says they tell nothing for a moment (between the story's events, while the game's transfer process runs, `followed_on_the_way`), and `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.hand_over` keeps the player's own story events from starting meanwhile (`held_following`), so their own copy of the story starts neither on the map they leave nor just after they arrived. Until they show up on the teller's map, or the telling ends, they stay among the `viewers` (`taken_along`, forgotten when a new telling starts, not by the story's next event), so the scene changes and pages told meanwhile reach them. *Teleport to the story* on the player's own row of the World overview (`MGQ_MpCoop::Offers.story_option`) goes to `MGQ_MpCoopGather.story_endpoint` once the player is free (`join_story`); it is closed to a player behind the world's story (`MGQ_MpWorldCatchup.holds_story?`), who could not play it there: the endpoint the relay keeps of the world's story (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.endpoint`, see [In the game](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)), whose warp ban this game does not know, so the player keeps their own; while the relay keeps none, where this game last saw the story told (`note_story_place`, forgotten when the world closes), with the warp ban there (`story_warp_ban`): the player's own `warp_ban?` for their own telling, the teller's `twb` for another's; without `twb` the player keeps their own.
- **The party's story** (`coop_story.rbx`) is off in a Raid World: `MGQ_MpCoopStory.leader` is nil, `follows_leader?` false and `synced_members` empty, so no member borrows a leader's story and no leader tells one; a member who borrowed one gets their own back. The world's story takes its place (`world_story.rbx`, see [In the game](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)), built on the same state model (`story_state.rbx`, `MGQ_MpStoryState`): `mix` (which takes the chests to keep), `story_lists`, `pack`, `unpack`, `decode_full`, `raw_state` and `set_raw`.

## Squads (`coop_squad.rbx`)

A party shares one single-player team: each player keeps a **squad** of their first characters, and the rest are cut. `share(position, count, max)` splits the Frontline's 4 places and the Backline's `max - 4` (`max` is the player's own `party_member_max`, 8 plus variable 56) between the players, one more each for the first ones while some are left over; the players are ordered the same way on every game (`ranked`): the leader first, then by player id. Outside a party, or alone in one, nothing is cut. Nothing here changes the game's party: a battle the player fights alone brings their whole team, and `party_member_max` and `max_battle_members` stay the game's, except in a Raid World (below). Variable 56 is the player's own in `story_state.rbx`, since the game cuts its party list down to `party_member_max` on every change, so a leader's smaller value would drop a member's characters.

- **Followers** (`Game_Follower#visible?`, `follower_shown?`): in a party only the player's share of the Frontline (a follower's `@member_index` below the share; the trail sent to the others lists only those shown), or nobody in a party whose leader chose *Leaders Only* (`mode`, the option *Party Followers* in `$game_system.conf[:mp_party_followers]`, registered in the Mod Config Remake's `MOD_CONTENTS` or the game's `CONTENTS`; members read the leader's from their state's `follow` and may not change it). `tick` keeps the share and the mode each frame, which `follower_shown?` reads, and refreshes `$game_player` once either changed.
- **Ghosts' followers**: every state carries `trail`, the looks of the followers the player shows (`name*index|...`); `Game_MpGhost` in `overworld.rbx` keeps a `Game_MpGhostFollower` per look while the player is a party member, each stepping onto where the one before it stood, before the ghost steps, as the game's followers do, and gathered when the ghost jumps.
- **The formation screens** (`place_of`, `place_at`): both hooks wrap the game's own `draw_item`, so the game's and the translation's layouts stay, and only change how a row draws by its place in the squad. The menu's formation list (`Window_MenuStatus`) draws the squad's Frontline faces opaque and its Backline's translucent, as the game draws the Backline (`draw_actor_face`), and the cut characters monochrome: faces and icons through `monochrome` (a grey copy per face and icon, made once per session, since `get_pixel`/`set_pixel` over every pixel is slow) and texts and gauges through `text_color` and `grey`. The party edit screen (`Foo::PTEdit::Window_PartyMember`) colors rows through `change_color`: Frontline normal, Backline the system color, cut grey. Both draw a line above the first cut row.
- **In a Raid World** (`raid?`, from `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.raid?`) every player has a squad, in a party or not (`squads?`): `own_share` is `RAID_SHARE`, one on the Frontline and two on the Backline, and the 4th place is cut, so the formation screens draw the line after the 3rd place and the 4th monochrome. Co-op battles take the same through `battle_share` (`own_build` sends the first 3, `arrange` gives every player 1 and 2); `share` stays as it is for Classic worlds and team duels, though a team duel takes only the squad (`team_build`, `raid_team`). Nobody follows on the map (`follower_shown?` is false for every follower, so the `trail` is empty).
  - **The team of four** (`fit_team`, `party_member_max`, `RAID_PARTY_MAX`): the game cuts its party list (a `Game_Party::BattleMember`) down to `party_member_max` on every change; a cut companion stays in `include_actors` and so waits on standby (the Pocket Castle). `party_member_max` is capped at 4 only for the `$game_party` that `tick` fitted first: on entering a Raid World, and for every new game or loaded save there (a new `Game_Party`), the companions past the 4th go to standby (`add_stand_actor`, then `move_stand_actor`, Luka always kept, `past_cap`, and swapped into the 3rd place when he stood past it, `luka_into_squad`) while the cap does not hold yet, so nothing depends on the order of the game's own cut. A team in a battle waits. A story's temporary party (`temp_actors_use?`) is its own standby list, so the cap never holds for it (a cut character would leave the story) and is lifted while it plays (`lift_cap`); the team is fitted again once it ends. Outside a Raid World the cap is lifted. A companion joining the full team, a recruit or `$game_party.add_actor`, lands on standby by the game's own cut (a recruit after the game's "The party is full" choice); items raising variable 56 change nothing below the cap.
  - **Battles of the player's own and duels** (`raid_battle?`: in battle, no co-op party formed, no team duel): `max_battle_members` is 1 and `bench_members` the next 2, both kept to the team's first 3 places (`in_squad`, since the game skips hidden members such as an eaten one; `own_order` is the existing members among the same first `RAID_SQUAD` places, so `raid_team` never brings the cut 4th either), so the 4th never fights, not even as a reserve, and a skill reaching the Backline too (`item_target_members`) reaches only those 3. The game's Backline window and swap count from `max_battle_members`, which is why the Frontline is narrowed there. This covers a player left alone in a co-op battle (`go_solo`, `take_over`), who fights on with 1 and 2. A duel's team (`MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Team.build`) is the squad, `raid_team`, and its Frontline is 1 (`pvp_front`), so without the Backline only that one fights. Outside a battle `max_battle_members` stays 4.

## NPCs in a party (`coop_npcs.rbx`)

Players outside a party share no NPCs: each game moves its own, as in single player. Between party members on the same map, one game moves the map's events and the others follow it.

- **The Map Owner** (`owner`): of the party members on a map, the one who entered it first. Every state message carries `since`, when the player entered their map (`Me.map_since`, milliseconds by that computer's clock), so every member's game finds the same Map Owner without asking: the earliest `since`, the lower player id among equals. A member whose connection is down counts no longer (`party_here`), so they never hold the events still. It changes as members come and go. It has nothing to do with who created the world.
- **The Map Owner's game** (`lead`) moves events as the game does and sends, at most every 15 frames (`SEND_FRAMES`) and only when something changed, each changed event's x, y, facing and page index (`-1` for none, as when erased): `npcs=<map id>`, `party=<party id>`, `full=0`, `events=<id>:<x>,<y>,<d>,<page>;...` to everyone. A member who comes onto the map gets every event (`full=1`) to their seat. Other members block events walking on their own there (`Game_Event#collide_with_characters?`), never a forced route nor any event while the leader's story plays, since a story waits for the routes it moves its events on and a member in the way would hold it up for good, and events that approach the player go for the nearest member (`near_the_player?`, `move_toward_player`). Touching a member's ghost starts nothing, since only the player starts touch events.
- **The other members' games** (`follow`) take only the Map Owner's messages for their party and map, stop events moving on their own (`update_self_movement`), and walk each event toward its place, one step at a time within 3 tiles (`MGQ_MpOverworld::CATCH_UP_TILES`), at once farther away or after 60 frames stuck (`STUCK_FRAMES`); a full picture places every event at once. An event talking to the player, moved by the player's own events, or on another page than the Map Owner's, which means another story state, stays where it is. An event walking into the player there starts its touch event there.
- It runs from a `Game_Map#update` hook, so behind menus and battles too (see [The world never pauses](#the-world-never-pauses-core_asyncrbx)).

## The party's story (`coop_story.rbx`)

The party's story plays in Classic worlds alone, built on the story's state model, `story_state.rbx` (`MGQ_MpStoryState`), which a Raid World's story shares: the story read and written as raw data, what is each player's own, a story joined with the player's own values, written for a message and read back, and the story's skills, companions and items granted. Outside a party every player plays their own story in their own world save. In a party, the members synced with the leader play the leader's: the player who made the party (`Party.leader`: the member whose id starts the party's id, else the lowest id while they are gone).

- **Who follows the leader's story** (`sync_state`, `check_sync`, `follows_leader?`, `synced_members`): every player tells how far their own story is in their state (`sm`: variables 1001, 1141, 1142, 1143; a member who borrows the story of a leader they are ahead of tells their own). Before the Great Decision (1001 below 40, `GREAT_DECISION`) two players are synced while at most 2 apart in 1001 (`SYNC_STEPS`); past it, while both are at 40 on the same route (the first of 1141-1143 above 0; the Great Decision itself sets 1143 to 1) at most 5 apart on it (`ROUTE_SYNC_STEPS`). Anything else is not synced: too far apart, one on each side of the Great Decision (as a player reset to it against one on a route), or different routes. The member decides after every map update, again whenever either side's progress changes, and tells the leader whose story they follow in their state (`ssync`: the leader's id, empty for none); a member who keeps the leader's story stays synced, as their story moves with the leader's. A member not synced plays their own story completely: no borrowing, catch-up or gifts (the leader's story messages are ignored, nothing waits), their own story events run (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.following?`), and the leader's pages, scene changes (sent to `synced_members` alone) and gathering never reach them. Chests, the map, co-op battles, chat, trading, duels and the castle are shared all the same. Notices: "You and \<leader> are too far apart in the story; each of you plays your own.", "... are on different sides of the Great Decision; each of you plays your own story.", "... are on different routes; each of you plays your own story." for the member and the same with the member's name for the leader, who also reads "\<member> follows your story while in the party."; a member who starts following reads the borrowing notice "You follow \<leader>'s story while in the party."

- **What is borrowed** (`MGQ_MpStoryState`): the story as the game keeps it, `$game_switches`, `$game_variables` and `$game_self_switches`, read and written as raw data (`data_of`, `MGQ_MpGame.get` and `set` of `:data`). The game's own `[]=` would run its side effects: a switch in 1001-2000 adds or removes that companion, an awakening switch refreshes an actor, an affection variable writes through to `$game_global_system`.
- **What stays the player's own** (`MGQ_MpStoryState.personal_switch?`, `personal_variable?`): the side they chose (switches 4 Alice Chosen and 5 Ilias Chosen, `SIDE_SWITCHES`) until the Great Decision, which sets the side anew with the route, so after it they follow the leader's (`personal_switch?` takes the story's variables), configuration mirrored in switches (95, 445-447, 502), party membership (switches 1001-2000), awakening (switches 6000-6999, `AWAKENING_SWITCHES` to `AWAKENING_LAST`; the game's switches from 7000 tell which story bosses were defeated and belong to the story), where a game over returns them (variable 1002), monsters' friendliness (variables 2000-2999) and affection (variables from 3000, one per companion). Every join of a story with the player's own values (`mix`) keeps these.
- **Borrowing**: a member asks the leader (`story=ask`, every 2 s until answered, again whenever the leader changes), who answers with the whole story (`story=full`: `s` and `sf` the switches on and off, `v` the variables set, zero included, `ss` and `ssf` the self switches on and off; what the story never set stays out). The lists travel packed in one field `z` (`pack`: deflated and Base64), and a story still above `MAX_FULL_BYTES` (200 KB, the DLL's frame is 256 KB) leaves out what it turned off or set to zero and says so (`part=1`), so a member who catches up keeps none of their own values in its gaps. The member keeps their own story aside (`@own`) the first time and plays the leader's with their own values. The leader then sends what changed, at most every 15 frames (`story=delta`, found by comparing with the story last sent), to the whole party. Variables carry only numbers, strings and true or false (`encode_value`), so no message can make objects in the other game.
- **Getting it back** (`restore`): leaving the party, the leader leaving or changing, or becoming the leader gives the member their own story back, with their own values as they are by then.
- **Playing along** (`same_story?`, `behind?`, `@keeps`, `@recorded`, `own_story`): a member whose main story and route progress (`STORY_MARKERS`: variables 1001, 1141-1143) are no further than the leader's when they first borrow keeps what they play together. One as far along has every change of the leader's story they take recorded and laid over their own story when they get it back and in every save; one behind keeps the leader's story as they played it (`caught_up_story`), with their own value of every switch, variable and self switch the leader's story never set, such as a side quest only they played, and their own chests on every map (`MGQ_MpCoopStoryRewards::CHESTS`), so a chest only the leader looted stays shut for them. A member ahead gets their own story back unchanged. A companion who joins the leader's roster (`Game_Party#add_stand_actor`) while the leader's main event plays story on the map (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.telling?`, `telling_party?`) is told to the party (`story=recruit`, `actor`), one the leader has already too, and joins the roster of every member who keeps the story and lacks them, unless only the other side gets them; one recruited outside the story, as after a battle, stays the leader's. What changes in a shop, a synthesis or a menu the story opens is never told, since those scenes are not the map.
- **Catching up** (`holdings`, `catch_up`, `coop_story_rewards.rbx`): the leader tells what of the story's rewards they hold with their story (`story=full`) and whenever it changes (`story=hold`, rebuilt and compared every 15 frames): `sk` the story skills Luka knows, his abilities included (`knows?`), `ac` the story companions in the roster, `it` the story items and key items with their counts, `side` "a" or "i". On first following the story, a member who was behind gets what the leader holds and they lack: the skills and companions, and the items, as many as the leader holds and one list of the story's events gives at most, summed over the lists (key items as many as the leader holds; equipment worn at the castle counts, `story_count`). After that only the story's own messages bring more: companions (`story=recruit`), items (`story=gain`) and skills (`story=learn`, which the leader sends when Luka learns a story skill while `telling_party?`, hooked on `Game_Actor#learn_skill`), so what the leader gets outside the story stays the leader's. The past catch-up covers the companions and skills the leader got from optional side events too, by design: the member takes over the leader's flags, so those events count as done for them. What waited before the whole story came (`drop_covered`) is forgotten where the story covers it: older holdings always, and for a member who just caught up the story's gifts, companions and skills, which they would get twice. Companions are read from the player's own roster (`roster_ids`, the party's `@include_actors`), past a story's temporary party, by their main persona (`main_id`, as the game's roster keeps every persona), and the game's roster never holds a companion twice; the game's switches 1001-2000 already read whether a companion is there.
- **Sides** (`MGQ_MpCoopStoryRewards::SIDED`, `GROUPS`, `due_groups`): a skill or companion only the events where the sides differ give before the Great Decision (`sided?`) never comes as it is, unless the Final Chapter gives it too (`ROUTE`, such as the companions the Great Decision brings with the route), which comes as the leader holds it once the story is past the decision; one the story gives while the sides still differ waits until then (`@route_waiting`, `take_route_rewards`), since the Great Decision brings its companions before the route's progress is set. A reward some event gives without a side, such as Morrigan at the Strategy Conference, comes as it is. Each such event the leader's story got past (`played?`) gives the member their own side's rewards once (`@granted_groups`), such as Angel Dance in place of Demon Decapitation at the first camp, or Eden at the Snow Shrine for Ilias's side where Alice's side fights her. An event counts as got past when everything one of its pages leaves in the story holds (`marked?`: the story variables it sets, else the switches and self switches it turns on, neither scratch, the player's own nor a Library Flag), or when the leader holds a reward only that event gives, to either side (`only_from?`), as for the camps, which leave nothing; either side counts, since the Great Decision sets the leader's side anew. The member's side here is the one they chose before the Great Decision (`story_side`, `@own_side`), past it the leader's for one who never chose, so a member holds one side's rewards whenever they joined. A member as far along leaves out the events the story had got past when they joined (`@baseline_groups`). After the Great Decision the side switches follow the leader's route (`follow_route_side`, also when the route's progress arrives alone in a delta, which `apply` takes before the switches), and route companions come as the leader's story gives them. Run `ruby Tools\story_rewards.rb "<game folder>\Data"` in MGQ-Paradox-Event-Checker to write the table again; it leaves out the Final Chapter's events (the Great Decision, common events from 9000, lists that read a route's progress, every map from 1000 on) but for `ROUTE`, counts gold-only chests as chests, and companions who join by their switch (1001-2000) or a party set by `set_actors(...)` as joining. It reads the maps from 1000 on where the game keeps them (`map_folders`, as `DataManager.map_file_name` finds them: `Data/Map/Data` for 1000-1999, `Data/Map2/Data` for 2000-2999, each with its own `MapInfos.rvdata2`), such as the Chaos route's snow shrine where Puruel and Inuel join.
- **Choosing a side** (`ask_side`, `choose_side`): only the leader plays the Iliasville scene where the player takes Alice or Ilias along, so a member without a side gets the same choice in their own game once the leader chose and the member is free on the map outside the leader's story scene; the answer sets their own switch 4 or 5, brings in the companion that choice brings (`:choice` events: Alice or Ilias), and catches them up with their side's rewards.
- **The Pocket Castle's way out** (`PERSONAL_VARIABLES` 21-23, `MGQ_MpCoopCastle.arriving`): the castle's item notes where it was used in variables 21-23, and its way out ("Leaving the Pocket Castle") transfers there. They are each player's own, so the leader's never send a member elsewhere, and a member the party brings into the castle (its front gate, map 126, or a resident's map) from outside, by a story's call, its follow or a teleport to a member, has them set to where they stood, as the item would. Before, such a member left the castle for a place of an old save, such as Iliasville.
- **Lent key items** (`lend_keys`, `return_lent`, `without_lent`): a member ahead of the leader gets none of the items and gold the leader's story gives (`story=gain`), but borrows the leader's key items (`RPG::Item#key_item?`) they lack, such as the one that opens the basement of Lily's mansion, so they can play the story along. The leader tells the key items they hold (`key_items`, `k=<id>:<count>,...`) with their story (`story=full`) and whenever they change (`story=keys`, checked every 15 frames); the member's game lends the difference to what they hold themselves (`@lent`) and takes back what the leader no longer holds, in one notice ("<Leader> lent you <item> for the story.", `key_list`). Once they get their own story back (`restore`), the lent items go back too, and a save made meanwhile, the autosave and a duel's snapshot included, leaves them out of its party (a copy of `Game_Party`). Since no save holds them, a member who crashed, loaded a save or came back from a duel borrows them again with the leader's story. A member who keeps the story gets key items to keep instead (see Catching up).
- **Held back** (`held_back?`, `hold_back`, `take_held`): what the leader's story gives or takes (`gain`, `recruit`, `depart`, `hold`) waits while a PvP battle runs, whose end puts the game back as it was before it, and until the member plays the leader's story again, as after a load or a duel; it is taken once they do (after the map's update), only from the leader who sent it, since another leader's story differs, and forgotten once the party is over. Chests other members open during a PvP battle wait the same way (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.take_held_chests`). Gifts never count as what a chest the player opens at that moment gives (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.granting`).
- **Saving** (`DataManager.make_save_contents`, `own_with_values`): while a member plays the leader's story, every save, the autosave included, holds their own story with their own values as they are. Loading a save or starting a new game (`extract_save_contents`, `create_game_objects`) brings its own story, so the member asks the leader again.

## The story's choices (`coop_choices.rbx`)

A member makes the story's choices for themselves, whatever the leader chose. The choices (`BRANCHES`, by the main story's progress 1001 from which each can be made): Alice or Ilias (7, switches 4/5), Amira in Iliasburg (9: ask her name, switch 2127 and Amira 540 joins, or kill her, switch 2108, after which she comes back), Magistea Village (17: defeat Lily, switches 2096/7017/2095, Lucia 167 joins, or defeat Lucia, 2097/7016/2094, Lily 163 joins; 2137/2138 and 1029 = 6), Plansect (24: the Priestess's side, Queen Bee defeated 7003, 1061 = 1, the Priestess 241 joins, or the Queen Bee's side, 2154/7002, 1061 = 2, Miria 245 joins; 1056 = 6), Succubus Village (27: Natasha's side, 7007, Natasha 288 joins with Lily, 2210, else 2211; or the mayor's side, 7008, the mayor 287 joins with Lucia, 2212, else 2213; 1065 = 3), the Spider Princess (30: 2280 and she, 334, joins, or the Queen Ants, 2281), the Sphinx (31: challenge her, 2090, or invite her, 153 joins) and the Great Decision (40).

- **What is asked** (`passed`, `made?`): every choice whose progress a catch-up or the shared story carries the member past, unless their own story made it (Amira only once she joined, so she is asked of every member who never had her join). The third way of the Great Decision only when the member's own save cleared both endings (`ssw(:ed1) && ssw(:ed2)`, as common event 380 asks).
- **Kept the member's own** (`personal?`, `MGQ_MpStoryState.choice_key?`): the switches and variables of every choice the member's own story made are personal while they play the leader's story, like the side switches, so the leader's full story and deltas never overwrite them. The choices' companions (`CHOICE_COMPANIONS`) never come with the catch-up or a `story=recruit`, only with the member's own outcome (`skip_reason`).
- **The leader's offer** (`offer`, `Offers`): the leader's World overview menu of a member who is behind (no progress beyond the leader's) and does not follow the leader's story offers *Sync story*. The offer stands in the leader's state (`ssoffer`: member id and number) for a minute, until the member follows or leaves; the member answers it in the notification box. Declining tells the leader (`choices=declined`). Accepting opens the screen of choices; *Bring me there* lays the outcomes over the member's own story first (`apply`), then the member follows the leader by the offer (`offer_with?`, before the rule of `check_sync`), catches up as a member behind, and `borrowed` ends the offer.
- **The Great Decision**: the same route as the leader's catches up as usual. Another route (`start_route`): the member keeps the leader's story up to the decision (1001 = 39, 1140-1143 = 0, `MGQ_MpCoopStory.keep_and_leave`), catches up with nothing only the leader's route gives (`MGQ_MpCoopStoryRewards::FINAL`), and once free plays `decision_commands`: common event 154 (the game's own reset of the Final Chapter), the outcome's part of common event 380 (from 1002 = 111 or 112 to the transfer, with its party changes when 912 = 0), switches 4/5 and the route's variable = 1, and the transfer to map 430 or 431 (25, 19, facing up), whose event plays the route's start. The third way sets 7091, 7092, 92, 1001 = 40 and 1143 = 1 and goes to map 437.
- **Synced play** (`story_passed`): a delta that carries the shared story past a choice, or resolves one, which the member has not made asks their own outcome on the same screen with one row once they are free on the map.
- **The screen** (`Scene_MpStoryChoices`, `Window_MpStoryChoices`): a `Window_MpWorldForm` with a switch per choice (`MGQ_MpWorld::Form::Field` of kind `:switch`, `Form#turn`) and the line of what the picked outcome brings below it (Succubus Village follows the Magistea answer), then *Bring me there* and *Cancel* (a prompt: *Confirm*). Left/Right or Enter turn a switch, a click turns it or presses a button; the lines at the top name the choice and the keys. Every turn and the final choices are logged.
- **In a Raid World** (`raid?`) every choice's outcome is each player's own (`personal?`, `raid_keys`), and nobody offers a story sync (`Offers.peer_option` is nil). A choice the world's story carries the player past (`story_passed`, called by `world_story.rbx`) asks their own outcome, the side included; the screen stays while no leader is followed, and what waits (choices to pick, a Raid World's route to confirm, the queued Great Decision, the accepted offer) is forgotten when a save loads, a new game starts or the world closes (`forget`). The Great Decision's labels are `decision_routes` (the world's route after `raid_decision`, else `MGQ_MpWorldStory.decision_routes`), the third way by the world's clears (`both_endings?` reads 7096 and 7097), and its note says the whole world plays the route (`raid_decision_note`). Confirmed on the screen titled "The world's route", only the player's own half plays (`take_raid_decision`, `raid_decision_commands`, see [In the game](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)).

## Events in a party (`coop_events.rbx`)

**What an event page is** (`kind`; `kind_of` for a list of commands): read from its commands when it starts, following called common events up to 5 deep. Only the commands that can run now count (`runnable`): a conditional branch on a switch or a variable (111) counts only when it holds now (`holds?`), its else only when it does not, and a branch on anything else counts whole. A branch on the variable a preceding `unlimited_choices` script writes the player's answer into (`CHOICE_SCRIPT`, `choice_variables`) counts whole, since the choice is not made yet, and in `marks_of` its sides count as a choice's branches (`choice_branch?`), so story behind it is `choice_story` and caught at the choice like any other: Alice's recap at the Strategy Conference and Iliasville's village chief keep their sets there. A scene that runs by itself (trigger 3) and hides its story behind such a choice is `:story` (`kind`), since a member's copy ended at the story would start over and over. Called common events count the same way (`common_marks`, kept once per common event and depth for the frame, since the game's state decides what runs), except those of `OWN_COMMON_EVENTS` (2, 3, 5 and 114): called through command 117 they add nothing to the caller's marks (`common_marks` returns `{}`), so an event that calls them is sorted by the rest of its list, so a shop calling a side quest's common event, as Vanilla's supply quest in Magistea and the other towns (common events 274-288), is a talk while that quest's step cannot run. What a choice's branch (402, 403) would make story counts as `:choice_story` instead: the page stays a talk that may turn into story (`may_tell?`), such as a blacksmith whose "Talk" moves a side quest on; a choice that gives items and sets the event's own switch stays a chest. While such a talk runs on the map, each command is watched before it runs (`guard`, a `Game_Interpreter#execute_command` hook): at the first that moves the story on (`story_command?`), a member's game ends the event there (on the list's closing empty command) with "Only <leader> can move the story on.", and the leader's game tells the party the story from there (`@telling`), without gathering. Exits hold the story's warnings in such branches (Iliasville's: "I need to be on time for my baptism", the village chief, the crying slime), which would make them story long after the warnings passed. Since the answer depends on the game's state, a page is sorted anew each time it starts.

- **The Pocket Castle** first (`MGQ_MpCoopCastle.resident?` in coop_castle.rbx, maps 227-230 and 268-278, not the side stories' copies of it): a companion's page (`companion_talk?`: its first speaker's name shows their affection variable, `\V[3000+n]`) and a page that calls one of the castle's services (`SERVICES`: Vanilla's shop, Papi's smithy, the maids' party saves, the item storage, Teeny's inn) or the coin shop (`@goods`) is a talk, whatever it sets: each player talks to them in their own game. The companions' scripts (`actor_label_jump`, presents, novel scenes) appear in the castle's story events too, so only the speaker tells them apart. This makes 728 of the castle's 745 story pages talks; the 17 left are its story events, such as the Sphinx's orb, the Elf Princess's room and Papi joining.
- **The leader's residents** (coop_castle.rbx): on the castle's maps the party's leader is the Map Owner of `coop_npcs.rbx` while there (`MGQ_MpCoopCastle.owner`), so the castle shows the leader's residents. A resident the member's own game shows nothing of (its event shows no page there, or one without a look) while the leader's page shows it, such as a companion only the leader recruited, stands as a see-through ghost (`Game_MpResident`, as see-through as players outside the party, `MGQ_MpOverworld::STRANGER_OPACITY`) with the look of the leader's page, walks where the leader's walks and starts nothing, since it is no event of the member's map (`update`, `ghost_of?`, drawn by a `Spriteset_Map` hook). Residents the member has stay their own to talk to; a resident whose page differs only (both show it) stays where the member's game puts it. Decided by the user on 2026-10-03.
