- **Exits** next (`exit?`): a page the player starts (trigger 0-2) that transfers them without dialogue, battle, items, its own self switch or a story script is `:travel`, even when it sets switches or variables on the way. These are bookkeeping: the warp and library flags of a town's entrance, "Warp OFF", "Encounter OFF", or a flag such as Iliasville's north exit noting Sonya. The leader's game sets the same when it passes, so a member takes the exit in their own game instead of handing it to the leader. This turns 370 map pages from story into travel, 128 of them the world map's entrances.
- `:battle`: the page starts a battle (301) and shows no dialogue itself (101, 102, 105). The wandering monsters call common events before and after their battle that set variables, so the page alone decides.
- `:story`: it sets a switch or variable that is neither scratch (named like "General", "temp", "System Only") nor the player's own (see `MGQ_MpStoryState.personal_switch?`); party membership switches 1001-2000 always count. Or it sets its own self switch along with anything but dialogue (dialogue and the self switch alone is a one-time talk, `one_time_talk?`: a signpost or a villager who speaks once, which each player reads in their own game; the self switch follows the leader's story like any other), fights with dialogue, changes the party (129), uses vehicles (206), asks for a name (303), ends the game (353, 354), or runs a script that is not a talk's or travel's. The flags the game toggles around its fights (22, 73, 97) and the event variant it picks before a call (variable 129) are each player's own (`MGQ_MpStoryState`), so clearing them keeps a battle page a battle and a trader's talk a talk.
- `:chest`: it gives items or gold (125-128) and sets its own self switch.
- `:travel`: it transfers the player (201, or the scripts in `TRAVEL_SCRIPTS`).

A talk or travel page with story only behind a choice (`may_tell?`, `WATCHED_KINDS`) is watched while it runs (`guard`): at the first command that moves the story on, a command 117 whose common event holds such a choice included, the leader's game tells it from there as story and a member's ends it. Travel counts since the Great Decision (map 407, events 39-41, common event 380) and 216 other pages, such as the Orb of Guidance at the Gold Coast, hold their transfer behind the same choice as their story; so the decision's transfer takes the players on the map along.
- `:talk`: everything else: conversations, choices, shops, and the scripts in `TALK_SCRIPTS` (companions' own lines, choices, presents and affection, medals, the job change, synthesis, casino and party menus, and shops that list their goods in a script, `goods` or `@goods`, as the Casino's coin sellers, and the cosmetic scripts: custom move routes, waits, character zooms and angles, map zooms, the mini-games' key input and picture checks).

Over the game's 107,478 pages (every map event page and common event, the maps from 1000 on included) this gives, in a new game on 2026-10-11, about 17,600 story, 70,600 talk, 13,700 travel, 5,200 chest and 400 battle pages. The event checker next to this repository (MGQ-Paradox-Event-Checker) measures what every page does in the game and, with its `Tools\sort_events.rb`, sorts every page with these scripts outside the game in seconds; run its sorting check after a change to the sorting, instead of a game run.

**Chests** are the player's own. While the map's main event runs a chest page in a party (`started`), the items and gold it gives are kept, only the outermost of the game's nested `gain_item` calls for enchanted items (`enter_gain`). When the event ends (`Game_Interpreter#run`), the party is told `chest=<map>.<event>.<letter>` and `gains=<kind><id>x<amount>,...` (kinds `i`, `w`, `a`, and `g0` for gold). Every member who has not looted that chest in their own story gets the same items and has it marked looted (`MGQ_MpCoopStory.keep_own_self_switch`). The message also carries the first sound the chest played (`se=<name>,<volume>,<pitch>`, noted after `RPG::SE#play` while it runs, "Chest" by default, `played_sound`); a member who gets the items hears it and reads the notice with the first item's icon (`tell_chest_news`, `first_icon`, `MGQ_MpOverworldSync.notice` with an icon), once on the map: in a battle the items come at once and the sound and notice wait. A sound named like a path plays the chest's own. A member playing the leader's story sees their own chests: the chests' self switches of the map they are on are the player's own in every join of stories (`MGQ_MpStoryState.personal_self_switch?`), and again whenever a map is set up (`map_entered`).

**Story scenes together** (coop_gather.rbx, `MGQ_MpCoopGather`, to which `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.take_party` hands `gather` and `follow`). Party event messages carry `pevent=<kind>`, the party's id, and the leader's place: `map`, `x`, `y`, `d`. Only the leader's are taken.

- Members do not follow the leader to other maps; only story scenes bring them over.
- **Teleports between members** (`coop_teleport.rbx`, `MGQ_MpCoopTeleport`, Classic and Raid Worlds alike): *Teleport to \<leader>* (`option`, offered by `MGQ_MpCoop::Offers.teleport_option` on the wheel in place of the invite) and *Ask to teleport to \<name>* in each member's row of the World overview (`Offers.peer_option`) ask through the player's state: `tpask=<member id>:<request number>` (`ask`, `state_fields`, a newer request replacing the one before). The leader's game allows a member's request as soon as it sees it (`observe`, through `MGQ_MpOverworldSync.on_observe`); any other member allows or declines it in the notification box (`Offers.notice_of`, "\<name> asks to teleport to you", *Allow*) or with *Allow teleport* in the asker's row of their World overview. The answer goes through the party's gate (`MGQ_MpCoop.route`) as `ptp=allow` with the member's `place_fields` or `ptp=deny`, each with the request's `nonce`, and each request is answered once (`answer`). An allowed request brings the asker over as a called member comes, without the five seconds (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.come_to`, which waits up to a minute for a busy asker, `ASKED_LAPSE_FRAMES`), an answer to another or older request is ignored. A request lapses after a minute (`ASK_FRAMES`, "\<name> did not answer your teleport."), ends once its member left the party, *Stop asking to teleport* on the player's own row takes it back, and a loaded save or the world's close forgets it (`forget`).
- `gather`: when the leader's main event starts a story scene (`scene?`: story with its own dialogue, or a novel scene) and a member stands away (`gathered?`: on the leader's map within `GATHER_TILES`, 3), the scene waits at its first command (`holding?`, a `Game_Interpreter#execute_command` hook that yields the interpreter's fiber), so the leader can neither move nor open the menu, and calls the members, again every 3 s (`CALL_FRAMES`). A member called (`called`) gets 5 s (`GATHER_FRAMES`, measured with `Graphics.frame_count`, a countdown above their head) to finish what they do, then is brought to the leader once free (`come?`, `update`): on the map, after a battle, menu, event or transfer of their own. The call carries whether warping is banned where the leader stands (`warp_ban`, the game's switch 100, `MGQ_MpStoryState::WARP_BAN`), and the member takes it over on arrival (`take_warp_ban`): the game sets the ban in the events a player walks through to enter or leave a cave, which a member brought over never touches, so a Harpy Feather would otherwise stay unusable. The switch is each player's own (`PERSONAL_SWITCHES`), since it tells of the place a player stands in, not of the story. While called (`coming?`) the member meets no random encounter (`Game_Player#encounter` starts none and draws new steps once they ran out) and turns co-op battle invites down, so no new battle holds them up. Once everyone stands near, or after 30 s (`HOLD_FRAMES`) without those still away (`missing`), the scene plays and the leader's state tells `telling=1` (`story_playing?`); meanwhile every member on the leader's map stands still and cannot open the menu (`blocked?`, through `Game_Player#movable?` and `Scene_Map#update_call_menu`). A member's call lapses once no call came for 6 s (`CALL_LAPSE_FRAMES`, `pending_call`), so one the scene started without is never brought over later; leader calls and a Classic follow keep that lapse, while a teleport the player asked for (`come_to`, from `coop_teleport.rbx` and `join_story`) and a Raid World's follow have their own, `ASKED_LAPSE_FRAMES` (60 s), after which the teleport says "You were too busy to teleport to \<name>.". A call or teleport to a map this game lacks says "Cannot teleport to \<name>: this game has no map \<id>." in place of the arrival. Whether this game has a map (`map_known?`) is read from the file where the game keeps it (`map_file`, the game's own `DataManager.map_file_name`: the maps from 1000 on lie in `Data/Map/Data`, `Data/Map2/Data` and so on), since `Data/Map1650.rvdata2` does not exist and every follow onto such a map was dropped. A loaded save sets `Graphics.frame_count` back, so a moment before it counts as long past (`within?`), and a loaded save or the world closing forgets the calls, a teleport the player asked for and the hold (`forget`).
- `follow`: when the leader's story moves the leader elsewhere (a transfer while `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.telling?`, noted around `Game_Player#perform_transfer`: `before_transfer`, `after_transfer`), such as a theater show's stage and the "Stage End" common event's way back to the Grand Theater (map 624), or a story's own teleport, the members who stood near the leader's old place are told the new one and come at once, without the five seconds (`followed`). Until they arrive, the leader's pages of that map reach them (`story_map?`), so none is missed. A follow that lapses tells "<Leader>'s story went on without you." (`lapse_notice`). The leader's other transfers take nobody along.
- A PvP battle keeps calls out (`pvp_running?`), and when a duel puts its snapshot of the game back (`MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Battle.restore`) a call that waited through it is dropped (`drop_call`), so the member answers the leader's next call; a member is only moved on a settled map (`settled?`: no PvP battle, no Library battle, `Graphics.brightness` 255). A call that waited through a duel and moved the player as the map started again left a lasting black screen in the field test of 2026-10-04.
- Gathering, calls, arrivals, follows and calls that lapsed each write a line to the in-game log (`co-op gather:`).
- A member is moved once they are free (`free?`: on the map, with no event, message or transfer of their own in the way), checked after every map update, so a member in a menu or battle follows when they are back. Another map means a transfer, the same map a plain `moveto`.

**Story in the leader's game.** A member's game never plays story itself:

- A story event the member starts (`Game_Map#setup_starting_map_event`, which looks at every starting event, so one left to the leader never keeps the member's other events from starting) does not start, in the member's game or the leader's: only the leader starts story, in their own game (`hand_over`). The member hears "Only <Leader> can move the story on.", once per event in 3 s (`refuse_story`, `REFUSE_FRAMES`), since an event the player stands on starts again with every step, with "Bring them here to go on." while the leader is elsewhere.
- Story events and story common events that run by themselves (autorun) are left to the leader's game (`leave_to_leader?` in `setup_autorun_common_event`), which runs them on its own map.
- While the leader's main event plays story (`@telling`, which ends with the event, `finished`, since a loaded save or the game put back after a PvP battle runs the map's last event's list once more), every message page and its choices (`show`, caught in `Game_Interpreter#wait_for_message`, since the Yanfly plugin writes its own `command_101` after the Patch folder loads) go to the party (`pevent=say`: face, index, background, position, and the lines and choices in Base64). Each page carries an id (`page_id`, a session tag and a count, so a page of an earlier session is never taken for one done), and once the leader moved past it the leader's game says so (`shown`, after `wait_for_message`: `pevent=done`). Members on the leader's map show the page in their own message window once free (`show_heard`, at most 30 kept), the choices as its last line ("<Leader> chooses: Yes / No"), and leave out the pages the leader moved past meanwhile (`done?`), so a member back from a menu or battle joins at the leader's page. Only the leader moves it on: `Window_Message#input_pause` waits on such a page (`mirroring?`) for its `done` instead of the player's buttons or the game's skip key (`page_done?`), and ends it anyway once the leader is gone or stopped telling the story for 2 s (`STALE_FRAMES`, since the leader's state and their pages travel apart). A page too long for the window shows its rest after 2.5 s (`OVERFLOW_FRAMES`); `process_input` marks the pause at a page's end, after which the player's own messages wait for their buttons again (`page_ended`). Escape codes such as `\V[n]` are read in the member's game, which plays the leader's story. Novel scenes are not shown to members yet.

**The story's scene** (coop_scene.rbx, `MGQ_MpCoopScene`). While the leader's story plays for the party (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.story_playing?`), each change the story makes besides its dialogue goes to the party as `pscene=<kind>` with the map and the arguments as one `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Wire` line: a picture of the map's screen (`picture.show`, `move`, `rotate`, `start_tone_change`, `erase`), such as a story's CG, the map's screen (`screen.start_fadeout`, `start_fadein`, `start_tone_change`, `start_flash`, `start_shake`, and the game's `od_fadein`/`od_fadeout`), the leader's character hidden or shown (`player.transparent`), as on a theater show's stage, and what the story does to the map's events: a move route (`event.route`, hooked after `Game_Character#force_move_route`: the event's id, whether it repeats or skips, and its commands up to code 44 with a sound as its file, volume and pitch, never a script, 45), an animation or balloon (`event.animation`, `event.balloon`, after the writers of `Game_CharacterBase`; -1 for the player's character, which a viewer sees on their own) and an erased event (`event.erase`). In a Raid World the story's switches, variables (whole numbers only) and self switches go too, gathered over the frame and sent once after the map's update (`story_value`, `send_values`, `story.values`, each as `[kind, key, value]`, what `MGQ_MpWorldStory.personal?` names each player's own left out): the world's story reaches the viewers only once the telling ended, so without them a viewer's game showed the scene's characters on no page (`coop_npcs.rbx`: "stays the player's own: page -1 here"), and the Map Owner's moves of them were dropped. A Classic party's members get the leader's values from `coop_story.rbx` every quarter second instead. A viewer's pages give way to whatever waits for the player to be free and lapses meanwhile (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.give_way_to`, `giving_way?`: the story's follow through `MGQ_MpCoopGather.due?`, a battle's invite or lead request through `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.waiting_for_player`): no page starts, a shown page the teller moved past ends at once (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.read?`), the passed pages are left out (`give_way`), and the rest show after the arrival, since the follow waits for a free player and the pages kept them busy for good in the field test of 2026-10-11. The values move a viewer's own progress past where the telling started (`tsm`), which alone would end their watching on both sides, so the teller keeps counting the players it sent values to in this telling as viewers (`Scope::Raid.sent_values_to`, `sent_values?`), and the viewer keeps watching the teller whose values it took (`Scope::Raid.takes_values`, `watches?`): it remembers, per teller and up to `TAKEN_STARTS`, the telling's `tsm` once the teller's state names it and its own progress before the values, never the teller's `sm`, which between the story's events on two maps stands in for `tsm` and dropped the viewer in the field test of 2026-10-11. The player's character's move routes go to the viewers too (`event.route` with -1), so their own character walks the route and the camera, which follows it, moves as the teller's; while it walks one, `walks_player?` keeps `Game_Player#update_nonmoving` and `check_event_trigger_touch` from starting what it steps on or bumps into and from meeting enemies. A route the viewer takes always skips a blocked step (`apply_route`), since their characters stand elsewhere than the teller's and a step that never succeeds would hold the character for good. The story's camera goes as its place, `map.camera` (`send_camera`: the teller's `display_x` and `display_y` in 32nds of a tile, once it moved and `CAMERA_FRAMES` after the last at most; the viewer's camera jumps there from more than `CAMERA_JUMP` tiles and eases a `CAMERA_EASE`-th of the way each frame nearer, after `Game_Player#update`, only on the teller's map, and comes back onto the viewer's character with `restore`), since a scroll moves the camera from where the viewer's own character stands, the story's sounds as `audio.<call>` (`audio`, before each of the game's `Audio` calls in `AUDIO_CALLS`: music, background sounds, fanfares and sound effects played with the file named without its folder, volume, pitch and the music's position, those the story left out as the game's defaults, `AUDIO_DEFAULTS`, stopped and faded; a viewer plays them through `RPG::BGM`, `RPG::BGS`, `RPG::ME` and `RPG::SE` on the teller's map screen alone, a name with a path left out and a file it cannot play rescued; away from it, as in a battle or a menu, whose end brings their own music back, or on the way to the teller's map, whose autoplay would replace it, the last music and background sound wait until the viewer is there, `hold_audio`, `play_held`, and the fanfares and sound effects are left out), the map's look as `map.tileset`, `map.battleback` and `map.parallax` (`map_look`, before `Game_Map#change_tileset`, `change_battleback` and `change_parallax`; on the teller's map alone, a tileset of `$data_tilesets` and pictures this game has), and `map.zoom` (after the game's `map_zoom`, kept to 100-2000 and put back by `restore`). Only the story's own changes count (`story_change?`, `map_turn?`): those made inside the update of the map's main interpreter, which a common event it calls runs inside too, noted around every `Game_Interpreter#update` (`updating`, `updated`); a parallel event, such as the map's own display, changes pictures every frame and stays the leader's own. The update tells interpreters apart rather than the command, since a command may wait frames before it runs, as while the party gathers. A member on the leader's map, or on the way there (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.story_map?`, since the screen keeps its pictures and tint through a transfer), makes the same change (`apply`), a picture only by a number of 1-100 and a file name that may hold dots but no path; an event's change only on the teller's map itself, since another map's events share its ids; a story value only for an id this game has, never one that is the viewer's own, taken as the world's (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.mirrored` sets it in the story the viewer built on, so it is not written as their own change); a move route without a command it cannot run (`route_command`: a graphic this game has, `MGQ_MpOverworld.graphic?`, a sound file in `Audio/SE`, whole numbers otherwise, a name without a path), never waited for. It notes what to put back, with the screen's tint before the story's first screen change. Once the member no longer sees the story (`watching?`: the leader stopped telling it, or either left the map) and no change came for 2 s (`STATE_GRACE_FRAMES`, since the leader's state that says the story plays travels apart from the changes), the story's pictures are erased, flash and shake cleared, the member's own tint comes back over 30 frames (`RESTORE_FRAMES`) on the map they watched from (on a map the story brought them to, the story's last tint stays), the brightness comes back, the member's own character shows as before on the map they watched from (on a map the story brought them to, as the story left it, so a new game's hidden start does not hide them again) and a teller's route their character still walks ends (`end_player_route`) (`restore`); a loaded save forgets it (`forget`).

## Shared UI (`ui.rbx`)

What every flat sprite box of the mod draws with, so no widget declares a color, a font size or a timing of its own; the mod's `Window_Base` windows wear the preset's window skin instead (see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)).

- **Style** (`MGQ_MpUi::Style`): the colors (`BACK` of every box, `PLATE` behind one line on a busy picture, `PICKED` for the picked row or choice, `FIELD` of a text field, `OUTLINE` solid black, `TEXT`, `DIM`, `GOOD`, `WARN`, `BAD`, `GOLD`, `HEADING`, `OWN` yellow for the player, `MEMBER` green for a party member, the same object as `GOOD`; `BACK`, `PLATE`, `PICKED`, `FIELD`, `HEADING`, `EDGE` the thin frame, `RIM` the line around it, `TITLE`, `WELL` a gauge's empty part and `TAB` an unpicked tab are the preset's, set in place by `ui_theme.rbx`), `DIM_OPACITY` of an icon that cannot be taken, the font sizes (`TITLE_SIZE` 20, `TEXT_SIZE` 18, `SMALL_SIZE` 16, `TINY_SIZE` 14), `PAD` (6), `GAP` (4, between columns and tabs), `HINT_GAP` (three spaces between a hint's parts) and the timings (`FADE_FRAMES` 12, `SLIDE_FRAMES` 8, `LINGER_FRAMES` 180 for a transient line). `use_font(bitmap, size, color)` sets a bitmap's font (never bold, solid outline), `draw_outlined` draws a text with outline copies as far as `OUTLINE_REACH` (the corners left out), keeping a copy of the font's color, since RGSS's `Font#color` hands out the font's own color, which `Font#color=` overwrites, and with the font's own outline off meanwhile, which each copy would grow a pixel further, `draw_plate` fills the plate behind a text as wide as the text plus `PAD` on either side, `draw_plated` both. `close_hint(*also)` words what closes a screen in one order everywhere ("Esc, Numpad 0 or F11: close", "Esc or Numpad 0: close"), which the wheels (`MGQ_MpWheel.draw_hint`), the list box, the overview and the chat use. `faded(color, frames)` lowers a color's alpha over the last `FADE_FRAMES` frames of a line about to go. `draw_box` fills a box with `BACK` inside its frame, `draw_frame` draws the frame alone (`RIM` outermost while it is not see-through, then `EDGE`; `frame_width` is 1 or 2), `draw_line_box` one outline. A new box draws through `draw_box` and `Style.use_font`, cuts names with `MGQ_MpUi.cut` and names no color of its own; a script's color of a meaning the style has is the style's: the formation's cut line (`MGQ_MpCoopSquad::CUT_COLOR`) and the Raid Worlds' marker (`MGQ_MpWorld::RAID_COLOR`) are `BAD`, the featured worlds' `GOLD`, a ghost's outline its member's `MEMBER` or `TEXT`, and every request and offer, the invites, challenges, trade offers, story offers, teleport asks and the join prompt, `MGQ_MpActions::LINE_COLOR` (`OWN`).
- **Motion** (`MGQ_MpUi::Motion`, one per sprite, owned by the sprite's owner, which calls `update` every frame from where it updates the sprite): `show(dx, dy)` makes a hidden sprite visible at an offset from its resting place with opacity 0 and heads for the place and full opacity, a slide with a fade; from a fade out it only turns the opacity back, so nothing jumps; `hide` heads for opacity 0 and sets `visible` false on arrival; `place(x, y)` sets the resting place without a slide (boxes that follow `MGQ_MpUi.top_room`), `slide_to(x, y)` slides there from where the sprite stands; `moving?`, `shown?` (target opacity 255), `hidden?`. Linear steps through `Motion.step_towards`, no easing, which the raid bar's own crop shares. An owner never sets `visible` or `opacity` itself once it has a Motion, and does not redraw while `!shown?`, so the last picture fades out; the owner disposes the sprite and drops the Motion with it.
- **PlayerColor** (`MGQ_MpUi::PlayerColor`): `of(who, member = nil, own = nil)` is `Style::OWN` for the player themself (`:me`, or an id equal to `MGQ_MpOverworldSync::Me.id`), `Style::MEMBER` for a party member (a Peer's `member`, a state Hash through `MGQ_MpCoop::Party.member?`), else `Style::TEXT`, also for a `who` it cannot read, never raising. `label` names the color for the log ("own", "member", "plain"). `who` is a Peer, a state Hash, an id String or `:me`. ui.rbx loads before overworld_sync.rbx and coop.rbx, so the derivations reach them at run time, guarded with `defined?`. Used by the labels above heads, the party and map box, the World overview's rows, the co-op status window and the edge arrows.
- **Confirm** (`MGQ_MpUi::Confirm`, `Sprite_MpConfirmBox`): one yes/no box at a time over any scene. `ask(question, [yes, no]) { |answer| }` opens it (refused and logged while one is open or the question is empty), wraps the question to `Sprite_MpConfirmBox::WIDTH` (400), draws the two choices with the no choice picked first and the hint `HINT` ("Leave: Y   Stay: N, Esc or Numpad 0   Enter: the picked one", the hotkeys by `MGQ_MpHotkeys.label`, drawn whole and smaller when long labels make it wide), at `Z[:confirm]` without a viewport, so it lasts across scenes, and takes the buttons (`Capture.start(SCREEN)`), so the trade scene's windows and the map stay quiet under it; the hotkeys are held by the gate in `MGQ_MpHotkeys.pressed?`. `tick` runs from the `Scene_Base#update` after-hook every frame: it reads the accept and decline hotkeys (`Key.triggered?`, watched anew on every `ask`), `MGQ_MpUi.cancel?`, confirm (`Capture.trigger?(:C)` takes the picked choice) and left or right (moves the pick), none while `$mgq_text_input` and none in the frame of `ask` (a caller asking from a window's own `update` without an `Input.update` would otherwise answer with the press that chose). An answer takes two ticks: the tick that reads it (or `close`, or the scene no longer being the one `ask` saw) stores it, plays the sound, disposes the sprite and returns with `open?` still true and the capture on, and the next tick, after `Input.update` ended the press, stops the capture and calls the block once; so the Enter or Esc that answered never reaches the overview's menu or the trade's windows in the same frame. The block gets false for a close from outside and a scene change. `leave_scene`, from the `Scene_Base#start` after-hook, answers no and finishes at once, since a scene whose start waits in a loop of its own (a co-op battle's `battle_start`) would never tick a box left open, and a start reads no key. The callers: the overview's *Remove from party* and *Leave the party* (`MGQ_MpCoop::Offers`, `REMOVE_CHOICES`, `LEAVE_CHOICES`), the trade screen's cancel (`Scene_MpTrade#on_cancel_trade`); the overview skips its own `update` while `Confirm.open?`.

## Interface presets and layout (`ui_theme.rbx`, `ui_layout.rbx`)

**Presets** (`MGQ_MpUi::Theme`). `PRESETS` holds Monarch, the default (the raid boss bar's maroon `36, 10, 14` with its gold edge `200, 164, 96` in a black rim, gold titles `255, 224, 140`), and Legacy (the chat's black glass, grey edge, no rim, white titles, the mod's look before the presets and the values `ui.rbx` starts the style's colors with), each a color per style constant from `BACK` to `TAB`. `apply(name, why)` copies the preset's values into those `Color` objects in place, so every alias of them elsewhere follows (`CLOSED_SHADE = Style::PLATE`, the raid bar's `EDGE`, `EMPTY` and `TITLE_COLOR`), and counts `serial`, which every picture drawn in the colors keeps in its redraw key; a `Color.new` copy of a style color would not follow. `load` applies `Player.ini`'s `ui_preset` as the script loads, `choose` applies and keeps it. The red HP fill and the damage trail of the raid bar, the text colors, the chat's colors and `PlayerColor` are the same in both. The row *Interface Look* of Mod Config Remake picks it (`MGQ_MpUi.add_option`: a `:mod_stored` row, so the menu reads and keeps nothing in the save; only a Mod Config Remake that knows such rows gets it).

**Window skin.** `skin` paints a 128x128 window skin in the preset (`paint_skin`, made once per preset): the background in `BACK` made opaque (the window's `back_opacity` carries the alpha), no pattern, a box's frame along the frame square's borders (`SKIN_BORDER`, its middle keeps the scroll arrows) and the cursor as a picked row in its frame; the rest (arrows, pause sign, text colors) is the game's own `Window` skin. `Skinned`, included into the mod's window classes (`Window_MpInfo`, the world screen's, the trade screen's, `Window_PvpLobbyCommand`, `Window_MpStoryChoices` through `Window_MpWorldForm`, `Window_MpWaiting`; the text screen's keep the game's skin), dresses a window as it is made and again once `serial` changed (`dress`: skin, `back_opacity`, no tone) and replaces `update_tone`, since the game's window color would tint the preset. `ui.rbx` declares the empty module, since `Window_MpInfo` includes it before `ui_theme.rbx` loads; a game window a mod scene makes, the trade screen's help window, is dressed and extended with it (`Scene_MpTrade#wear_theme`).

**Layout** (`MGQ_MpUi::Layout`). A floating box registers once (`register(id, name, min_width, min_height, resize)`, resize `:both`, `:width` with its height following what it shows, or `:none`), asks `rect(id, default)` every frame for where to draw (the drag's place while dragged, else `Player.ini`'s `ui_<id>` as `x,y,width,height`, else its default, fitted to its least size and wholly on the screen) and `offer`s the rect it drew while it shows, with its depth. `tick`, after `Graphics.update`, reads the left Alt (`ALT_KEY`, 0xA4; the right Alt is AltGr) and the left button against the boxes offered since the tick before: Alt with the button going down on the topmost box under the mouse starts a `Drag`, which moves the box, or resizes the sides within `GRAB` (8) of the mouse; the sides stop at the least size and the screen's edges (`span`); letting go keeps the place (`finish`) and logs it. A second click within `DOUBLE_CLICK_FRAMES` puts the box back (`reset`). While Alt is held an overlay above everything (`Z[:layout]`) outlines every offered box with its name, washes the one under the mouse, draws a grip into a resizable box's corner and names the controls at the bottom (`HINT`). `MGQ_Multiplayer::Mouse.clicked?` and `held?` answer false while `arranging?`, so no box takes a click meant for the layout. The row *Interface Positions* says *Moved* while `any_moved?` and puts every box back with *Default* (`reset_all`). The boxes of `ui.rbx` register from its `Scene_Base#start` hook too, since the layout loads after it and *Default* must reach a box not shown yet.

**The boxes.** Each box keeps today's place as its default and works out its least size from its own content, so it always shows everything it holds:

| Box | Id | Resizes | Least size |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chat on the map, in battle | `:chat`, `:battle_chat` | both | 400 wide (tabs and hints), 5 rows (the whisper names) |
| Notification box | `:notices` | both | the hotkey column plus a name (298), 2 rows (`capped` shows nothing in one) |
| Party box and a Raid World's map box | `:party_box` | width | `WIDTH` (296); small mode keeps `SMALL_WIDTH` at the box's right edge |
| World status lines | `:world_status` | width | 266, 4 rows |
| World overview | `:overview` | both | the Players and World pages' columns (560), title, tabs, hint and 3 rows or the longest menu |
| Raid boss bar (map and battle strip) | `:raid_bar` | width | the wider of the title line with the hint at both sides and three gauges with their share (`min_strip_width`), measured at load |
| Waiting box | `:waiting_box` | width | its text and the leave offer, up to 360 |
| Confirm box, message box | `:confirm_box`, `:message_box` | width | the confirm box's choices and either box's whole hint, up to the default width |
| List box of the world screen | `:list_box` | both | the item and button columns (460), a view's note and hint, 3 rows |

A box at its default moves down by `MGQ_MpUi.top_room`; one the player moved stays where they put it, and `top_room` counts the raid strip only while it lies at the top. Labels, bubbles, wheels, the overhead raid bar, the edge arrows and the join prompt follow a character and only take the preset's colors. The DLL's `AltMenuGuard` swallows `SC_KEYMENU` for Alt pressed alone and while a text screen takes the keyboard, so letting Alt go never opens the window's menu, in which the game would wait.

## Actions (`ui_actions.rbx`)

**The action wheel** (`Wheel`, `Sprite_MpActionWheel`). Its hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys`, B by default, read with `GetAsyncKeyState`, since neither the game's `Input` nor its gamepad plugin reads it) opens it on the map while no event or message runs, and closes it again. Its choices (`wheel_options`, rebuilt every frame from what the scripts registered) are a ring of up to eight, spread over the arrows' directions in their order (`wheel_places`), and one over the player, the World overview: a globe (`MGQ_MpWorldOverview::WORLD_ICON`, the game's icon 3988) in a square box narrower than the player's sprite, so it never covers the boxes beside it. `Sprite_MpActionWheel.layout` lays the ring out from its directions: the side and diagonal boxes in a column left and right of the player, up and down above and below all of them, and the picture is sized to fit. It opens on the middle; the arrows held point at a choice, which stays picked once they are let go, and a direction without a choice picks the middle. The game's confirm button takes it, and cancel closes the wheel. A choice without `run` is grey, and taking it shows its `refusal` as a notice. While the wheel is open it holds the buttons (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Capture`, which has the guarded `Input` report none to the game, while the wheel reads them past it), so the player stands still and the game's menu and map keys stay shut. Leaving the map, an event or a message closes it.

- Middle: the World overview (`MGQ_MpWorldOverview.open`).
- Up: accept the invite of a player nearby (`MGQ_MpCoop::NEAR_TILES`, 2, on the same map) who is outside the party, else invite the players nearby who are outside it.
- Down: leave the party, or stop an invite nobody took.
- Left: chat, grey while the keyboard cannot reach the game (`Background.running?`).
- Right: accept the challenge of a player nearby, else challenge the players nearby, or stop challenging (`MGQ_MpBattlesDuel`).

While the World overview is open, the wheel stays shut and the wheel's hotkey closes the overview instead.

**The line above the player's own head** (`own_lines`, `own_line`, drawn by the Spriteset hook): the first line an `own_line_from` block names wins; else the doings the `own_doing_from` blocks name, joined with ", " and " . . .". `own_lines` gives the line with the seconds and without them: the sprite draws the first while it fits its width, else cuts the second, so a cut never drops a countdown that fits. A doing is `nil`, a String ("Inviting to a party"), or `[String, Time]` with the moment it lapses, which the line writes as "Inviting to a party (12 s)" (`TIME_LEFT`, `doing_text`: the seconds rounded up, the text alone once lapsed or when the lapse is no Time, so a frame count by mistake breaks nothing). Timers of the mod count frames, so a caller converts with `MGQ_MpActions.lapse_in(frames)`, or `lapse_at(since, frames)` for a moment counted from a `Graphics.frame_count`; `MGQ_MpCoop::Invite#frames_left` and `MGQ_MpCoopGather.frames_left(since, frames)` give them. One `Time.now` per frame, read after the blocks ran. Each block runs through `named_by`: one that raises is logged once and left out, so the line and the wheel keep showing. The log compares the texts without the seconds, so a countdown logs once. The doings today: the party invite (coop.rbx), a teleport request to a member (coop_teleport.rbx), the leader's gathering with up to `NAMED_MISSING` (2) missing members and "+n", and a member's time before coming over (coop_gather.rbx), a challenge, the wait for an accepted duel and a team duel's readiness (battles_duel.rbx), a trade offer and the wait for an accepted trade (trade.rbx).

## Chat (`ui_chat.rbx`)

**The chat box.** The chat's hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:chat`, T by default) or the wheel's chat choice opens the chat box, which takes the keyboard through `mp_typing`/`mp_take_typed` like the world screen's text box, and holds the buttons (`Capture`), so keys that type move nobody. It opens while the map is quiet, or while the player only waits for the party's story (`map_open?`, `MGQ_MpCoopGather.waiting?`: gathering, called, held or reading the leader's pages), and a box already open stays when an event starts, whose messages then wait for it. The character of the key that opened it arrives after it opened, since the key is read from Windows before the game's window gets the character, and is left out (`without_echo`, within `KEY_ECHO_FRAMES`). Enter sends, Escape or the numpad's 0 closes, as the numpad's 0 closes the game's windows (`MGQ_MpUi.numpad_cancel?`, see below, read from Windows; its 0 arrives as the number row's, so what came in its frame is dropped, and a later one finds the typing off), and a line has at most 120 characters (`MAX_LENGTH`). The box has a cursor that blinks every half second (`BLINK_FRAMES`) and shows at once after a change. Typing and Backspace work at the cursor. Left and right move it (`Capture.repeat?`, so a held arrow repeats), Home and End jump to either end, and Delete removes the character after it. None of these keys types a character, so they are read as the game's buttons or from Windows (`Key`). The text moves left as far as the cursor needs to stay in sight. A line goes to the chosen chat (see below) as its own message: the global chat's `chat=<line>` and `name=<sender>` without a `map` to everyone, so `overworld.rbx` hands it to the chat's route rather than taking it for a state, the map chat's with `ch=say` (its name before, which released games read) to each player whose state names the player's map (`tell_map`, `on_this_map?`), and a receiver takes a `ch=say` line into the map chat and drops one from a player on another map; the name covers a line that arrives before its sender's first state. Every line, the player's own included:

- joins the chat log (`Sprite_MpChatLog`, font 16) at the bottom left, above the status line, for 10 s (`LOG_FRAMES`); the open chat box shows the kept lines (`KEPT`), wrapped, with the box on the row below, and scrolls them: up and down a row, Page Up and Page Down a page (`PAGE_ROWS`, the page keys read from Windows, since the game's L and R are Q and W too). `MGQ_MpChat.scroll` counts the rows below the last one shown; the sprite breaks the lines into rows (`rows_of`, once per text), keeps the scroll above the oldest row (`limit_scroll`), scrolls on by the rows of lines that come in meanwhile so the rows shown stay put (`keep_place`), and draws an arrow at the right while older rows lie above or newer ones below. Closing the box shows the newest rows again. The log lies on a viewport of its own, which rises above everything while the box is open (`MGQ_MpUi::Z[:typing]`) and otherwise rests just under the map's topmost viewport (`@viewport3.z - 1`), whose wheels and World overview lie above it, or in a battle above its windows (`BATTLE_ROOM`, `Z[:wheels]`); and T and typing work through `Scene_Battle#update_basic` (`on_battle`), which runs in the battle's waits too;
- shows for 6 s (`BUBBLE_FRAMES`) in a bubble (`Sprite_MpChatBubble`) above the sender, while their ghost is on the player's map, placed from the character's `screen_x`/`screen_y`, so it needs no sprite of `overworld.rbx`.

**Chats, tabs and colors.** A line goes to the global chat (everyone in the world), the map chat (the players on the same map), the party chat or the whisper chat (one player). `/g`, `/m` or `/p` (also `/global`, `/map`, `/party`, `COMMANDS`) at a line's start chooses the chat for it and every line after it (`take_command`; with a space after it the command is taken out of the box as it is typed, and the box names the chosen chat before the text); the chat starts in the global chat, also in every world after the first (`reset`), and a chosen chat holds until the player chooses another; `/s`, which chose it once, is plain text now. `/w <name>` or `/whisper <name>` chooses the whisper chat and whom it goes to: while the box holds the command and the start of a name, a list above the box offers the names of the world's players that fit (`suggestions`, at most `SUGGESTIONS`), Up and Down move the pick, and Enter or Tab (a typed `\t`, or the Tab key read from Windows) takes it and empties the box (`take_suggestion`); a line sent with the command finds the longest name that starts it (`named_in`). A whisper is `whisper=<line>` and `name=<sender>` to that player's seat alone (`tell_whisper`, routed to `receive_whisper`); the sender's log shows `[To <name>] `, the receiver's `[Whisper] `, and Multiplayer InGame.log leaves its text out. `/help` opens a help tab at the far right of the tabs (`open_help`), which shows `HELP` and nothing else, takes no typing but Escape, and closes once another tab is picked or the box closes (`leave_help`). The empty box names scrolling, Alt and `/help` (`BOX_HINT`), and the tab and resize controls while Alt is held (`ALT_HINT`). A click on another player's name in the open box's log (each line keeps its sender's id, the sprite where their name lies, `@name_spots`) opens their menu above the name (`open_menu`, drawn by `draw_menu` on a sprite of its own): `MGQ_MpActions.peer_options`, which the World overview shows too, and Whisper. Pointing or Up and Down pick a choice, a click or Enter takes it through `MGQ_MpActions.choose` (one that leaves, such as accepting a duel, closes the box too), and Escape, the numpad's 0 or a click outside closes the menu alone; it closes once the player leaves the world. The party chat sends `pchat=<line>` through `MGQ_MpCoop.tell`, so it carries the party's id and the party's gate (`coop.rbx` routes `pchat` to `receive_party`, since `ui_chat.rbx` loads before the gate) drops it for everyone else; outside a party it is refused with a notice. The open box shows tabs above the log (`TABS`: All, Global, Map, Party, Whisper; `tabs` leaves the Whisper tab out until the player whispered, got a whisper or chose the whisper chat, `whisper_tab?`, set in `choose` and `add` and cleared with the whisper chat in `reset` when the world closes), picked with a click or the left Alt with left or right (`alt_keys`, the arrows read from Windows every frame, the editor's keys left alone while Alt is held; the right Alt is AltGr, which types). What arrives while the left Alt is held and for `ALT_CODE_FRAMES` after is dropped (`without_alt_code`): Alt with the numpad's arrows, which are its digits, types an Alt code once Alt is let go, a symbol the game's font lacks; a chat's tab also chooses that chat, a command chosen on a chat's tab moves to its tab, and All keeps the chat. A tab shows its chat's lines and the game's own (`Line#shown_in?`). The log is a layout box, `:chat` on the map and `:battle_chat` in battle (`Sprite_MpChatLog::BOXES`), which the left Alt and the mouse move and resize (see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)): its height gives its rows (snapped to whole rows, drawn at the rect's bottom), at least `MIN_WIDTH` (400, which the tabs and the hints need) and `MIN_ROWS` (5, the whisper names above the box); `chat_size` in Player.ini, which the chat's own resize kept before, is read only as the default size. The open chat and each tab have a thin outline in the preset's edge (`OUTLINE`, `Style::EDGE`, `draw_frame`), the chat's white while Alt is held (`RESIZE_OUTLINE`). The picked tab is filled with its chat's color under dark text (`TAB_INK`). A line of any chat, the whispers too (`UNREAD_CHATS`), that comes while neither its tab nor All is picked lets that tab glow yellow at its top right corner, fading toward its middle and opaque at the corner (`UNREAD_GLOW`, `draw_unread_glow`), until its tab or All is picked (`see_tab`, every frame). Letting Alt go never opens the window's menu, in which the game would wait (`AltMenuGuard`, see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)). The log keeps each line as a `Line` (name, text, `who`, channel, frames left) and draws its head in color (`draw_row`): the tag (`[Map] `, `[Party] `, the global chat none; `TAGS`) in its chat's color (`CHANNEL_COLORS`, World of Warcraft's: the global chat white as its say chat, the map chat its zone pink 255, 192, 192, the party chat its party blue 170, 170, 255, whispers its whisper pink 255, 128, 255), then the sender's name by `who` (`NAME_COLORS`: the player's own yellow, a member of the player's party green as their label on the map, anyone else white, an admin's gold as the featured worlds, the game's own lines grey), then the text, in the chat's color for the map chat and the whispers (`COLORED_TEXT`) and white otherwise. A bubble is filled with its chat's color, white for the global chat, at 85 % opacity (`Sprite_MpChatBubble::OPACITY`). `log_lines` writes the lines as text, as the tests read them.

**Sent lines, replies and whispers heard.** Every line sent is kept as typed, commands included, in `history` (`remember_sent`: the last `HISTORY_SIZE` (20), no repeat of the last, for the session only, cleared when the world closes); the left Alt with up or down (`UP_KEY`, `DOWN_KEY`, read from Windows with the tab arrows in `alt_keys`) walks through them (`walk_history`: the draft being typed is kept and comes back past the newest; not on the help tab). `/r` (`REPLY_COMMAND`) answers the last player who whispered (`reply_to`, noted in `whispered` with their name and id): it chooses the whisper chat for them as `/w` does, refused while nobody whispered or once they left (`player_of`: by id, else by name for a sender without a state yet; a known id never falls back to the name). A whisper that arrives while the box is closed plays `WHISPER_SOUND` (`Sound.play_recovery`, which nothing on the map plays by itself), once a frame. `UNREAD_CHATS` is every chat, the whispers too, so the Whisper tab glows; while the box is closed and a tab has unread lines, the log draws the tab row dimmed above itself (`draw_tabs(chat, true)`, `CLOSED_SHADE` over each tab, the glow over the shade), so the glow is seen. A character the box refuses buzzes once a frame (`@buzzed`), so a paste longer than the box takes buzzes once; Ctrl+V pastes through the DLL's typing path, as the world screen's text boxes do. `ALT_HINT` names the sent lines, `HELP` the sent lines, `/r` and Ctrl+V.

**The admins' chat.** Every line of the global chat is also mirrored to the relay (`MGQ_MpOverworldSync.say`, `mp_world_say`), which keeps a world's last lines for its admins; the map and the party chat are not. A line an admin says in the World Admin tool arrives as a `chat` inbox entry with the admin's `name`, which `overworld_sync.rbx` hands to the chat's route as `chat=<line>`, `name=<admin>` and the Symbol key `:relay`, which no player's message can carry, since those are parsed into strings; the log shows it as `[Admin] <name>: <line>` in gold, without a bubble, since no seat said it.

While the chat box is open, `ui_actions.rbx` leaves the wheel's hotkey to it, and while the wheel is open, the chat's hotkey does nothing.

## Emotes (`ui_emotes.rbx`)

The emote wheel's hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:emotes`, E by default, which neither the game's `Input` nor its gamepad plugin reads) opens a ring of eight emotes around the player (`Sprite_MpEmoteWheel`): a jump, then the game's balloons (`EMOTES`: surprise, question, music, heart, anger, sweat and idea, rows 1-6 and 9 of `Graphics/System/Balloon.png`, drawn from its full frame). It opens only on a quiet map with nothing holding the player, no action wheel, no chat box and no World overview (`free?`), holds the buttons (`Capture`) and covers the action wheel (`MGQ_MpActions.cover`). The arrows go round the ring (right and down clockwise), confirm plays the picked emote on the player's character (`balloon_id`, or `jump(0, 0)`) and tells every game `emote=<place>` with the map (`play_own`), at most one every half second (`COOLDOWN_FRAMES`, ended too by a loaded save or a new game, which set the frame count back); cancel or the hotkey closes it. Another player's emote plays on their ghost while they are on the player's map (`take`), only one the wheel has.

## Edge arrows (`ui_edge_arrows.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpEdgeArrows` points at the players on the map whose ghost is off the screen: a filled triangle (`LENGTH` 14, `WIDTH` 12, `fill_triangle` row by row, since a Bitmap draws only rects, in the player's `PlayerColor` with outline copies in `Style::OUTLINE`) where the line from the player's sprite to the ghost crosses the screen's edge (`point`: the edge rect is the screen less `MARGIN` (14) on each side and `MGQ_MpUi.top_room` at the top; `axis_reach` finds the crossing), and on the side away from the edge a plate with the name (`MGQ_MpOverworld.name_tag`, cut to `NAME_WIDTH` 90) and the tiles away (`LABEL` "%s %d"; `plate_distance` keeps the plate `LABEL_GAP` clear of the triangle). It hooks `Spriteset_Map#update` and `#dispose` on its own through `MGQ_MpHooks`, with a `Pool` of `Sprite_MpEdgeArrow`s on the map's topmost viewport at `Z[:arrows]` (190, under every line and box).

- **Whom** (`targets`): party members always, everyone in a Raid World (`MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.raid?`), on the player's map with a ghost, whose tile's middle lies off the screen (`target_of`, `off_screen?`); the nearest `MAX_ARROWS` (8) by tiles (`distance_x_from`/`distance_y_from`, so a looping map counts the short way), ties by id. A player in a menu or a battle keeps their arrow, since it is them the others look for.
- **When not** (`hidden_reason`): outside a world, while a screen of the mod lies over the map (`MGQ_MpActions.covered?`: the action wheel, or any screen registered with `cover`, the emote wheel, the World overview and the chat box), and while a message shows; then every arrow fades out. Each arrow fades in and out with a `Motion` (`SLIDE_PIXELS` 10 along its angle); the pool keeps up to `LIMIT` (16) sprites, so an arrow fading out can be given to a new player, and finds one by the player's id first, so an arrow follows its player without a new fade.
- **Drawing** (`Sprite_MpEdgeArrow#show`, `draw`): the picture is drawn anew only when the name, the tiles, the color or the angle stepped to `ANGLE_STEP` (10 degrees) changed; the sprite's `ox`/`oy` put the triangle's center at the sprite's place, which `Motion#place` sets every frame.
- **Log** (`note`, `gone_reason`): an arrow that appears is logged with the player, the tiles and the color's name; one that goes with why (left the world, connection down, left the map, not in the party, on the screen, others nearer); a reason that hides them all once when it starts and ends. A frame that fails is logged once and fades every arrow out (`Pool#update`).

## PvP battles (`battles_pvp.rbx`)

Two games swap their teams' builds, then fight the same battle live (see [Live battles](#live-battles-battles_syncrbx)), each player commanding their own team; on the host, the friend's characters are rebuilt as real characters and take the friend's commands. A **mirror match** fights the player's own team the same way, played by the computer, without any network.

**Flow.** The World overview's hotkey on the map (`MGQ_MpHotkeys`, F11 by default, read with `GetAsyncKeyState` while the game window is in front (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Background.in_front?`), since the game's `Input` knows only F5 to F9 and all are taken, F8 by the game's message hiding) opens `Scene_PvpLobby` (named without "Battle", which `GameState.scene` reads as a fight). *Host* calls `mp_host` with the team, *Join with the copied code* `mp_join_clipboard`. An invite the player accepted in Discord joins by itself (`MGQ_MpBattlesPvp.join_invite`, `mp_join_invite`) as soon as the map or the screen sees it, and the map opens the screen to show the join; Discord starts a closed game for it, and the title screen then loads the newest save, the one *Continue* picks first (`DataManager.latest_savefile_index`), finished by the game's own `Scene_Load#on_load_success` (`MGQ_MpBattlesPvp.on_title`). Its `Scene_Title#update` hook must wrap the Discord mod's, which skips the methods it wraps while its invite box is open; `battles_pvp.rbx` loads after `Discord_RPC.rb`, so it does. The map and the screen poll `mp_status` every 20 frames; once it says `received`, the map reads the team through `mp_state` and starts the battle. *Fight your own team* returns to the map, which starts a mirror match: the player's own `Team.build`, read back through `Team.parse`, so it meets exactly what a friend would.

**Exchange (DLL, `Network/Pvp/`).** `Session.Current` holds the state; threads of an earlier start (a generation) change nothing.
- The host makes a **join code** (`JoinCode`): `mgqmp2;<token>;<relay>`. The token is 16 characters (about 79 bits); the relay is an id of `Relays`, the one this version hosts on (`Relays.Current`). The games never connect to each other directly, so the code holds no address. The host enters its **relay room** (`Serve`) and only then offers the code: on the clipboard (`Clipboard`) and, while hosting, in the activity as party and join secret (through the Discord bridge, see below); until then the PvP battle screen says it is reaching the relay. When the relay cannot be reached at first, hosting fails (`RelayUnreachable`). Once the code is out, the host keeps the connection open with `ping`. When the relay turns it away or was lost, the host tries again, since a friend may hold the code already: after 10 s the first time (`RelayFirstRetry`), since the relay may still hold its last connection, then every 30 s (`RelayRetry`). Hosting stops with a message (`NobodyJoined`) once nobody joined for 30 minutes (`HostWaitLimit`) or the relay closes the lone host for waiting too long (close code 4004, `RelayFrameChannel.WaitedTooLong`). The first guest swaps teams, which cuts the wait (`StopWaiting`). A team too large for a frame fails hosting and joining at once (`TeamTooLarge`). `mp_cancel` turns a waiting Discord invite down, except once a team arrived: ending a battle's link keeps an invite the player accepted during the battle.
- The guest (`Reach`) enters the room the code's token leads to and waits there at most 10 s for the host (`RelayPairTimeout`). The game says whether the relay found no host in the room (`NotHosting`), could not be reached (`RelayUnreachable`) or is unknown to this version (`UnknownRelay`). A code that starts like one of any mod version (`mgqmp`, `JoinCode.IsOfAnyVersion`) but does not read is from another version (`OtherModVersion`), which the game tells apart from no code at all; an invite with such a code waits like any other, so joining it can say so. Each side sends one **frame** of at most 256 KB (`IFrameChannel.MaxFrameBytes`; four late-game builds take about 35 KB) over the **frame channel** (`IFrameChannel`; `RelayFrameChannel` carries each frame as one binary WebSocket message through the relay). Every body is **encrypted** (`FrameCipher`): AES-GCM with a key derived from the join code's token and two salts of 16 random bytes each, in the clear (HKDF-SHA256): the guest sends its own first and the host answers with its own, so every connection has a key of its own even when a guest tries the same code again or a relay replays an earlier guest; a counter per direction ahead of each frame and in its nonce, so a frame of another token, or changed, repeated, left out or sent back, fails the check. Inside is a `Message`: `key=value` headers (`game`, `player`, or `refused`), an empty line, the team, in UTF-8. The token itself never travels: a guest's first frame that decrypts proves its join code, and the host turns any other guest away without a message and goes on hosting. The host then checks the `game` fingerprint and answers with its team. The connection then stays open as the **link** (`Link`) that a live battle will need: `kind=game` frames carry the game script's own text both ways (`mp_send`, and `mp_receive`, which the game script polls), `kind=ping` goes out after 2 s without another frame, and `kind=bye` says goodbye; the link goes on with the exchange's cipher. A reader and a writer thread of its own keep the game from ever waiting on the network; only the writer encrypts, pings included, so frames go out in the order of their counters. The link counts as `closed` after a goodbye and as `dropped` when the connection breaks or stays silent for 10 s, which the pings of a game still running never let happen; `mp_state` reports it as `link`. Cancelling or starting anew closes it, so the PvP battle without a live battle still ends it at once.
- **The relay** passes frames between the two games, which only connect out to it, so it works where a home connection cannot be reached from outside (DS-Lite, CGNAT, no IPv6, no forwarded port). The room is named after the token by HKDF with a label of its own (`Relays.RoomOf`), so the relay learns neither the token nor the frames' key and only ever sees encrypted frames. A join code names its relay by id, and `Relays` maps each id to an address; moving to another relay is a new id beside the old one, never a new address for an old id, since a guest always takes the relay the host's code names. The server, its protocol and the move to a rented machine: [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md).
- Discord invites and the profile: see [Discord](#discord-optional).

**Team (`Team`, on `MGQ_MpActors::Builds` and `MGQ_MpActors::Items` in `core_actors.rbx`).** `Team` writes and reads the whole team through `Builds`, the Frontline first, then the Backline, at most `MOST_MEMBERS`, below a first line `backline=1` or `backline=0`, the sender's *PvP Backline* option. The host's line holds for the battle (`Team.sent_backline?` on the host, `Team.backline?` of the host's team on the guest); without the Backline, `Battle.start` takes the first 4 members only. A line per member, the **build** rather than its results: `member=` then, split by `;`, the actor id; personal level; job; race; every job and race level (`id:level`); the eight stat growths from items; the skills learned; the abilities learned and the abilities set (`skill type:id.id`); the equipment per slot; the eight stats plus hit, evasion and critical in per mille as the sender sees them, for the self-check; then what of the sender's *save* the character reads: the counters `Builds::COUNTERS` (battles fought, the character's Library counts, affection) and the switches its battle start states wait for that are on. Equipment (`Items`): `w<id>`/`a<id>` for a database item, `s…` for a socket item with its gems, `e…` for an enchanted item with its rolls (rarity, upgrade, sockets, variance, enchantments, stat rolls, trait rolls, gems), which the receiver turns into its own copy and lets the game remake the traits from (`reset_data`, as on loading a save). Only numbers leave the game, never the save format, since loading someone else's Marshal data can run code. The receiver takes only ids its own data has, and logs a line or item it cannot read. `Builds.game` is the build format (`Builds::FORMAT`) plus the sizes of the databases, which must match, since ids and fields differ between versions. The format was checked by writing and reading back real Part 1 and late-game parties (socket and enchanted equipment, 1,136 skills, 182 job and race levels) outside the game.

**Rebuilt characters (`Game_MpActor` in `core_actors.rbx`, `Opponent`).** Each friend's character is a `Game_MpActor`, a `Game_Actor` subclass made for its actor id and given the build (`rebuild`), with its owner's affection, save counters and battle start switches, the extra accessory slot and enchanted equipment its owner's game allowed, and items from its owner's bag; `Opponent` builds on it for the enemy side. The build: job, race, levels (and the matching EXP), stat growth, skills, abilities, equipment, then the game's own `refresh` and `recover_all`. As a real character it has every trait of its equipment, gems, abilities, job and race: pre-battle spells and the other automatic skills, passives, and the stat boosts only a battle applies. It answers for the enemy side where the game assumes the player's party (audited: the battle scripts' references to `$game_party`, `$game_actors`, `actor?`/`enemy?` and Luka):
- allies `$game_troop`, enemies `$game_party`, its index in the troop, always a battle member;
- drawn as a sprite (`use_sprite?`), picture by `Pictures`, placed side by side at the screen's bottom;
- `luca?` false, so Luka's binding, giving up and ero offers stay with the player's Luka;
- `booster_actor_exist_param` counts its own side, where the game counts `$game_party.members`;
- the hit effect of a monster (blink) instead of shaking the screen, and on defeat a monster's flash and sound (`:whiten`) instead of its collapse: it stays on the battlefield and turns grey and see-through once that flash ends (`Sprite_Battler#update`: the game marks it `dead?` when the hit lands, before the battle log tells of the defeat, so a `:whiten` effect while dead starts the silhouette; the opacity is set every frame, since every sprite effect makes it opaque again when it starts), since its team can revive it. It stays dead to the game, so every target choice (`Game_Unit#random_target`, the enemy window, all-enemy scopes) skips it as it skips dead monsters;
- the monster data the battle's code reads of the enemy side (`enemy`, `enemy_id`, `friend`, `lose_event_id`, `escape_level`, `escape_not_count?`, `steal_list`, `boss?`, `hide_name`, `lefx`), with no rewards or recruiting; any other method only monsters have is answered by the monster stand-in (`method_missing`) and logged once, so a call the audit missed does not end the game mid-battle;
- the extra accessory slot when the sender had it (6 slots sent), where the game reads a switch of the receiver's save;
- enchanted equipment even while the receiver's save has enchanting off (`NWConst::Sw::ENCHANT_OFF`, which `equippable?` reads), since the game's `refresh` would take it off: `equippable?` turns the switch off for the check in the switches' own entries (`MGQ_MpGame` `:data`), since the game's setter takes every enchanted item off the player's party when it turns on;
- the sender's save where the game reads the receiver's for a character (fields 12 and 13): the damage boosts of `booster_ex_count` (battles fought in the save, the character's Library counts of carries, defeated enemies, times down, orgasms and steals, its affection), `love` itself, which a won battle never raises in the receiver's save (`love=` does nothing), and the battle start states that wait for a switch (`auto_state_with_switch`), which `on_battle_start` adds or removes to match the switches that were on in the sender's save. A mirror match cannot show any of these, since both sides read the same save;
- actions from the game's own actor auto-battle (`make_auto_battle_actions`), which targets its `opponents_unit` and checks what is usable;
- no action without a target: skills aimed at one sex only, or at Luka only while they bind (`ext_scope`), find none in a team without one, which the game never had to handle since only monsters use them on the party, and the battle log then names a target that is not there (`replace_ext_character` fails on `nil`). A `Scene_Battle#use_item` hook shows the game's own "is watching the situation" line instead (`Battle.targetless?`), for both teams;
- the conditions of its automatic skills (pre-battle spells, counters, turn start and end) from its own side (`firing_auto_skills` through a `BattleManager._auto_skill_per` hook): the game checks `$game_party` for "an ally of these ids" and "an ally has this state". A mirror match cannot show this, since both teams are the same. Which automatic skill fires is random (`rand < per`, and a `sample` among skills of the same priority), so a mirror match's pre-battle states, and the element rates they change, differ between the two sides.

What stays as the player's side is harmless in a PvP battle or undone by the snapshot: gold and items spent on skills, Library counters, the local actor of the same id that `Game_Actor#refresh` touches. `Battle.check` logs where a rebuilt character's stats or rates differ from the sender's; both measure outside of battle. A mirror match also writes `Patch/Multiplayer/Mirror Match.log` (`MirrorReport`): each character next to its rebuild, once outside of battle and once when the first turn starts (a `BattleManager.turn_start` hook), after pre-battle spells and battle-only boosts: the 8 stats, 10 extra and 10 special rates, max SP, levels, job and race, element rates and states that differ from normal, states resisted, states on the character, skills, set abilities and equipment with gems, every differing value marked.

**Battle (`Opponents`, `Battle`).** An empty troop is appended to `$data_troops` for the battle only; after `BattleManager.setup`, the rebuilt characters are put into `$game_troop`'s `@enemies` and the escape ratio is made again. Picture: the full picture the Library shows (`NWConst::Library::ACTOR_IMAGE`, 640 × 480 in Graphics/Pictures), at full size, cut to the box around its visible pixels (sampled every 4th pixel, reading them all is too slow) down to the picture's bottom edge, which stands at the screen's bottom like the game's own full-size monsters; the face, enlarged, when there is none. The Library's pictures are the same files as the monsters' battle pictures and the largest the game has; shrunk, they turn jagged, since RGSS scales without smoothing. Drawn by `Pictures.stand_in_for` through `Sprite_Battler#update_bitmap`; their HP bars through `Sprite_Battler#update`, since the HP bar plugin only draws for `Game_Enemy`. The battle runs in the game's own battle replay mode (`$game_temp.in_memory_battle`: no EXP, gold, drops, recruiting or autosave) with *can lose* (defeat revives and returns to the map); `Game_Troop`'s EXP, gold and drop totals are 0 for other mods such as a victory screen. It also turns on the rules every multiplayer battle shares (`MGQ_MpBattles.begin(:pvp)`): the switch that removes the battle's *Party* command (`NWConst::Sw::FORBID_BATTLE_SHIFT_CHANGE`, 27) unless the battle has the Backline (`MGQ_MpBattles.begin(:pvp, backline)`), and the switch *No Seduction* (86), which ends common event 6 before it runs a monster's **ero offer** (the low-HP offer of an H-scene for giving up, common event 2000 + enemy id) or conversation. Before it, `Battle.start` Marshals the save contents plus `$game_library`, `$game_system_switches` and `$game_global_system`; the `Scene_Map#start` hook extracts them again and removes the troop, the way the game's own Retry restores a battle. This also undoes whatever rebuilding the characters touched.

**Nothing reaches the disk.** The game writes the system save (Library, system switches, affection) at every scene change (`Scene_Base#pre_terminate` → `SaveSystemData.auto_save`) and when it closes, so the battle scene's end would write the battle's changes before the map puts the snapshot back. While a PvP battle runs, hooks on `DataManager.save_system` (`Battle.as_before_for_system`) and on `save_game_without_rescue`, `auto_save_game_without_rescue` and `save_game_backup_without_rescue` (`Battle.as_before_for_save`) swap in the snapshot for the write and swap back after it. A reset (F12) during a battle goes to the title screen, which makes the save's objects anew but keeps the shared ones; `Battle.forget` puts those back and calls `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.finish`, which closes a live battle's link, so the friend's game wins instead of waiting. The restore also drops the notices of medals earned in the battle (`$game_temp`'s `@gain_medals`, the medals themselves are in the Library) and the game's Retry data of the battle (`BattleManager`'s `@retry_data`). The feature caches (`CacheActorFeatures`, `CacheUniq`) are keyed by object, not by actor id, so a rebuilt character never shares an entry with the player's character of the same id.

## Backline (`battles_pvp_backline.rbx`)

A PvP battle between two players has the Backline when its host's *PvP Backline* option says so (`Backline.wanted?`, `$game_system.conf[:mp_pvp_backline]`, on by default); a duel's challenger hosts, and tells the option in their state (`challenge_backline`), which the challenge's lines show. Team duels never have it. `Battle.start` then rebuilds every member of the other team: the first 4 stand in the troop, the others wait in `Backline.reserve`, and the balance fills the HP of both Backlines too.

- **The player's own swap** is the game's own shift change (`Scene_Battle#bench_member_ok`, `Game_Party#swap_order`), so it stands at once on the player's screen. `Backline.round_front` reads the Frontline once per round, keyed by the troop's turn count, which only changes as a turn starts; a character standing in a place another held then was swapped in (`swapped_in?`), gives no commands (`Game_Actor#inputable?`) and has no actions (`Game_Actor#make_actions`, which the game runs anew after a shift change). Swapped back within the same command phase, it costs nothing.
- **Telling the other game** (`Backline::Mode`): a guest's commands carry its Frontline's actor ids (`own_order`), which the host puts in its troop before the commands (`take_order`, `take_front`). Once the commands came, the host takes away any actions of the characters swapped in and posts its own Frontline as `pvp_front` after a flush, before the turn's events (`share_order`, `settle_round`); the guest takes it in the stream's order (`stream_kinds`, `take`). So both games reorder between the same two sends, and the stream's places (`a0`, `e0`) mean the same characters on both. `take_front` leaves out a Frontline that names a character the team lacks, names one twice or has another size, and draws the troop's sprites anew (`Spriteset_Battle#dispose_enemies`, `create_enemies`). A sprite made for a dead character starts as the silhouette (`Sprite_Battler#update` in `battles_pvp.rbx`).
- **Targets** are places: the game finds a single target by its index when the action runs, so an attack hits whoever stands there by then.
- **Outside both Frontlines**: the stream names the host's own Backline `b<actor id>` and the other side's `r<actor id>` (`Mode.reserve_ref`, `Mode.reserve`), and records their values and names with the battlers' (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.party_side`, `troop_side`), since skills that reach the Backline change them. `Game_Troop#item_target_members` gives such skills the other side's reserve, as `Game_Party`'s gives the player's.
- **Defeat** is the game's: a side loses once its whole Frontline is down, whoever waits behind it.

## Duels (`battles_duel.rbx`)

A duel is `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp`'s PvP battle between two players of a world, carried over the world's room instead of the PvP link.

- **Challenge** (`invite`, `stop`): like a party invite, for 15 s (`INVITE_FRAMES`), to the players nearby, and to a player anywhere named by the World overview. The state tells `challenge=1` and `challenge_to` (ids); never a field `duel`, which marks the duel's messages (`MGQ_MpOverworldSync.route("duel")`) and would turn every state into one. Anyone may duel, in a party or not. The line above the challenger's ghost says "Challenges you to a duel (B)" to the players it reaches (`challenged_by?`), and the challenger's own line "Challenging to a duel (15 s) . . ." (`own_doing`, with the invite's `frames_left`; also "Waiting for \<name\>'s duel" after accepting, "Getting the sides ready" for a team duel's challenger and "Ready for the duel" for a called player, each with its seconds).
- **Accept** (`accept`): only while free on the map (`free?`: no event, message, transfer, menu or other multiplayer battle). It stops a challenge of the player's own, since they cannot take two duels, sends `duel=accept` to the challenger with the game's fingerprint and team (`Team.game`, `Team.build`) and waits 6 s (`ANSWER_FRAMES`) for the answer.
- **Start** (`take_accept`, `take_start`, `on_map`): the challenger, still challenging and free, answers `duel=start` with a battle id and its own team, and hosts; both games then call `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.join_world(role, id, [seat], name, :pvp)` and `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Battle.start` on the map's next frame. Whichever side cannot start any more (busy, a `start` it no longer expects, an unreadable team, or an event that came first on the map) calls the duel off (`call_off`, `give_up`): `duel=cancel` with the id drops the other side's duel still waiting to start (`take_cancel`), and `battle=broken` ends a battle already waiting.
- **Decline** (`decline`, `take_decline`): `duel=decline` with a reason (`REASONS`: busy, stopped challenging, another version of the game, an unreadable team), shown as a notice.
- **Over the world's room**: `MGQ_MpBattlesSync` already speaks it for co-op battles; in PvP mode the seats are the other player's, a player who leaves the world ends the battle (`world_gone?`), and `finish` leaves the PvP link alone. Discord and the ghosts need nothing new: `Battle.running?` feeds `pvp_battle_with`, and the state's scene is `battle`.

## Team duels (`battles_team.rbx`)

A duel becomes a team duel when either player leads a party of two or more, unless both play in the same party (`MGQ_MpBattlesDuel.team_duel?`). Each side is that party, or the single player; party members who do not lead still duel one against one.

- **Gathering** (in `battles_duel.rbx`): on the accept, the challenger sends `duel=team` to its own party and to the player who accepted (`gather`). A call counts only from the challenger the player accepted or from the leader of the player's party (`take_call`). The player who accepted, leading a party, passes the call on through the party's gate (`duel_call`, `call_party`, `take_passed_call`) and tells the challenger whom (`duel=side`, `take_side`), who then count on the other side. Each answers `duel=ready` with their game's fingerprint, their Frontline (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.team_build`, the places the duel counts) and their `party_member_max` once free on the map (`tick_called`). After everyone answered, or 10 s (`READY_FRAMES`), the challenger arranges both sides as a co-op battle's party (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.arrange`: the leader first, each player's share of the Frontline, a single player's whole Frontline) and sends `duel=start` with `team=1` and both sides to those ready, `duel=cancel` (`late`) to the others; without anyone of the other side ready the duel is off. A player busy by then sends `battle=leave`, which counts them as having left.
- **The battle**: `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.join_world(..., :pvp, true)` (`team?`). Every game has its own side as its party, through `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.form` (`MGQ_MpBattlesTeam.form`), and the other side as its troop, rebuilt as `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Opponent`s that carry their owner's seat (`opponents`, through `Battle.start`'s block). The challenger hosts. A guest on the host's side has the host's party and troop (`same_side?`, like a co-op guest); one on the other side has them swapped (like a one-against-one guest). The host takes commands from every guest (`several?`), each for their own characters (`Commands.commanded_by?`): a guest of its side in its party, a guest of the other side in its troop, by the same places. The Backline stays out of a team duel, whatever the players' options say.
- **Leaving**: a guest who escapes leaves the duel (`leave`) and returns to the map; their characters stay. At the next command phase the host hands them to the first player of their side still in it (`settle`, `heir_of`), told as `team_heirs`, so that player commands them (`Game_MpAlly#inputable?`). The host wins once the other side is empty; a host who leaves or gives up ends the duel: lost for its side, won for the other (`guest_end`, `end_early`).

## Trades (`trade.rbx`)

A trade moves items and gold between two players on the same map. Key items stay out (`Items.tradeable?`: `key_item?`, enchant stones excepted); only the bag counts, not equipment or the storehouse.

- **Offer and accept**: like a duel challenge (`MGQ_MpCoop::Invite`), told as `trading=1` and `trading_to`, never a field `trade`, which marks the trade's messages. It reaches only players on the same map (`same_map?`). The player who accepts stops an offer of their own and sends `trade=accept`; the one who offered, still offering and free (`free?`), answers `trade=open` with a 24-character id, and both games open `Scene_MpTrade` from the map (`on_map`). The line above the player's head counts the offer down ("Offering a trade (15 s)") and the wait for the trade to open ("Waiting for \<name\>'s trade", `ANSWER_FRAMES`), through `own_doing`. The trade screen's cancel asks first through `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm` (`Scene_MpTrade#on_cancel_trade`, `CANCEL_QUESTION`, `CANCEL_CHOICES`): yes cancels, no activates the commands again, unless the trade ended under the box, whose windows are disposed by then.
- **Offers**: each change sends `trade=offer` with the offer's revision and the offer as text (`Items.write_offer`: `g<gold>` and `<token>*<amount>` separated by semicolons). A token is `i`, `w` or `a` and the id, or `u`, an enchanted or socketed copy as `MGQ_MpActors::Items` writes it, and its name prefix in hexadecimal after `~`. Every change takes both confirmations back.
- **Confirm**: `trade=confirm` names the revisions of both offers (`mine`, `yours`). A game whose own confirmation and the other's name the offers as they stand (`Session#agreed?`) hands both offers to the relay (`commit`): `mp_trade_commit` with an id of the trade's id and both revisions ordered by player id (`relay_id`), so both games name it alike and a later confirmation is a trade of its own, and the text `<id>=<offer>|<id>=<offer>` ordered by player id (`agreed_text`). The relay commits once both games sent the same text.
- **Commit**: while the relay decides (`:committing`) nothing changes. `committed` applies the trade (`Items.apply`, inside `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.granting`, so a leader's story does not pass it on), notes the relay's id in the save (`$game_system`'s `@mgq_mp_trades`, the last 50), saves (`Saving`: the slot last loaded or saved in the world, else the first free one) and sends `mp_trade_done`. `cancelled` opens the trade again unconfirmed; an offer or unconfirm from the other player cancels a commit the relay has not decided.
- **Recovery** (`Recovery`): once per save loaded in a world, on the map, `mp_trade_pending` lists the committed trades the player has not marked done. One the save lacks is applied from its text and saved; each is then marked done.

## World overview (`world_overview.rbx`)
