A box over the map (`Sprite_MpWorldOverview`), not a scene, so the world runs on behind it; it holds the buttons (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Capture`) while open. Its hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys`, F11 by default, which opens the PvP battle screen outside a world) or the wheel's middle opens it; its hotkey, cancel, the wheel's hotkey, a click outside the box, leaving the map, an event, a message or the chat box close it.

- **Pages** (`PAGES`, `page`, `turn_to`, `turn`, `turn_by_keys`): *Players* and *World*, switched with left and right (`Capture.repeat?`) or a click on the tabs drawn under the title (`Sprite_MpWorldOverview.tab_at`, `TAB_WIDTH`, the style's `GAP` between them); turning closes a player's menu. While the World page shows and a player's call waits on the Players page (`view[:calls]`), the Players tab carries a gold mark (`CALL_MARK`), since the notification box hides behind the overview. The title (`draw_title`) is the world's name with its type and a Raid World's difficulty after it (`title_tail`: " | Raid | Hard", `WORLD_TYPES`, `difficulty_name` from `MGQ_MpWorldDifficulty.value`), cut before the party's size on the right. `update` returns at its top while `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm.open?`; `page_lines` and `page_view` build the page's lines and view, `HINTS` the hint per page. The World page (`world_lines`, `update_world`, `mouse_world`; up and down scroll `@world_scroll`) lists `:info` lines (`WORLD_LABELS`: the name, the type, the difficulty or `PER_PLAYER`, the players online of the seats, `PLAYERS_OF`) and in a Raid World the story (`world_story_text`: the world's part from `MGQ_MpWorldStory.world` and the route by its counter's variable, `STORY_UNTOLD` before one is told) and its raid bosses under a `:place` heading per section of the story (`BOSSES_HEADING`), a `:boss` line per boss (`boss_sections`: the sections of `MGQ_MpRaidBosses.sections` in story order, each boss once in the order the story meets it, a pool of a fight with its own name, such as Sisel's, after its section's bosses; only the sections the world's story reached, `reached?` by `SECTION_REACH` from `MGQ_MpWorldStory.part_of` and the routes in `done`, and those holding a boss the relay told a pool of; the pools whose bosses the tables lack last under `OTHER_SECTION`; `tier_of` and `section_of` find a pool's tier and section by name, else by `MGQ_MpRaidPool.troop_of_key`; `boss_of` averages the share left of a boss's pools from `MGQ_MpRaidPool.pools` and `left_now`, `UNTOUCHED` for one never reported, `defeated` once all are, and `by` of the pool hurt last). `draw_boss` draws the name (dim once defeated), `TIER`, a thin gauge (`GAUGE_WIDTH` 90, `Style::BAD` on `Style::PLATE`), the share (`share_text`, rounded, at least 1% while anything is left) or `DEFEATED` in red, and `LAST_HURT_BY`. The overview reads the pools through `MGQ_MpRaidPool.pools` (a copy) and nothing else there.
- **What every state tells** (`state_fields`): `place` (the map's shown name, else its name in the editor), `lv` (the highest base level of the companions in the party and waiting, `stand_members`, Luka left out) and `progress` (`part`, `part:side` or `part:route`, such as `2:alice` or `3:destroyer`). Level and story are read every 3 s (`READ_FRAMES`). The story is read by name in `$data_system`, the translation's or the untranslated game's (`PROGRESS`, `SIDES`, `ROUTES`): Part 2 from progress 20, Part 3 from 40; the route only in Part 3, else the side. In a party the members borrow the leader's story, so they show the leader's. In a Raid World it also tells `flv`, the highest base level of the player's share of the Frontline, Luka included (`front_level`: `battle_members` cut to `MGQ_MpCoopSquad.own_share`'s Frontline share, else the first character); a Classic world never sends it.
- **The list** (`rows`, `lines`, `own_row`, `row_of`): every player online and the player, grouped by place (the player's first, then by name), each group by name with the player first. A row shows what they do (the ghost's icon), a crown for a party's leader, the name in the player's color (`PlayerColor.of(row.player, row.member)`, cut to its column), the party's size, "Lv n", the place in the story ("Part 2: Alice side") and the ping. A player whose party invite or duel challenge reaches the player shows it in place of their story (`call_of`). The title shows the world's name and the player's party ("Party 2 / 4").
- **The menu** (`menu_options`): for another player, accept their party invite (also one that names the player from afar, `Party.invited_by?`), invite them wherever they are (`Party.invite(id)`, told as `invite_to`), or remove them from the party as its leader (`Party.remove`, after the confirm box's "Remove \<name\> from the party?", `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm.ask`, `REMOVE_QUESTION`, `REMOVE_CHOICES`), and accept their challenge or challenge them (`MGQ_MpBattlesDuel`); on the player's own row, leave the party (after "Leave the party?", `LEAVE_QUESTION`; a party that dissolved while the box asked is not left, `party_options(asking)`; the wheel's *Leave the party* asks nothing, since picking a side and confirming is a deliberate pair already) and stop inviting or challenging. The arrows and confirm, or the mouse, pick and take; choices that cannot be taken are grey and say why.
- **The mouse** (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Mouse` in `Multiplayer.rb`): `GetCursorPos`, `ScreenToClient` on the window in front and `GetClientRect` scale the pointer to the game's screen while the game is in front; the left button is read like F11. Pointing picks a row or a choice once the mouse moved, a click opens or takes it.
- **The party box** (`Sprite_MpPartyBox`, `party_rows`): while the player is in a party of two or more and the overview is closed, a box in the top right corner of the map lists the party: the leader first with a crown, then by name, each with the icon of what they do (`row.icon`; `AWAY_ICON` and `Style::DIM` while their connection is down, `away?`), the name in its color (`color_of`: `PlayerColor.of(row.player, row.member)`, the player's own yellow, a member green; cut to the name column with `MGQ_MpUi.cut`), "Lv n" and the ping, each in a fixed column (`COLUMNS`, `LEVEL_WIDTH` fits "Lv 100", `MGQ_MpOverworld::PING_WIDTH` (64) the widest ping "999+ ms", drawn by `MGQ_MpOverworld.draw_ping` as in the overview, `WIDTH` is their sum, so level and ping never meet), and their place on a smaller line below (`draw_place`). The log names each listed player with whether they lead and their color (`color_label`). `fit_place` shortens a place to the box: the Pocket Castle's short name (`SHORT_PLACES`), then without its part in brackets, then cut with "..." before a floor, layer or area (`PLACE_FLOOR`) it keeps; the game's names run up to 45 letters. It builds rows for the party only, since it redraws every frame it changed. Its hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:party_box`, Tab by default, which the game does not read) makes it small and full again on the map, in a party, with no screen of the mod over the map (`MGQ_MpPartyBox.on_map`, `toggle`); the choice is kept in Player.ini (`party_box_small`), so it holds after a restart and in every world. Small, it lists a row per player with the crown, the icon, the name and the ping, without title, level or place, `SMALL_WIDTH` (198) wide (`draw_small`, through the same `draw_line`).
- **The map box** (`draw_map`, `map_rows`): in a Raid World (`MGQ_MpPartyBox.map_box?`, `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.raid?`) the party box lists everyone on the player's map (`Scope.peers_here`) while someone else is on it: the party's members first and green, its leader first with the only crown, then the other players by name in their `PlayerColor`, each with their icon, "Lv n" from their `flv` and the ping, and no place line. The title says "Map n", the number of players on the map. The box grows with its players up to `MAP_LINES` (10) lines; past them it lists 9 and a line "+n more" (`capped`, through `MGQ_MpUi.capped` and `MORE_TEXT`, keeping the player's own line), small too. Its hotkey works while someone else is on the map.

## Party size and leader

`MGQ_MpCoop.size_of(id)` counts a party's players from the states, `leader_of(id)` finds its leader (the player the party's id names, else the lowest id, as `Party.leader`; a player who just joined counts only once the leader's list names them, `Party.admitted_me?`, so the leader turning them away reaches them) and `leads?` tells whether a player leads their party of two or more. Every party script asks `MGQ_MpCoop.party_leader` and `party_leading?` for the leader of the player's own party of two or more. Any party can be shown, not only the player's own: a ghost's label shows "2 / 4" after the name and a crown (`MGQ_MpOverworld::CROWN_ICON`, 226, the game's King's Proof) before it; the player's own line above their head (`Sprite_MpOwnPing`) shows the same next to their ping. A party holds at most four players (`Party::MAX_PLAYERS`).

## Co-op battles (`battles_coop.rbx`)

When a party member's game sets up a battle (`BattleManager.setup`, which random encounters, event battles and story bosses all go through), the party members on the same map who are walking it, reading an event's messages, or on it with their window in the background, which keeps running (their state's `scene` is `map`, `event` or `away`), are invited to it. When nobody is, the in-game log says what each party member does. The party's leader is the **host** and computes the battle, so the leader's game and mods decide it; the others are **guests** and play it back through `battles_sync.rbx`, now over the world's room. When the leader is not among the members invited, the game that set the battle up hosts it.

- **Leader leads** (`offer`, `ask_leader`, `await_leader`; `serve_request`, `lead`, `refuse_lead`): a member whose game sets up a battle while the leader is invited sends the leader `coop=lead` with a battle id it chose, the troop, escape, lose and the map, and becomes a guest of that battle (`join_world(:guest, ...)`). Its battle start (`Live` in `battles_sync.rbx`) waits up to 6 s (`LEADER_FRAMES`, "Asking <leader> to lead the battle...") for the leader's `coop=invite` with that id. The leader, on the member's map, no other battle running and free within 3 s (`ACCEPT_FRAMES`), sets up the same troop and hosts it with the member's id, inviting the member though their state says `battle`; otherwise it answers `coop=no_lead`. A refusal, a leader gone or a silent one leaves the battle to the member, who hosts it (`host`) and drops a late invite for the id it asked with (`@dropped_bid`). A leader whose member did not join calls the battle off for everyone (`call_off`, `broken`), since the encounter was the member's.
- **Stretched encounter steps** (`party_encounter_steps`, after `Game_Player#make_encounter_count`, installed at run time): in a Classic party the steps the game drew are multiplied by the party members on the player's map, the player included, and by a random factor of 1 ± `ENCOUNTER_MARGIN` (0.5), wider than the game's own margin, so the party as a whole meets encounters about as often as one player would and the members' steps never fall into step; in a Raid World the players on the map whose connection stands, up to `RAID_PLAYERS`, each add `RAID_ENCOUNTER_SHARE` (0.5) of a draw instead (`encounter_players`); a player alone on their map keeps the game's draw.
- **Held encounters** (`encounter`, `hold`, `tick_hold`, `take_soon`): a random encounter (`Game_Player#encounter`) whose battle the game just set up waits up to 3 s (`HOLD_FRAMES`) while party members on the map are busy (`busy_members`: any scene but `map`, `event`, `away` and `battle`, such as a menu, typing or a vehicle). `offer` holds it instead of inviting, `encounter` then returns false, the player stands still (`Game_Player#movable?`) and opens no menu (`Scene_Map#update_call_menu`), and each busy member is told `coop=soon`, which shows for a moment as a message in their notification box (`MGQ_MpNotices.message`). Once every one of them is back on the map, or the time ran out, `tick_hold` offers the battle as usual and calls the battle scene; an event that started meanwhile drops it, and so does the title screen (`drop_hold`).
- **The party stands still** (`freeze_party`, `take_freeze`, `frozen?`): whenever a member's encounter is held, hosted or asked of the leader, every party member is told `coop=freeze` with the map. A member on that map stands still and opens no menu (through `hold_player`) until they join that battle (`accept`), turn it down (`decline`), set up a battle of their own (`offer`) or 10 s pass (`FREEZE_SECONDS`, by the clock, since a member in a menu counts no map frames), and reads "Enemies! <Member> is in a battle." in the notification box for as long as the hold (`FREEZE_FRAMES`, keyed by `soon_key(seat)`, which the `coop=soon` notice shares); every way the hold ends early goes through `thaw(reason)`, which drops those notices with the reason. Before, only the member who met the enemies and the leader stood still while the others could walk off before their invite came.
- **Invite** (`host`): the host makes the battle a live co-op battle (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.join_world(:host, ...)`), turns on the multiplayer rules (`MGQ_MpBattles.begin(:coop)`) and tells the party `coop=invite` with the battle's id, the troop, escape and lose, the invited seats and the map. Not while another multiplayer battle, a Library replay or no party is there.
- **Accept** (`take`, `on_map`): an invited member on the host's map joins once free on the map (no event or transfer of their own), within 3 s (`ACCEPT_FRAMES`, counted by the clock in `expired?`, since an invite that came outside the map waits for it), else turns it down (`battle=decline`). A member in a battle of their own turns it down at once. Joining sets up the same troop (without inviting anyone itself), becomes a guest (`join_world(:guest, ...)`) and calls the battle scene from the map, which plays the game's own battle music and transition. The leader's story dialogue on screen is cleared.
- **Gather** (`gather`, at the host's battle start): the host waits up to 6 s (`JOIN_FRAMES`) for each invited member's `join` (`own_build`: the builds and HP and MP of as many characters as their squad holds with two players, the most any battle needs, and their `party_member_max`) or decline. Without anyone, the battle is the host's own (`stand_down`). Otherwise it sends `roster`: the troop (`troop_entries`: each enemy's id, screen x and y, and whether hidden) and every player (`arrange`): seat, name, builds, HP and MP, `party_member_max`, the order of their places, and their shares of the Frontline and the Backline (`MGQ_MpCoopSquad.share`), the party's leader first, then by player id. A guest (`join`) sends its `join` at its battle start and waits for the `roster`. A guest whose own troop differs, as through a mod of either game, fights the host's instead (`take_troop`): it rebuilds the enemies as `Game_Troop#setup` does, names them apart again (`make_unique_names` with `@names_count` emptied) and draws them anew (`Spriteset_Battle#dispose_enemies`, `create_enemies`), since the host names every enemy by its place. It pays out its own rewards for them. Only a host's enemy missing from this game's database keeps the guest's own troop.
- **The party** (`form`): every game builds the same party in the roster's order, each player's share of the Frontline (`Player#lines`; a player is a `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop::Player` record, which the messages carry as its fields in order). A player's own characters are their real ones; everyone else's are `Game_MpAlly`, a `Game_MpActor` on the party side with its owner's seat (`mp_seat`), HP and MP, which this game's player never commands (`inputable?`) and for which the host's computer chooses nothing while it waits for its owner's commands (`make_actions`, `awaits_commands?`). The party is named after the player's own characters (`Game_Party#name`, `party_name`), as when it runs away or is defeated, since its first character may be another player's. `Game_Party#battle_members` returns this party while the battle runs (hooked once the first scene starts, since plugins may define it anew), and `bench_members` the player's own share of the Backline (`own_bench`); `all_members` stays the player's own, so EXP goes to the player's own characters. Others' characters are rebuilt once per battle (`ally`), so one swapped out and back keeps what the battle did to it.
- **Swaps** (`swap`, `take_order`, `share_order`): the battle's *Party* command works, since `MGQ_MpBattles` forbids it only in PvP. In its shift change a player may pick only their own characters (`Window_BattleStatus#current_item_enabled?`), and `Scene_Battle#bench_member_ok` swaps within their squad's order, in their own party too, as the game's shift change does. A guest's commands carry their order, which the host takes before the commands (`Commands.apply`); once every guest's commands came, the host sends `coop_party` when any order changed, before the turn's events. A swap only changes who stands in a player's own places, so the others' commands still mean the places they chose.
- **Leaving** (`settle`, `reform`, `go_solo`, `take_over`): whoever gets away leaves the battle. At the next command phase, before it is recorded, the host takes the players who left out: the others fight on with the shares of the smaller party (`arrange` again), the roster carrying everyone's squad for two players for that; the host sends what it recorded so far, then `coop_party` with the players who stay, which a guest's playback takes between two sends (`Channel.take_first`, `Playback.next_event`), so every battler reference after it means the new party. Characters who stay keep what the battle did to them (`mp_seat`, `mp_place`). A host left alone, and a guest whose host got away or is gone, fight on as a battle of their own (`stand_alone`): their own full team, the game's own settings, no live battle; a guest starts a new command phase from the host's last values. Every player still in the battle hears of it at once (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Departures`): a `leave` goes to the whole room, so each game tells "<name> left the battle." in the chat log (`MGQ_MpChat.system`), which shows in battle, and in the status line as it arrives, mid-turn or during command input; a battle player who leaves the world is told too (`on_leave`), each once per battle. A player who joins the running battle is told the same way, "<name> joined the battle." (`Departures.joined`, once per battle and player apart from their leaving): on the host as it takes them in (`MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.take_in`, `tell_joined`), on a guest as the host's `coop_party` names seats new to the party (`reform`).
- **Status names** (`install_status_names`, with `install_shift_change`, since plugins define `Window_BattleStatus` anew; `draw_status_name`, an `around` of `Window_BattleStatus#draw_actor_name`): while a co-op battle runs, the player's own characters are drawn in `Style::OWN` and a `Game_MpAlly` by its `base_name`, without its owner, in its owner's `PlayerColor` (the peer at its `mp_seat`, else its `owner` by name), cut to the width the game passes (100). A character below a quarter of its max HP is left to the game, which colors it (`hp_color`, which covers 0); so is every character outside a co-op battle, and a name the mod cannot draw, logged once. The battle's messages keep the full `name`.
- **Troop changes** (`share_troop`, `note_troop`): the host notes the troop its guests know (each enemy, what it is and whether it is hidden; an enemy the game hid as it was eaten counts as fallen, `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.mark`, so the guests keep it among the defeated that pay out) with the roster, once a battle fought alone turns live (`go_live`) and whenever it tells them. Whenever its troop differs from that note, such as enemies another mod added mid-battle, an encounter taken in or an enemy that transformed, appeared or hid, it sends the guests still in the battle `coop_troop` with the troop and the names, once per change: right before each send of its stream (`Mode.before_send`, which `Recorder.flush` asks before it posts, a no-op in other modes), so a guest always knows an enemy before the stream names it, and as a command phase opens (`Mode.settle`). Hotjoining's own sends go the same way. A guest transforms, shows or hides its enemies as the host's first ones (`match_enemies`, each keeping its HP and states, their letters as the game's Enemy Transform and Enemy Appear give them) and adds the new ones after them; when the host's troop is shorter or holds enemies this game lacks, it takes the troop whole. Never in a PvP battle or a duel, whose troop is the other side's characters.
- **End** (`restore`, after `BattleManager.battle_end`; `ended`, after `Scene_Battle#terminate`): what the battle lent the player's game that a save keeps, the host's enemy rates and the level sync's stats, goes back as `battle_end` ends (`restore`), since the game's victory saves its autosave right after it and before the scene terminates. The terminate hook then does the scene's cleanup: the player's own party again, the game's own settings back (`MGQ_MpBattles.finish`) and the live battle over, the world's room left open.
- **In a Raid World** (`raid?`, from `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.raid?`) a battle takes the players on the map, party or not; Classic worlds keep everything above.
  - **Who joins** (`raid_candidates`): the other players on the battle's map (`Scope.peers_on`) whose state's `scene` is `map` or `away` (`RAID_FREE_SCENES`: walking it, or on it with their window in the background, which keeps running, as a Classic world invites them; not `event`) and who are not busy (`raid_free?`: their state's `busy`, 1 while they trade or are about to teleport, `state_fields`, sent only in a Raid World), and for a boss battle (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.battle?`, `invitees(troop_id)`) also those whose `scene` is `event` because they watch the story the player tells (`watching?`: among `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.viewers` and not busy), so the teller's watchers fight its boss with it; the party's members first, then by the steps (diagonals counted as one) from the party leader's tile when the leader is on the map, else from the player's own (`raid_center`, `distance`; positions from the states, not the ghosts), at most 3 with the player (`RAID_PLAYERS`). Outside a party too (`host_possible?`).
  - **The player who starts it hosts it** (`offer`): no `ask_leader`, a request to lead is refused at once (`take_request`), and the host comes first in the battle's order (`arrange`). The invite and the hold (`freeze_party`) go to the players taken alone, through the map's gate (`tell_map`, `map_coop=<kind>`), which only a Raid World takes (`take_map`; a Classic world keeps the party's gate); a busy party member is still waited for (`hold`, `coop=soon`), and the players the hold froze whom the battle then leaves out get `coop=off` (`release`). Of two invites waiting for a player, the one whose battle hosts first (`hosts_first?`: an event's battle, else the host who entered the map first) stays and the other is turned down (`keeps_waiting_invite?`). The invite tells `enc`, 1 for a random encounter, else 0. A battle that begins while another player's invite waits for its player also invites that player, whose own battle gathers and so is no candidate (`with_waiting_host`), so of two battles one yields. The host waits 10 s for them to join (`RAID_JOIN_FRAMES`, `join_frames`), a guest 4 s more for the roster (`roster_frames`). A guest who trades turns the invite down (`on_map`). No level sync (`MGQ_MpCoopLevelSync.level_for` is nil).
  - **Two battles started at the same moment** (`rival?`, `keep_rival`, `yield_to_rival`): a player whose random encounter (`offer`'s `encounter`, also a held one) still gathers its players and who is invited to another battle on the same map, of at most 4 players, that is an event's (`enc=0`) or whose host entered the map first (`Scope.key_of`, the lower `[since, id]`), keeps that invite instead of turning it down (`yields_to?`). Its gathering ends, it tells the players it invited `coop=off` (`take_off`: a waiting invite is forgotten, a guest waiting for the roster follows or breaks off, and nobody stands still for it any more unless another host froze them or their invite waits, `unfreeze_for`), takes the rival's troop (`adopt_troop`: `Game_Troop#setup`, the escape ratio, escape and lose, no first strike, its enemies drawn anew) and becomes the rival's guest: `Mode.host_start` returns `:guest`, and `Live.host_start` starts the battle as a guest. A rival's invite that waited as the player's own battle began counts too (`answer_waiting_invite`; the player then hosts even with nobody to invite, `waiting_rival?`), any other waiting invite is turned down then; of two rivals the one that hosts second is turned down (`keep_rival`), and a rival whose `coop=off` comes is forgotten. The rival turns the other's invite down as usual. An event's battle never yields, since its event goes on by the battle's result. A guest waiting for the roster of a random encounter's battle (`@awaited`) keeps an invite to a battle it would yield to instead of turning it down (`follows?`); when its host's `coop=off` comes it takes that battle's troop and joins it (`follow`, `join` sends its join anew), and when its own roster comes it turns that invite down.
  - **Hotjoining** (`battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`, `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin`): every game tells the battle it fights in its state, `rb` (the battle's id) and `rbh` (its host's seat), also a battle of its own (`fight_alone`: nobody to invite, nobody joined, or left alone), both empty outside a battle (`state_fields`, sent only in a Raid World), and told, with the state's `scene` `battle` (`busy_scene`), also while the battle's scene waits on the scene stack because its party command called the Library or Config (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.in_battle_scene?`), so the battle stays joinable meanwhile. A random encounter on a map where another player's state tells such a battle (`target_battle`: the one whose host entered the map first, whose own state tells the same battle) asks its host instead of hosting (`ask`, from `offer`, only while no invite waits): `coop=hot` through the map's gate with the battle's id, the troop, its enemies (`id:x:y:hidden`, `;` between them) and the seats of the free players it would have taken (`raid_candidates`, who stand still; none whose state tells a battle), and waits as that battle's guest up to 3 s (`ANSWER_FRAMES`, `await` from `Mode.before_start`). The host takes it in (`take_request`, `refusal`) when it hosts that battle or fights it alone, its battle no longer gathers, nobody left it to the computer, neither troop is a boss battle (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.battle?` for its own, or the `boss=1` its host's invite carried for a battle it took over, `boss?` for the encounter's), its players, those taken in before and the new ones stay at most 4 (a request that would bring more is refused whole, and its encounter starts as its own battle, which invites them), this game has the enemies, and they find places (`place`: at most 8 in the troop, `TROOP_LIMIT`, which counts every member of the troop, the fallen too, since the troop keeps them; a fallen enemy's place on the screen free, `fallen?`; on the screen where at most half of the narrower of two pictures, measured through `Cache.battler`, hides behind another, `MAX_OVERLAP`, the place nearest the middle among equals; pictures as wide as the screen stand in its middle and count for no overlap, as the game's own troops stack them there). It answers `coop=hot_ok` with its escape and lose, invites the players who come along with `hot=1`, lets in their battle messages (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.expect`), and, fighting alone, hosts its battle without turning it live yet; otherwise `coop=hot_no`. A player another request brought along who asks with their own encounter, both requests sent at once, is taken out of that request first (`release_companion`), and ignores that request's invite to the battle it asks (`asks?`). Taken in, the player joins as a late guest (`join_late`: the host's escape and lose, no first strike or surprise); refused or without an answer it hosts its encounter as usual, telling a silent host `coop=off`, and the players the request held in place walk on again (`release_companions`, `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.release`) unless the encounter invites them anew. A late guest (`late?`, also one invited with `hot=1`) waits up to 75 s for the roster (`ROSTER_FRAMES`), "Joining <host>'s battle at its next turn..." with "Up to 75 s" on the box's names line (`UP_TO_PREFIX`, since the wait names nobody and the text line would clip) and the seconds waited below. The host takes them in as a command phase opens, before the live battle opens it (`settle`, first in `Mode.settle`): a host fighting alone turns its battle live first (`before_phase`, `go_live`, its hook wrapping `battles_sync_live.rbx`'s): the multiplayer rules, its squad as the party's first player, the stream. The new players' characters join the party (`arrange`, `form`), and the encounter's enemies the troop once its player joined (`add_enemies`: placed anew with the troop as it stands then, so they fill the places of enemies that fell since the request, around the places of the encounters still to join; when enemies added since leave no room for them all, only the first that still find a place join, never past `TROOP_LIMIT` (`place_anew`); started as the game starts a battle's, named apart, the escape ratio anew, drawn anew); the guests in the battle get `coop_party` and, through `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.share_troop` (see *Troop changes*), `coop_troop` (the troop and the names, which a guest adds after its own enemies or takes whole, `take_troop`, between two sends; a guest's `grow_troop` starts, discovers and rescales the enemies it adds the way the host's `add_enemies` does), the new players `roster` (the troop with the fallen enemies, eaten ones too, marked `2`, `late_entries`, which a guest rebuilds dead and unseen, `lay_down`, once the game lettered them, which it does only for the living, so the guest names every enemy as the host does, its escape ratio anew, the players, no level and the turn) and `ready` with the names, and then the command phase's stream. Players who turned the invite down or stopped waiting (`give_up`: any end of a late guest's wait without the roster sends `decline` and puts its encounter's own escape and lose back), left the battle or the world, or did not join within 60 s (`WAIT_SECONDS`, told `coop=off`) are forgotten (`expire`); a host fighting alone whom nobody joins keeps its battle its own (`stay_alone`), its Retry too should the battle end first. A host whose co-op battle goes on alone (`stand_alone`) calls off those taken in who did not join. The host's `coop=off` lets a late guest's encounter start on its own (`call_off_own`, `fight_own`, through `take_over`: its own escape and lose, from its start), and so does a roster that never comes; an invited late guest breaks off as any guest. The battle's end tells those taken in who did not join `coop=off` (`ended`, after `Scene_Battle#terminate`, though not while the battle's scene waits on the scene stack because the party command's Library or Config called another screen, `called_away?`). Every game pays out the whole troop it fought, as in any co-op battle. An event's battle never asks, since its event goes on by its own troop's result and pages.
  - **Joining by choice** (`battles_coop_join.rbx`, `MGQ_MpBattlesJoin`): the host of a battle, or a player who fights one alone, also tells `rbn` in its state: the players in it with those taken in, while it takes players in, else 0 (`state_fields`, `closed_reason`: no PvP battle, no longer gathering, nobody left it to the computer, not ending, fewer than 4). While the player is free on the map (`busy_reason`: the map the scene and quiet, no transfer, no multiplayer battle, not held, no chat box, wheel or World overview, neither trading nor about to teleport) and another player within 3 steps (`JOIN_DISTANCE`, diagonals counted as one, from the states' `x` and `y`) tells a battle whose host, on the map and telling the same battle, tells `rbn` 1 to 3 (`target`, `open_battle`, the nearest first), a prompt below the player's character (`Sprite_MpJoinPrompt`) says "Press J to join battle" with the hotkey's current key (`MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:join_battle`, J by default; `on_map` finds the battle every frame, `prompt_text` hides it while the map is not free); never in a Classic world, and never near the battle the player was in last, whose host keeps a guest who left among its seats. The hotkey is read only in a Raid World, and not at all while another hotkey has its key (`reads_hotkey?`), since a key's press goes to the first who reads it in a frame. The hotkey sends the host `coop=pick` with the battle's id and the map through the map's gate (`request`), and the player stands still (`hold_player`) up to 3 s (`ANSWER_SECONDS`), "Asking <host> to join their battle..." in the notification box. The host takes them in (`take_request`, `refusal`: `closed_reason`, the same battle and map, the player neither in it before nor taken in already) as a player another encounter brings along, but without enemies (`MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.take_in_player`): `coop=invite` with `hot=1`, `boss=1` for a boss battle and `pool` for one that fights a raid boss's pools (`invite_fields`, which a late guest who takes the battle over keeps), so an event's battle and a boss battle take them too. The player joins it as any late guest; the event's result and the boss pool's report stay the host's, and the player's rewards are a co-op guest's. Otherwise the host answers `coop=pick_no` with `why` (`full`, `ending`, `gathering`, `pvp` or `closed`), which the player reads in the notification box ("Cannot join <host>'s battle: it is full."), as they read a host who stays silent ("No answer from <host>'s battle."). A player who stops waiting sends the host `coop=off` with the battle's id, which forgets them (`MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.take_off`), and ignores its invite should it come late (`gave_up?`); an invite that came in time but waits for the player to be free keeps them waiting (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.invited_by?`). **Side battles** (`side_target`, `side_battles`, `fight_alongside`): while no battle near the player takes them in and they are free, a raid boss battle on the map whose host tells `rpb` (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.side_of`: a boss the player's story has not beaten, its pools not all empty) with one of its players, or the boss's map event (`MGQ_MpRaidBar.distance_to_boss`), within `JOIN_DISTANCE` makes the prompt say "Press J to fight \<boss> alongside". The hotkey starts the player's own side battle against the same pools (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.start_side`, see [In the game (`battles_raid_pool.rbx`)](#in-the-game-battles_raid_poolrbx)), or says "Cannot fight \<boss> now." in the notification box when it cannot start. A side battle starts at once as the player's own: `offer` neither gathers, holds nor invites for it (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.side_battle?`, asking `MGQ_MpRaidPool.side_battle?`), and other players near it join it by choice; its host tells `rpb` as any raid boss battle's does, so next to a full side battle the hotkey offers yet another, and every player can fight a boss at once.

Each game ends the battle as the host's ended: a victory with its own EXP, gold and item drops (the guest's enemies carry the host's death states, which `exp_total`, `gold_total` and `make_drop_items` count), a defeat with its own defeat, an escape as an escape.

## Live battles (`battles_sync.rbx`)

**First version.** Both players command their own team. The host's game computes the battle and streams what it shows; the guest's game plays that back and sends its commands. Each game sees its own team as the party, so the two would draw their random numbers in a different order if both computed. When the teams arrive over an open link, `battles_pvp.rbx` calls `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.join`, which takes the role the DLL reports (`role=host` or `guest`), and keeps the link instead of cancelling it; `Battle.start` calls `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.battle_started` as it calls the battle scene, and `Battle.restore` calls `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.finish`, which closes the link. A mirror match calls `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.record_to_file` instead of joining.
- **Co-op** (`mode` `:coop`, `join_world`): the same system with several guests over the world's room instead of the link. A message is `battle=<kind>`, `bid=<battle id>`, then the body (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.tell`), sent to each of the battle's other players (`player_seats`) and taken only for the running battle (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.take` through overworld_sync.rbx's routes); a guest takes only its host's, the host only its guests' (`Channel.receive`, `take_from`). The sides stay as they are, since every game has the same party and troop (`Playback.battler`, `Names.setup`). A guest's commands cover only its own characters, and the host gives them to that guest's allies (`Commands.apply` by `mp_seat`). The host waits for every guest still in the battle (`all_ready?`, which starts each wait with nobody ready, and `host_guests_commands`); a guest who left (gone from the room, or `leave` once their escape succeeded, `Live.escaped`) does nothing more, their characters' actions taken away at once on the host (`guest_left`, `Mode#left`, `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.idle`; commands they sent before are left out), and taken out of the party at the next command phase (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.settle`). Every player's Escape is the game's own escape attempt (`escape_leaves?` is false in a co-op battle); a failed one spends that player's turn, since its empty commands take its characters' actions away. When the host gets away (its stream ends with `process_abort`) or is gone, each guest takes the battle over as its own (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.take_over`). Each game ends it as the host's ended (`guest_end`), never swapped. The world's room stays open afterwards (`finish`).
- **Messages** (`Channel`) are the game script's own: a kind on the first line (`ready`, `commands`, `events`, `forfeit`, `broken`), the rest after it. Values travel as `Wire` lines, tab-separated typed tokens (`i5`, `sText` with `\t`, `\n` and `\` escaped, `@a0` for a battler, `k12` a skill, `t3` an item, `z2` a state, `w5`/`a7` a weapon or armor, `g` in front of one of these for an equipped or stolen item, `xcount` an action's kind, `[` … `]` an array, `{` … `}` an action result as field names and values), never as Ruby code or Marshal data. The guest only calls the methods its lists name, takes picture names without a path and waits of at most 10 s.
- **Start:** both battle scenes send `ready` and wait for the other's (`Waiting`, "Waiting for <Player>..."; the host's box names the guests not ready yet, `NOT_READY`, `not_ready`). The host's `ready` carries its party's and troop's names, which the guest swaps for its own in every text that still arrives as text (`Names`: the host's characters are the guest's enemies, named with their owner), but not in a speaker's name box (`\n<Name>`) or the untranslated game's speaker line (`【Name】`). An untranslated guest has no name box, so it shows a translated host's as a speaker line (`Names.readable`). The host turns its skip settings off for the battle (`SKIP_SETTINGS`), since the guest sees only what the host's battle shows; `finish` puts them back.
- **Host:** `Recorder` streams every event every 10 frames, with each battler's HP, MP, SP, states with the turns they have left, buffs and barriers when they changed: before and after every action, after every hit (`apply_item_effects`, which counters and reflections go through too) and before every sprite effect, so the guest has a hit's HP and a defeat's death before it shows them; at every command phase every battler's, which corrects whatever the guest missed. Sprite effects are recorded at the setter and after `perform_damage_effect` and `perform_collapse_effect` of `Game_Actor` and `Game_Enemy`, which write the field directly (`Recorder.sprite_effect`). When recording fails, the host breaks the battle off (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.break_off`, which sends `broken`), since the guest sees nothing but the stream. In a PvP battle, a host's character that falls with an actor's collapse is the guest's enemy, so its collapse becomes the defeat flash its silhouette starts from (`Playback.sprite_effect`). The stream also carries a `commands` marker where the host's command phase starts, sent at once as `start_party_command_selection` begins (`Recorder.command_phase`, once per phase), since with none of the host's characters able to act (stunned, bound, frozen by time stop) the game goes straight on to `turn_start` and the host waits for the guest's commands within that call; and an `end` marker in `process_victory`, `process_defeat` or `process_abort`. At each turn's start the host waits for the guest's `commands` (`Live.host_commands`) and gives them to the friend's characters (`Commands.apply`: skill or item, id, target index; a troop is the other game's party in the same order, so an index means the same member). A character the guest names no commands for (`nil`) keeps the computer's choice, an empty list takes its actions away, and a list counts only as many commands as the host's game gave the character actions, since the host rolls the extra ones. The friend's characters use items from the friend's bag, so `Opponent` checks no item count and uses none up; the host records an `item_used` event for a consumable one, which the guest's own game uses up (`Playback.use_up`).
- **Guest:** never runs battle logic. `battle_start` and `turn_start` send its `ready` or its `commands` (`Commands.build`) and play the stream (`Playback.run`) until the next `commands` marker or the end, and `judge_win_loss` never ends the battle on its own. Animations are only started on the guest's battlers, unless the guest holds its skip key, since the host's recorded waits that follow keep the pace and the game's own animation methods would wait a second time. When four or more sends of the stream wait (`BEHIND_SENDS`), the guest skips waits and animations until it caught up. A message goes into a box as soon as its last line arrived. Values are set directly (`@hp`, `@states`, …), with no side effects of adding a state; once a character's states or buffs changed, the game's cache of its features (`CacheActorFeatures`, which only `Game_Actor#refresh` clears) is cleared (`features_changed`), or a state's extra actions, such as Division's (states 208-210, 663), and its counters would never count on the guest. A recorded call the guest does not make (`callable?` false) is logged once per method ("left out the host's log call ..."), since a call left out shows nothing, such as a hit's damage numbers; the host logs where a co-op guest's rebuilt character differs from what the guest's game showed (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.check`, `Game_MpActor#differences`), whose rates now include magic reflection and counter (`MGQ_MpActors::Builds::RATES`, xparams 0, 1, 2, 5 and 6). The host's end is turned around (`Live.guest_end`). After a played turn the guest ends its turn (`BattleManager.turn_end`) before the next command phase, since `BattleManager.input_start` makes new actions and starts at the first member only when the phase is not `:input` already. While it plays a turn, the guest keeps its party's status windows up (`@battle_actor_status_windows_show`), which the game shows only during its own turn logic. Time stop (the game's *over drive*, `Game_Party#od_turn` and `od_user`, which decide who may act) travels as a `time_stop` event whenever it changes, and its darkening and music fade as `Game_Screen#od_fadein`/`od_fadeout` and `Audio.start_over_drive`/`end_over_drive`.
- **Each game's language:** a translated and an untranslated game play together, so the host sends what happened rather than its texts wherever the game builds a line from data, and the guest writes the line with its own game's code and texts (`Recorder.call`, `Playback.call`). These are every `display_*` method of `Window_BattleLog` (the battle log's lines, built from the target's `Game_ActionResult`, the database and `Vocab`), `Scene_Battle#process_skill_word` (the line a character says with a skill, and its cut-in) and `#display_skill_name`. The host records the method, its arguments, the action results of the battlers among them and a seed; it draws its own random numbers from that seed during the call (`Recorder.seeded`), and so does the guest, so the guest's `.sample` picks the same one of a skill's lines. Everything the call does itself is left out of the recording (`Recorder.muted`), since the guest's call does it too: its log primitives, popups, messages, sounds, pictures, sprite effects (on the guest's own battlers, so a hit on the host's character is a hit on an enemy) and waits, which the guest skips while it catches up (`Playback.skip_wait?`). The battlers' values are recorded right before the call. The guest calls only the `display_*` methods its own hooks wrapped (`Hooks.log_calls`) and the battle's `SCENE_CALLS`, compared as text, never making a symbol of the host's. "X appears!" comes from `BattleManager.battle_start`, which the guest never runs, so the host records an `emerge` event instead of those lines, with whether its party struck first or was surprised, and the guest names its own troop, says who struck first and gives its first command phase the same start (`Playback.emerge`, `take_encounter`). A call with an argument `Wire` cannot carry is recorded as what it does, in the host's language, like every line built outside these methods.
- **Rules:** Escape ends the battle at once as a forfeit (`Live.forfeit`), which also sends `forfeit`; Give Up is off, as in every multiplayer battle (`MGQ_MpBattles`, `BattleManager.can_giveup?`). Both sides watch for the friend's `forfeit`, a `broken` and the link ending (`Channel.ending`) every frame of `Scene_Battle#update` (`Live.watch`) and while they wait (`Waiting.wait_for`, `Playback.run`), so a forfeit or a friend who leaves ends the battle while a player chooses commands too; `Live.end_early` ends it. No message holds either player up: the Battle Dialogue mod, when installed, shows them in boxes (see Dialogue boxes under [Discord (optional)](#discord-optional)), and one the message window shows moves on by itself after 90 frames (`Window_Message#input_pause`). When the link ends, the remaining player wins; `DROPOUT = :computer` lets the computer play the friend's team on the host instead. A battle broken off has no winner (`process_abort`, "broke off" on the map). There is no turn timer: once a wait lasted 10 s (`Waiting::LEAVE_FRAMES`), the box offers to leave the battle with Cancel, which counts as a forfeit, so a friend whose game hangs with the link still open never holds the other player for good.
- **The waiting box** (`Waiting`): `wait_for(scene, text, details = nil, prefix = WAITING_PREFIX)` opens a `Window_MpWaiting` (a `Window_Base` in the preset's window skin and the layout box `:waiting_box`, `WIDTH` 360 at first) of `LINES` (3) lines (`HEIGHT` from `LINE_HEIGHT` 24 and `PADDING` 12 as the game's `Window_Base` has them, given to the window at `open`, since the contents are made once with the window) on the first frame without an answer: the text, the names line and the seconds. `details` is a lambda returning the names still waited for (asked every frame, so cheap; one that raises is logged once and the line stays empty, `names_of`), drawn after `prefix` ("Still choosing: ", "Not ready: ") only when they changed, cut to the window; the seconds line (`MGQ_MpUi::SECONDS_TEXT`) is drawn once every `REDRAW_FRAMES` (60), from "0 s", until `LEAVE_TEXT` takes it at `LEAVE_FRAMES` (`refresh`, from `leave?`). `open`/`leave?`/`close` take a `Box` record for the playback's own wait. `WAITING_FOR` ("Waiting for %s...") words the wait for a player, which the live battle and the playback share. `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.seat_name(seat)` names a seat for the line: "you" (`OWN_NAME`), the peer's name, or "seat n" for one the game does not know, never raising (`peer_name`). The host waits through `Live.wait_telling`, which names the seats its block waits for and tells the guests those seats whenever they change (`tell_waiting`, the `waiting` message, `WAITING_KIND`, sorted seats joined by commas, nobody once answered; also everyone as a command phase opens, and those asked to choose again), so a guest's box names the same players: the playback's wait and the guest's start read them with `host_waits_for` (`Channel.take(WAITING_KIND)`, the player themself left out), taken as they come so none piles up; `forget_waiting` at each battle's start. Over the PvP link nobody is named.
- **Mirror matches** record into `Logs/Battle Recording.log` instead, one `Wire` line per event after its frame.

`Hooks.install` puts the hooks in once the first scene starts (a `SceneManager.run` hook), since the game's plugins load after the Patch folder and `Promestein2` defines `Scene_Battle#battle_start`, `Scene_Battle#turn_start` and `Window_BattleLog#add_text` anew, which drops a hook installed before it. `Hooks.wrap` keeps each wrapped method under a name of its own, since `turn_start` is wrapped twice. What the stream carries: the calls the guest makes itself (see *Each game's language*), and outside them the battle log's primitives (`add_text`, `replace_text`, `back_one`, `back_to`, `clear`, `clear_popup`), `PopupResults#push`, messages with their face and speaker (`process_skill_word` and `process_down_word` set it, since a line names its speaker only by name, which both teams can share), pictures of the troop screen (cut-ins; erases only of pictures that were shown), its shakes, flashes and tone changes, every `Audio` call (the log plays some sounds directly), animations as `show_animation` is asked for them, before it checks the skip key, sprite effects, the skill name, turns, actions and the scene's waits.

**Not yet in the live battle:** effects outside the battle log's calls turned around for the other side, a confirmation before Escape, and the computer taking over the host's team when the host leaves.

**Not covered yet:** the Backline in team duels and levelling the two teams.
